and faith

===Chinese===
**本书**

写于战时

为传送安慰

并盼能坚固盼望

与信心

===Original===
**is dedicated**

by permission

to

**Sir John R. Jellicoe**

G.C.B., K.C.V.O.

Admiral of the Grand Fleet

===Chinese===
**谨此奉献**

蒙允

敬献于

**约翰·杰利科爵士（Sir John R. Jellicoe）**

G.C.B.、K.C.V.O.

===Original===
> “There are nettles everywhere,

 But smooth green grasses are more common still;

 The blue of heaven is larger than the cloud.”
> — E. B. Browning

===Chinese===
> 「荨麻无处不在，

 柔滑的绿草却更为常见；

 苍穹的蔚蓝比云朵更辽阔。」
> ——勃朗宁夫人（E. B. Browning）

## I A Day at a Time / I 一天一天地过

===Original===
> "As thy days, so shall thy strength be."
> — Deuteronomy 33:25

===Chinese===
> 「你的日子如何，你的力量也必如何。」
> — Deuteronomy 33:25

===Original===
If any one of us knows a word of hope or has picked up a message of comfort anywhere, it is his plain duty to share it, these days. We owe it to each other to cherish as exceeding precious, and to pass on to others, every brave and helpful word or thought we come across.

Well, here is a splendid one for us all, and especially for those who have most at stake in this great conflict, and are looking anxiously ahead and fearing what the weeks may have in store—"As thy days, so shall thy strength be." It is a great and glorious promise. And just a couple of verses further on, it is caught up and included in one greater still—"The eternal God is thy refuge and underneath are the everlasting arms." Fathers and mothers, with a boy, or more than one, perhaps, away on active service for King and country, this promise is for you, to take to your heart and hide there, like some precious secret between you and God—As thy days, so shall thy strength be.

===Chinese===
在这些日子里，我们中间若有谁晓得一句盼望的话语，或从何处拾得一条安慰的信息，把它分享出来，便是他明白的本分。凡遇见勇敢而有益的言语或思想，都当视作极贵重的珍宝加以珍惜，并传递给他人——这是我们彼此相欠的债。

那么，这里就有一句极好的话，给我们每一个人，尤其给那些在这场大冲突中利害攸关最深的人，他们正焦灼地朝前张望，害怕往后的几个星期里不知藏着什么——「你的日子如何，你的力量也必如何。」这是一个伟大而荣耀的应许。再往下不过两节经文，它便被接续起来，包纳在一个更伟大的应许之中——「永生的神是你的居所，他永久的膀臂在你以下。」做父亲、做母亲的，你们或许有一个儿子，或不止一个，正为王与国家奔赴战地服役——这应许就是给你们的，要把它领到心里珍藏起来，像你们与神之间的一个宝贵秘密——你的日子如何，你的力量也必如何。

===Original===
Notice carefully, however, how the promise runs. Not, mark you, as your life is, not as your years are, not even as your weeks are, but as your days, so shall your strength be. For each day as it comes, God's promise is that strength will be given you, but just for a day at a time. The way to live under any circumstances, but especially in these hard weeks, is just a day at a time. Leave tomorrow with God, my brother, until it comes. That is what the Word of God lays upon you as a duty. Live this day at your best and bravest, trusting that God's help will not fail you. And for the duties and trials of tomorrow, however hard and heavy, believe that strength for that day also will be given you, when it comes.

You cannot have failed to observe what an important place this way of living had in the teaching of Jesus Christ. He was always trying to get men to trust the coming days to God, and to live fully worthily and nobly today. He was dead against the practice of adding to the burdens of today fears and forebodings for tomorrow. It is in love to us, in His desire to save us unnecessary pain, that He bids us remember that "sufficient unto the day is the evil thereof."

===Chinese===
然而，请仔细留意这应许是怎么说的。请注意，不是照你的一生如何，不是照你的年岁如何，甚至不是照你的星期如何，而是照你的日子如何，你的力量也必如何。神的应许是：每一天来到之时，必有力量赐给你，但一次只给一天。无论身处何种境况，尤其在这艰难的几个星期里，过日子的法门就是一天一天地过。我的弟兄，把明天留给神，直到它来到。这是神的道加在你身上的本分。尽你最大的勇敢活好今天，深信神的帮助必不致叫你落空。至于明天的职责与试炼，无论多么艰难沉重，要相信等到那一天来到，那一天的力量也必赐给你。

你们必定早已看出，这种活法在耶稣基督的教导中占着何等要紧的位置。他总是设法叫人把将来的日子交托给神，而使今天活得完全配得上、完全高贵。对于把对明天的恐惧与不祥预感加在今天重担上的做法，他是断然反对的。他吩咐我们记念「一天的难处一天当就够了」，正是出于他对我们的爱，出于他要救我们免受不必要之苦的心意。

===Original===
In one of R. D. Blackmore's fine open-air stories, there is a character who talks at length about horses. After comparing good ones and bad ones in their behaviour the first time they breast a hill with a load behind them, he sums the matter up thus: "Howsoever good a horse be, he longeth to see over the top of the hill before he be halfway up it." The man who is listening to him confesses that he has often felt that way himself! And I do not know that there are many of us who can claim to be guiltless in this respect. Yet it is perfectly plain that the men and women who are living the bravest and most successful lives around us, and are proving towers of strength to others, are those who have learned the art of living just a day at a time, and of depending upon God for strength for that day in the simplest and most trustful fashion.

Why, my brothers, if God our Father had meant us to carry on our backs the fears and anxieties of the coming days, He would surely have told us more about them! If we were meant to bear today what next week holds, surely we should have been permitted to see into next week. But we cannot. We cannot see a single second ahead. God gives us Now, and Tomorrow He keeps to Himself. Is there anything wiser or better we can do with our tomorrows than just to leave them quietly and trustfully with Him?

===Chinese===
在 R. D. 布莱克莫尔（R. D. Blackmore）一部精彩的露天小说里，有一个人物对马大谈特谈。他把好马与劣马头一回驮着重负爬坡时的表现比较了一番之后，这样总括道：「无论一匹马多么好，它没爬到半坡，就巴望能望见山顶那边的光景。」听他说话的人承认，他自己也常有这般心情！而我们中间，敢说在这方面毫无亏欠的，恐怕也没有几个。然而有一件事再明白不过：在我们周围活得最勇敢、最有成就、并成为他人力量之柱石的男男女女，正是那些学会了「一天一天地过」这门艺术、并以最单纯最信靠的方式仰赖神赐下当天之力的人。

我的弟兄们，想一想吧，倘若天父本意是要我们把将来日子的恐惧与忧虑背在自己背上，他必定会把关于这些日子的更多事告诉我们！倘若今天就该担起下星期所要发生的事，我们理当蒙容许窥见下星期才是。但我们不能。往前一秒钟我们都看不见。神把「现在」赐给我们，把「明天」留在他自己手中。对于我们的种种明天，除了安安静静、满心信靠地交在他手中，我们还能做什么更智慧、更美好的事呢？

===Original===
The habit of living ahead, as so many of us do, prevents us from getting the full taste and flavour of the happiness and blessing that are ours today. I defy any man to be adequately grateful for this day's sunshine if he is worrying all the time about the chance of a bad day tomorrow. Mark Rutherford, merciless self-critic as he was, takes himself severely to task for this habit in his *Autobiography*. "I learned, alas! when it was almost too late," he says, "to live in each moment as it passed over my head, believing that the sun as it is now rising, is as good as it ever will be." Yes, in great things as well as in little things, that is true. If we are to live our lives at the full, and anywhere on the Christian level, the only way is to live one day at a time.

Our forefathers in the pulpit were fond of reminding their hearers to live each day as if it were their last. And in solemn truth, without being in the least morbid, that is the way to live. If a man knew that after today, he would not smell the sea again, how fully and gratefully would he fill his lungs with its ozone today! If he knew he were not to enter God's House again, how earnestly and sincerely and reverently he would join in its worship today! Yes, but the point is, why should his hope, that he has other days to come, prevent him taking out of this day all that he possibly can? Why should this day be any less prized, because others in all probability will follow it?

===Chinese===
像我们许多人那样「预支着生活」的习惯，使我们尝不到今日幸福与福分的十足滋味。我要说，一个人若时时刻刻都在担心明天也许是个坏日子，他就绝不可能对今天的阳光有充分的感恩。马克·拉瑟福德（Mark Rutherford）这位毫不留情的自我批评者，在《自传》（Autobiography）里就这习惯严厉地责备了自己。「唉！等到几乎太迟，我才学会，」他说，「活在每一个掠过我头顶的瞬间，相信此刻正在升起的太阳，与它日后任何时刻一样美好。」是的，无论大事小事，这话都真。我们若要活得丰满，活得有一点基督徒的样式，唯一的路就是一天一天地活。

从前站在讲坛上的先辈们喜欢提醒会众：要把每一天当作最后一天来活。而这确是庄重的真理，没有半分病态，这原是对的活法。一个人若知道今天过后就再闻不到海的气息，今天他该会何等饱满、何等感激地让肺腑充满海上清新的空气！他若知道自己再不能踏进神的殿，今天他该会何等恳切、真诚、虔敬地投入殿中的敬拜！是啊，但关键在于：为什么他「还有别的日子可活」的盼望，就该拦阻他尽力从今天汲取一切呢？为什么因为别的日子多半会接踵而至，今天就该少受珍重呢？

===Original===
But the great value of this word is the comfort of it to those who are anxious and fear the coming days. And which of us is not in that category? I do not suppose there is one of my readers upon whom, somehow or other, the war has not levied its tax. Nearly everyone has somebody belonging to him or her who is in this gigantic struggle, and whose welfare is a matter of real concern. And, closer still, there are fathers and mothers, sisters and brothers, whose very dearest are "in it" or are getting ready to do their share. They have joined, and we are proud that they have joined, for this is a cause that ennobles every mother's son who fights for it. But who shall say what the mother's thoughts are, these days? How proud, and justly proud, the father is that his boy has played the man, and offered himself to his King and for his country! But only God, who made the father—and the mother—heart, knows what the surrender costs. And only God knows how eagerly and anxiously they look ahead to try to see what the future may hold.

And, knowing that, He sends His comfort to you, fathers and mothers. The comfort of His promise—As thy days, so shall thy strength be. Just a day at a time, my friend! Do not take fears for next month on your shoulders now. You will get strength given you for today, certain and sure, and when next month comes, the strength and comfort for that day will come too, as certain and as sure. Be not overanxious about the morrow. Leave your tomorrow, and your soldier-son, in God's hands. You can do nothing more at the best, and this is the best. But it is such a mistake to do anything less. Leave all your tomorrows with God—it is what He wants you to do—and humbly and gratefully take from His hands His gift of Today, and the strength that comes with it. If that be not enough—and it is not enough for God has said more—when that is not enough, still your heart a moment, and listen! And you will hear, beneath that promise for today, like the grand deep tones of an organ, the magnificent diapason of the Father's constant love and mindfulness—"The eternal God is thy refuge, and underneath are the everlasting arms." And surely that is enough!

===Chinese===
然而这话最大的价值，在于它对那些忧虑而惧怕将来日子之人的安慰。而我们哪一个不在其列呢？我想，我的读者当中不会有一个人，不曾以这样那样的方式被这场大战征收过它的税。几乎人人都有一位亲人正陷身这场巨大的搏斗，他或她的平安实在令人挂心。再近一层，还有那些做父母、做兄弟姐妹的，他们至亲至爱的人正「在其中」，或正准备尽自己的一份。他们已投身其中，我们也为他们投身而自豪，因为这是一项使每一个为它战斗的儿子都得尊荣的事业。可是这些日子里，谁能道尽一位母亲心中的念头呢？儿子已经作了大丈夫，把自己献给了他的王、献给了他的国家，做父亲的是何等自豪，而且自豪得有理！但唯有造了父亲的心、也造了母亲的心的神，知道这一舍出代价几何。也唯有神知道，他们是怎样急切而焦灼地朝前张望，想看一看未来究竟藏着什么。

而神知道这一切，他把他的安慰差给你们，做父母的人们。他应许的安慰——你的日子如何，你的力量也必如何。一次只过一天，我的朋友！现在不要把下个月的恐惧扛上肩头。今天的力量必赐给你，确定无疑；到了下个月，那一天的力量与安慰也必照样确定无疑地来到。不要为明天过分忧虑。把你的明天，把你从军的儿子，都交在神的手中。尽你所能，此外再无可做，而这已是最好的；做得再少，便是大错。把你一切的明天都留给神——这正是他要你做的——再谦卑而感激地从他的手中领受他那「今日」的礼物，和随之而来的力量。若这还不够——那的确还不够，因为神说得更多——当这还不够时，让你的心静默片刻，侧耳而听！你就要听见，在那今日应许的下面，仿佛管风琴宏大深沉的音调，回荡着天父恒久眷爱与看顾的雄浑谐音——「永生的神是你的居所，他永久的膀臂在你以下。」而这必定够了！

===Original===
> "So for Tomorrow and its needs
> I do not pray,
> But keep me, guide me, help me, Lord,
> Just for Today."

===Chinese===
> 「所以，为着明天和它的需用，
> 我并不祈求；
> 只求主保守我、引导我、帮助我——
> 只为今天。」

===Original===
Prayer
O Lord our God, who dost appoint the way for each of us, give us the grace to trust that as Thou hast helped us hitherto, so, in Thy great mercy, Thou wilt bless us still. We do not ask to see the distant scene. Keep us, and our beloved, this day; and in quietness and confidence teach us to leave tomorrow with Thee, our Father. Through Jesus Christ our Lord. Amen.

===Chinese===
祈祷
主我们的神啊，你为各人指派当行的路，求你赐恩，使我们得以信靠：你既帮助我们到如今，也必在你的大怜悯中仍旧赐福我们。我们不求看见远方的光景。求你保守我们和我们亲爱的人度过这一天；并在安静与信心中，教导我们把明天交托给你——我们的父。奉耶稣基督我们的主求。阿们。

## II God in the Wheels / II 轮中的上帝

===Original===
> “The Spirit of life was in the wheels.”

> — Ezekiel 1:21

===Chinese===
> 「生命的灵在轮中。」

> — Ezekiel 1:21

===Original===
The prophet Ezekiel once had an extraordinary vision of God. He tries to tell us about it, but his description seems to be a meaningless jumble of cherubim, and wheels—wheels within wheels, complex, wonderful, unresting. Behind all, he saw the Glory of God. And again and again he tells us that “the Spirit of Life was in the wheels.”

Now that at least is intelligible, and it is a good thing for us to think about. The Spirit of God is in the wheels.

I want to suggest to you that He is in the wheels of industry. We have no hesitation in saying that God gives the farmer his harvest, and we actually thank Him for it in His temple. A shepherd with a lamb in his arms is for a pastoral people like the Jews the very image of the Saviour God. But men who dwell in towns, and work in mills and factories and yards and railways, or who control or manage such places, have little to do with either corn or sheep. Is it not worth while to remind them that God is also in the wheels? Do you remember how Kipling’s old chief engineer Macandrew believed that his twin monsters, driving the liner onward on her way, sang their hourly hymn of praise to God? And why not? From all the wheels of industry and man’s inventiveness, goes there not up to Him a praise as real as the song of His little birds?

===Chinese===
先知以西结曾得见一个非同寻常的上帝异象。他竭力要向我们描述，可他的描写读来像一团无从索解的纷乱：基路伯、轮子——轮中套轮，繁复、奇妙、永不停歇。而在这一切背后，他看见了上帝的荣耀。他又一再告诉我们：「生命的灵在轮中。」

这一点至少是能领会的，我们细细思想它必有益处。上帝的灵就在轮子里。

我要向你们提出：祂也在产业的轮子之中。我们毫不犹豫地承认，收成是上帝赐给农夫的，我们甚至还在祂的殿里为此感谢祂。对犹太人这样一个畜牧民族来说，怀抱羊羔的牧人正是救主上帝的写照。可是住在城镇里、在磨坊、工厂、工场和铁路上劳作的人，或是掌管、经营这些场所的人，与五谷和羊群却少有接触。提醒他们上帝也在轮子之中，岂不是大有必要？你们还记得吉卜林（Rudyard Kipling）笔下那位老轮机长麦卡安德鲁（Macandrew）吗？他相信他那两台「双胞胎怪物」推动邮轮一路向前时，正时时刻刻唱着赞美上帝的颂歌。这有何不可呢？从一切产业的轮子和人类的发明创造中，岂不也有真实的赞美升到祂面前，一如祂那些小鸟的歌声？

===Original===
Where two or three gather together on Lord’s days, God is truly and graciously present. But I want you to remember that out in the noisy moving world of industry and business, God is present also, guiding, controlling and bringing His long, long plans to pass. It is by His decree that all the countless wheels of traffic and production turn and spin, for He needs them all, and has brought them into being by the hands of men, and they are His, as the Church is His. I would not have you, as Christian men, look upon your weekday world with its mechanism and its traffic, that world of yours that goes so literally upon wheels, as a province of life very far remote from the presence of God. I would remind you rather that God’s spirit is in those wheels, that they move at His bidding, and that they are working out His purposes upon the earth.

I would suggest, further, that God is in those wheels whose turning brings us Change. If you will allow the figure, I would say that God is in the wheels of Change and time.

===Chinese===
主日里，两三个人聚集的地方，上帝就真实而满有恩慈地同在。但我也要你们记住：在产业与商务那喧嚣奔忙的世界里，上帝同样临在，引导着，掌管着，使祂那长远又长远的计划得以成就。交通与生产的无数轮子转动飞旋，无不出于祂的旨意；因为这一切祂全需要，是祂借着人的手使它们存在，它们属于祂，正如教会属于祂。我不愿你们这些基督徒，把你那周间的世界——连同它的机械与交通，你那名副其实在轮子上运行的世界——看作生命中一个远离上帝临在的领域。我倒宁愿提醒你们：上帝的灵就在那些轮子里，它们听祂的吩咐而转动，正在地上成就祂的旨意。

我还要进一步说：上帝也在那些转动着、为我们带来变迁的轮子之中。倘若容许我用这个比喻，我要说，上帝就在变迁与时间的轮子里。

===Original===
As we grow older, we resent more and more the constant alteration of the surroundings of life. It saddens us that there should be such a continual moving on. But perhaps it is in the realm of doctrine and practice that changes hurt and perplex us most. Godly old customs die out. The face of truth seems to alter. Old notes in religion disappear and new ones take their place, and we are sorely tempted to ask if it be possible that the children can know God better or serve His Christ more truly than their fathers. Ah yes, from forty years and upwards, men are very apt to have a quarrel with change. They resent it, and would spike Time’s wheels if they could.

Forgetting that the Spirit of God is in those very wheels. Change is God’s method and His blessing. The Bible does not envy the man who has no changes. It is afraid for him, afraid that for want of them, he may settle on his lees, and forget the fear of God.

Of course, no one will defend every new fashion, or assert that everything recent is an improvement on what went before. But I, for one, do believe that generation after generation men are moving up, being shepherded up, the long slope of history nearer to God. I believe that God’s promise is that He will do better for us than at the beginnings, and I believe He is keeping His promise. I must believe that the history of this world which man rough hews, is—spite of all the wars—being shaped by God Himself, or else there is no God at all. And so I would say to those who distrust the continual changes of life, and would fain stop the wheels that turn on and on and never halt, “Fear not! Be of good courage! For aback of all change is God our Father, and it is His Spirit that is working in the wheels.”

===Chinese===
年岁渐长，我们对生活环境无休止变动的反感也与日俱增。世事竟要这样川流不息地向前推移，令我们黯然神伤。但变迁伤害我们、困扰我们最深的，或许还是在教义与践行的领域。敬虔的旧习俗消逝了。真理的面貌似乎在改变。信仰里的旧调消失了，新调取而代之，于是我们大受试探，禁不住要问：儿辈难道竟能比父辈更认识上帝、更真实地事奉祂的基督吗？是啊，人一过四十岁，就很容易同变迁争吵起来。他们怨恨变迁，只要办得到，恨不得给时间的轮子打上楔子，叫它停转。

他们忘了，上帝的灵恰恰就在那些轮子里。变迁是上帝的方法，也是祂的赐福。圣经并不羡慕那没有变迁的人，反倒替他担忧，怕他因缺少变迁，像酒渣一般沉滞下来，忘记了敬畏上帝。

当然，没有人会为每一种新风尚辩护，也不会有人断言，凡是晚近的都是对往昔的改进。但就我而论，我确实相信，一代又一代，人正沿着历史漫长的坡道被牧领着向上走，一步步靠近上帝。我相信上帝的应许是：祂待我们，末后必胜于起先；我相信祂正在持守这应许。我必须相信：这个世界的历史，这人不过粗加斧凿的坯料，纵然历经种种战乱，仍由上帝亲手加以塑造——否则就根本没有上帝。所以，我要对那些不信人生连绵的变迁、巴不得叫那转动不休、从不停歇的轮子停下来的人说：「不要惧怕！当刚强壮胆！因为在一切变迁的背后是我们的天父上帝，在轮子中运行的正是祂的灵。」

===Original===
Again, I would suggest to you that God is in the wheels that shape your own lot and mine. The wheels of Chance, they are sometimes called, the mere whirligig of destiny, as if the world were some blind irresponsible machine grinding on in the dark, and heeding not which or how many lives were broken in its teeth.

And I grant you that there be times when that idea seems feasible. For life is full of mysterious happenings, and chance sometimes seems the most probable explanation. The tragedy of Job is always being played somewhere. There are men who up to a certain point in life have known nothing but good fortune, and after that, nothing but disappointment and disaster. Out of a blue sky the bolt may fall on anyone; while from clouds lowering and heavy, it is waited for, expected and dreaded—and never comes! The merest knife-edge of circumstance sometimes affects results out of all proportion to its importance. “A grain of sand in a man’s flesh” as Pascal remarks, “has changed the course of Empires.” Yes, I grant you, there be times when the blind chance theory does suggest itself.

===Chinese===
再者，我要向你们提出：上帝也在那些塑造你我命数的轮子之中。有人称之为机遇的轮子，不过是命运的胡乱旋转，仿佛世界是一台盲目而不负责任的机器，在黑暗中碾磨不休，全然不管碾碎在它齿间的是谁的生命、是多少生命。

我承认，有时候那种想法似乎也讲得通。人生充满奥妙难测的事，偶然有时像是最合理的解释。约伯的悲剧总在世上的某处上演。有的人，在人生某一点之前尽交好运，过了那一点，却尽是失望与灾祸。晴天朗朗，霹雳会落在任何人头上；而当乌云低垂、阴沉欲雨时，人们等候那雷霆、料想它、惧怕它——它却偏偏不来！境遇中最微末的一线之差，有时竟对结果产生与自身轻重全不相称的影响。正如帕斯卡（Pascal）所言：「人肉中的一粒沙，改变了帝国的进程。」不错，我承认，有时候盲目的机遇论确实会自己浮上心头。

===Original===
But by an overwhelming majority the instinct of man is against it. And best of all, Jesus Christ, our supreme authority, has pledged Himself in His life and death, that the Ruler and Disposer of all events is Eternal Love. We have learned from Jesus to say and to trust “Our Father who art in Heaven.” We know and believe that whatever is to come falls not by chance, but is sent and permitted by the Love of God, who makes no mistakes. Taught and inspired by Jesus, many thousands of men and women have committed themselves and all their interests—home, health, happiness, reputation, loved ones—to the keeping of God the Father, and known by the peace that came to them, that it was a real transaction.

Soulless wheels of destiny! say some. The blind mechanism of law! Ah, no, Jesus is the refutation of that. Law there is, and mechanism there must be. But neither blind nor soulless. For, above all, is the Father Love of God, and it is His spirit that is guiding and governing the wheels.

Wheels of Industry, Wheels of Change, Wheels of Destiny. And God’s Spirit in them all!

===Chinese===
但人的直觉以压倒之势反对这种说法。而最要紧的是，耶稣基督——我们至高的权柄——已借着祂的生与死亲身作保：万事的掌管者与安排者乃是永恒之爱。我们已从耶稣学会了说，也学会了信靠：「我们在天上的父。」我们知道并且相信，将来临到的一切并非偶然落下的，而是由那从不失误的上帝之爱所差遣、所容许。受耶稣的教导与激励，千千万万的男男女女已把自己和一切所珍视的事——家庭、健康、幸福、名誉、所爱的人——交托给天父上帝看顾，并且借着临到他们身上的平安，知道这是一桩真实成就的托付。

「命运之轮没有灵魂！」有人这样说。「律法只是盲目的机械！」啊，不，耶稣正是对这种论调的反驳。律法固然有，机械也必然有，但它们既不盲目，也非无魂。因为在万有之上，是上帝为父的爱；引导并掌管那些轮子的，正是祂的灵。

产业的轮子，变迁的轮子，命运的轮子——而上帝的灵在这一切之中！

===Original===
Prayer

O Lord our God, to whom not only the Church but our whole work-a-day world belongs, give us the purged sight that can see Thy tokens there. Deliver us from all foolish fear of changes since the goad moving all things onward is in our Father’s hand. And help us to be sure that whatsoever befalleth us and ours has been permitted and appointed by a Love that passeth knowledge. Amen.

===Chinese===
祈祷

主我们的神啊，不但教会属于祢，我们整个劳作度日的尘世也属于祢；求祢赐我们炼净的眼光，能在那里认出祢的记号。求祢救我们脱离一切对变迁的愚妄惧怕，因为驱动万物向前的刺棒，握在我们天父的手中。并帮助我们笃定地相信：凡临到我们和我们亲人的事，都经过那过于人所能测度的爱的容许与命定。阿们。

## III A Triple Best / III 三重最好

===Original===
> “The just shall live by faith.”
> — Romans 1:17

===Chinese===
> 「义人必因信得生。」
> — Romans 1:17

===Original===
Some time ago I came across the life-motto of George Stephenson, the “father of the locomotive,” as he has been called, the man whose brains and sagacity made possible the network of railways which spreads now over the earth. The crystallised experience of such a life is worth studying. Here, then, was Stephenson’s working formula:—“Make the best of everything; think the best of everybody; hope the best for yourself.”

===Chinese===
前些时候，我偶然读到乔治·斯蒂芬森（George Stephenson）的一句生平格言。这位被人称作「火车之父」的人物，凭他的头脑与见识，使如今遍布全球的铁路网成为可能。这样一生凝结而成的经验，值得细细研究。斯蒂芬森的行事准则便是：「凡事务求最好；逢人想着最好；对己盼望最好。」

===Original===
First, *make the best of everything*. In every set of circumstances possible or conceivable, there are always, at any rate, two ways of acting. You can look for the helpful, bright, and hopeful things, and “freeze on” to these meantime. Or, you can select all the doleful, sombre aspects, and sit down in the dust with them. Now, if it did not matter which a man did, there would be no good saying any more. But it has long since become abundantly clear that the man who makes the best of his circumstances, however hard they be, comes most happily out of them in the end. In other words, it pays to make the best of things. It is the cheery people who recover quickest when they are sick. There are men who, if their house should fall in ruins about them, will contrive some sort of shelter meantime with the broken beams! That is the type that wins out in the end somehow; these are the men to whom the miracles happen—who never know when they are beaten, who will face the most tremendous odds with “the half of a broken hope” for a shield, who are never done until they are dead. What makes for success or failure in a man is nothing external to him at all. It is something within him. It is the temper of his spirit. It is the way he captains his own soul.

The other day I saw a photograph of a backyard. It was a little bit of a place, of the most forlorn appearance, littered with tin cans, overgrown with weeds, and hemmed round with blank walls of brick. But it came into the hands of a man who believed in making the best of things. Another photograph showed that same backyard after a year had passed. It was still as small as ever, still overlooked by high walls and surrounded by chimneys. But it was now a perfect little oasis of beauty amid a wilderness of bricks and slates. Will anybody deny that that spirit pays?

Right up the scale, from little things to the highest things, the man who looks for the shining possibilities and follows them, is the man on whom, in our shortsighted way, we say that Fortune smiles. Rather, he smiles in such a determined way to Fortune, that she has at length to smile back!

===Chinese===
第一，*凡事务求最好*。无论哪种可能发生或可以想见的境遇，起码总有两条路可走。你可以去寻找那些有益、光明、有盼望的事物，并且暂且紧紧攀住它们；也可以专拣那些凄凉阴郁的方面，一屁股坐进尘埃里与它们作伴。倘若人怎么做都无关紧要，那就再说无益了。但很久以来早已昭然若揭：那个善处自己境遇的人，无论境遇多么艰难，末了总能最愉快地从其中走出来。换言之，凡事往好处做是有回报的。生病时好得最快的，正是那些开朗的人。有的人哪怕房屋在四周塌成废墟，也会用断裂的梁木设法搭出个权且栖身之处！正是这一类人终究能胜出；奇迹偏偏发生在这样的人身上——他们从不承认自己已被打败，敢以「半段破碎的希望」为盾牌去迎对最悬殊的劣势，不到生命终点绝不停手。决定一个人成败的，全然不是他身外的东西；是他里面的东西——是他心灵的气质，是他统率自己灵魂的方式。

前几天我见到一张后院的照片。那是一小块地方，模样荒凉至极：满地扔着锡罐，野草蔓生，四周围着光秃秃的砖墙。可它落到了一个相信凡事都该往好处做的人手里。另一张照片拍的是一年之后的同一个后院：它还是那么小，依然处在高墙的俯视之下，依然被烟囱环抱；但如今，它已成了砖石板瓦的荒野当中一方完美的美丽绿洲。谁能否认这种精神有回报呢？

从小事直到至高之事，沿着整个阶梯一路向上，那寻找闪光的可能并追随不舍的人，就是我们目光短浅地所说「幸运女神向他微笑」的那种人。倒不如说，是他以那样坚定的笑意面向幸运女神，逼得她到头来只好回以微笑！

===Original===
Nobody pretends that it is easy, when we have failed, to gather our powers together and try again. But nearly all the big men have had to do that very thing. It certainly is not easy, when you have a heavy burden of your own, to spare a cheery word or a hand of sympathy for somebody who is really much better off, but there are plenty of people doing it at this moment. Nero’s palace is the last place in this world where you would expect to find a company of loyal Christian folk. Yet there were such people there, “the saints of Caesar’s household.” And the grace of God that made that possible can achieve all these lesser wonders too.

Second, *think the best of everybody*. There is a winsome legend that Jesus once revealed Himself in this way:—A knot of idlers had gathered in the street round a dead dog. One remarked how mangy and unkempt its hide was. Another said, “What ugly ears!” But a stranger, who had come forward, said, “Pearls are not whiter than its teeth!” And men said to one another, “This must be Jesus of Nazareth, for nobody but He would find something good even in a dead dog.” Certainly it is the mark of the most Christlike men and women that they delight rather in emphasising the merest speck of goodness than in denouncing the too visible evil. We can, all too easily, see the fault in another. What we cannot see is the heart of the defaulter, the weight of temptation he struggled under, and his bitter inner penitence. “Granted,” as Carlyle says, “the ship comes into harbour with shrouds and tackle damaged; the pilot is blameworthy. He has not been all-wise and all-powerful. But, to know how blameworthy, tell us first whether his voyage has been round the globe, or only to Ramsgate and the Isle of Dogs.”

The way to get the best out of people is to think the best about them. Let a man see that you have good hopes of him, and recognise what is best in him, and, in ways of which science can give no explanation, you add to his chances of reaching better things. In any case, who would not wish to stand on Christ’s side rather than on Judas’s. “This ointment might have been sold for three hundred pence and given to the poor.” That is Judas. “Let her alone. Why trouble ye her? She hath wrought a good work in me. She hath done what she could.” That is Jesus Christ.

===Chinese===
没有人会说，失败之后重新凝聚力量再试一次是容易的；可是几乎所有大人物都非做这件事不可。自己背着沉重担子的时候，还要对一个境遇其实好得多的人说句宽慰的话、伸出同情的手，这自然也不容易——但此刻正有许多人在这样做。尼禄的皇宫，是这世上最想不到能找到一群忠贞基督徒的地方；然而那里恰恰有这样的人——「该撒家里的圣徒」。那使之成为可能的上帝的恩典，也能照样成就所有这些较小的奇迹。

第二，*逢人想着最好*。有一则动人的传说，讲耶稣曾这样显明他自己：一群闲人聚在街上，围着一条死狗。一个说它那层皮多么肮脏邋遢；另一个说：「好丑的耳朵！」这时一个陌生人走上前来，说道：「珍珠也没有它的牙齿白！」人们便彼此议论：「这一定是拿撒勒人耶稣，因为除了他，谁也不能连在一条死狗身上都找出好处来。」诚然，最像基督的男女，其标记正在于此：他们宁可着意强调那微乎其微的善的斑点，也不肯痛斥那太过显眼的恶。别人的过失，我们看得太容易了；我们看不见的，是那过失之人的心，是他挣扎其下的试探之重，是他内心苦涩的悔恨。卡莱尔（Carlyle）说得好：「就算承认船进港时帆缆索具俱已损坏，领航员难辞其咎，他并非全知全能；但要知道他该受多重的责备，请先告诉我们：他这一趟航行，是环绕了地球一周，还是只到过拉姆斯盖特和狗岛。」

要让人显出他最好的一面，方法就是往好处想他。让一个人看见你对他怀着良好的期望，看见你赏识他身上最好的东西，那么，以一种科学无法解释的方式，你就增添了他抵达更好之境的机会。无论如何，谁不愿站在基督一边，而站在犹大一边呢？「这香膏本可以卖三十两银子周济穷人。」——那是犹大。「由她吧！为什么难为她呢？她在我身上做的是一件美事。她所作的，是尽她所能的。」——那是耶稣基督。

===Original===
Third—Don’t leave yourself out of the picture. *Hope the best for yourself.* George Eliot, in her *Scenes of Clerical Life*, gives, in one chapter, an account of how the Rev. Amos Barton is criticised and discussed in his parish. In the next chapter we see the Rev. Amos himself going on his way blissfully unconscious of the poor opinion in which he is held, believing quite honestly in himself, and not a little proud of his abilities. “We are poor plants,” says this keen student of character, “buoyed up by the air vessels of our own conceit.” And a blessed thing, too, when you think of it! If we only knew all the disparaging remarks people make about us, we should never face up to our duties at all. What helps us along is our innocent belief in our powers, in the esteem in which we are held—our little conceits, if you like. Since they send us to our tasks with more spirit, and keep us at them with more determination, aren’t they good things in their way? They are indeed just a lower form of that hope that we are speaking of—Hope’s poor relations.

If these are of such value, how much more pure quiet steady Hope itself, purged of all pride and undue self-esteem? Hope the best for yourself, and you are already a good way on the road to it. Suggestion is a tremendously powerful instrument, even when you make it yourself. By self suggestion, the psychologists tell us, you can influence your actions, your character, and your general outlook in a wonderful fashion, either to your advantage or your hurt. Therefore, they say, be careful never to suggest evil to yourself. Never say to yourself, “I’m going to make a mess of this,” or “I am not fit for that.” Suggest success, happiness, health, and you beckon them to you. Hope the best for yourself, and you pave the way for its coming.

On higher planes, the same holds true. Hope on, and, though you fall you will rise again. Believe that you will be enabled to face your trouble or temptation, and you will be brought through it somehow. Even when the end of life is near, hope still, for beyond this best there is a better, and God’s road winds uphill all the way.

But, you say, this is just faith. I know it is. Run your hopes for yourself up as high as you can reach, and they will touch God and become faith. That is why you are to hope the best for yourself. Because—God. Because God the Father loves you, and desires the best for you too. I believe in the optimism which Stephenson’s motto embodies, because I believe in the Fatherhood of God through our Lord Jesus Christ. That is why I counsel you to go on hoping that the best is yet to be. Not that we can earn it at all, or that we deserve it at all. But—because God, our Father. And, for the daring and faith of that saying, this sufficient ground.—Because—Jesus Christ.

===Chinese===
第三——别把自己撇在画面之外。*对己盼望最好*。乔治·艾略特（George Eliot）在《教区生活场景》（Scenes of Clerical Life）里用一章的篇幅，记述埃莫斯·巴顿牧师（Rev. Amos Barton）如何在教区里受人批评议论；到了下一章，我们却看见巴顿牧师本人浑然不觉人们对他的轻看，照旧心满意足地走自己的路，实实在在地相信自己，还颇为为自己的才干自豪。「我们是可怜的植物，」这位洞察人性的高手说，「靠着自己自负的气囊撑浮着。」可细想起来，这未尝不是一件福气！倘若我们尽知人们在背后对我们的贬语，我们就再也不敢面对自己的职分了。扶助我们前行的，是我们对自身能力的无辜信赖，是我们对自己所受尊重的天真想望——你尽可以称之为小小的自负。既然它们使我们更起劲地奔赴职分、更坚定地坚守不移，它们难道不也算得上好东西吗？它们其实正是我们所说的那种盼望的低级形态——盼望的穷亲戚。

这些尚且如此有价值，何况那纯粹、安静、坚定的盼望本身——一切骄傲与过分的自高都涤除净尽的盼望呢？对己盼望最好，你就已经在这条路上走出很远了。暗示是一件威力巨大的器具，哪怕是你给自己作的暗示。心理学家告诉我们，借着自我暗示，你可以奇妙地影响自己的行为、性格与整个人生态度——或使你受益，或使你受损。所以他们说，务必小心，千万不要向自己暗示邪恶。永远不要对自己说：「这件事我要搞砸了」，或「我干不了那个」。去暗示成功、幸福与健康吧，你就是在向它们招手。对己盼望最好，你就已经为它的到来铺平了道路。

在更高的层面上，道理依然如此。继续盼望吧，纵然跌倒，你也必再起来。相信你必蒙能力去面对你的患难或试探，你也终必被带领走过。哪怕生命的终点已在眼前，仍要盼望，因为在这最好之外还有更好，而上帝的道路蜿蜒曲折，却一路向上。

可你要说，这不过是信心罢了。我知道正是。把你对自己的盼望升到你力所能及的最高处，它们就会触到上帝，变成信心。这正是你当对己盼望最好的缘故。因为——上帝。因为天父上帝爱你，也为你盼望最好。我之所以相信斯蒂芬森的格言所体现的那种乐观主义，是因为我借着我主耶稣基督，相信上帝是父。所以我劝你继续盼望，相信最好的尚在前头。这并非我们能赚得丝毫，也非我们配得分毫；乃是——因为上帝，我们的天父。而这句满有胆量与信心的话，有这充足的根据——因为——耶稣基督。

===Original===
Prayer

Help us all, Heavenly Father, to meet the discipline of life with stouter hearts. May we all try harder to cultivate the Christlike mark of charity. And spite of our many sins and shortcomings, and our poor love of Thee, grant us the courage to believe that all things, in Thy great Love for us, are working together for our good. We ask it for Jesus’ sake. Amen.

===Chinese===
祈祷

天父啊，求你帮助我们众人，以更刚强的心去迎接人生的操练。愿我们都更加努力，培养那基督般的仁爱标记。尽管我们有诸多的罪与亏欠，对你的爱又如此贫乏，仍求你赐我们勇气，相信在你对我们的大爱里，万事都正互相效力，叫我们得益处。我们是奉耶稣的名求的。阿们。

## IV Finical Farming / IV 过分讲究的耕作

===Original===
> "He that observeth the wind shall not sow, and he that regardeth the clouds shall not reap."
> — Ecclesiastes 11:4

===Chinese===
> 「看风的必不撒种，望云的必不收割。」
> — Ecclesiastes 11:4

===Original===
When a man like the writer of Ecclesiastes gives his views on life, it is worth everybody's while to listen. A tabloid of experience is worth a ton of theory. And it is from his own knowledge of men and experience of life that he has discovered that "he that observeth the wind shall not sow, and he that regardeth the clouds shall not reap."

Was ever a temper of mind, that we all know something about, more neatly hit off than that? You can see the very picture which this wise preacher had before his eyes. Agricola was a farmer in his parish who would not sow his fields unless the wind was blowing soft and gentle from a certain direction, and the clouds were just as he wished to see them. He held there was no hope of a harvest unless wind and clouds were right. And I observed, says the wise man, that Agricola, my farmer friend, waiting for the exactly suitable conditions, never got his seed in at all.

===Chinese===
像《传道书》作者这样的人对人生发表见解时，人人都值得花工夫一听。一小块浓缩的经验，抵得过一吨理论。他正是凭着他对人的洞察、对人生的阅历，才发现了「看风的必不撒种，望云的必不收割」这个道理。

我们人人都有几分体会的那种心境，还有比这描摹得更传神的吗？你几乎能看见这位智慧的传道者眼前浮现的那幅图画。阿格里科拉（Agricola）是他教区里的一位农夫：若不是风正从一个特定方向柔和轻轻吹着，云也恰恰是他爱看的样子，他便不肯把田种上。他认定，风与云若不合适，收成就全无指望。智慧人说，我留心观察到，我的农夫朋友阿格里科拉，就这样一直等候着恰到好处的条件，结果种子始终没能下过地。

===Original===
He was speaking chiefly about benevolence and charity when he used this figure. And that is one reason why we need to give heed to it. For ours is an age of charity. We give more to the poor and needy today than ever any nation gave before. It is said, indeed, that a good deal of our giving is not very wise. Our charities overlap. The truly necessitous are forgotten, and the improvident, the lazy, and the wasteful reap the largest share. Certainly that is one of the perils of charity-giving. But I question very much if, in our efforts to avoid it, we are not running the risk of falling into a graver mistake still, namely, of observing the wind overmuch before we sow. If I refuse to give my mite for Christ's sake till I have made perfectly certain that it will not be misused, if we withhold our subscription from a charity till we are assured that it is managed in the very most economical fashion, it will end in us giving nothing at all. There is, of course, a reasonable amount of inquiry that is not only legitimate but necessary. Just as there is a regarding of the clouds before reaping which is simply wise. But, to wait till every scruple is satisfied, till every risk has been eliminated and there is not a cloud in the sky, is to wait for a state of matters that may be long enough in coming. Meantime the needy person may die; or the corn blacken in the fields.

Charity, however, is but a small part of Christian benevolence. The law of Christ says "neighbour" whether he be poor or not. He is in trouble, and I feel inclined to visit him. Must I wait till I am sure he will not misunderstand my motive? I have it in my heart to forgive him. Shall I defer the reconciliation till I am convinced he will not offend again? Or I have hurt and offended him, and wish to apologise. Had I not better wait till I know that he will not reject my advances? The wise man's answer to all these questions is an emphatic "no." If you wait for all that, he says, you will wait too long, and the chance will go past. Wait till the wind and the clouds are just as you would wish them, and you will neither sow nor reap at all.

===Chinese===
他说这个比喻时，主要针对的是仁爱与慈善。这正是我们需要留意这话的一个缘由。因为我们所处的时代是个慈善的时代。今天我们施给贫穷困乏之人的，比以往任何国家所施的都多。诚然，据说我们的捐输有许多并不十分明智：各慈善事业彼此重叠，真正穷乏的人反倒被遗忘，而那不为将来打算的、懒惰的、挥霍的，却收去了最大的份额。这固然是施舍的一大危险。但我很怀疑，我们在力图避免这危险时，是否正冒着陷入一个更严重错误的风险——那就是，撒种之前把风看得太多。倘若我非等到全然确知它必不致被滥用，才肯为基督的缘故拿出我那微末的奉献；倘若我们非等到确证某项慈善是以最最节省的方式管理，才肯解囊认捐——那么到头来，我们会一分钱也拿不出来。当然，适度的查问不仅正当，而且必要；正如收割之前看看云色，本身就是智慧。但若要等到一切顾虑都得到满足，等到一切风险都被消除、天上再无一丝云彩，那就是在等候一种不知何日才会到来的光景。而在这等候期间，穷乏人或许已经死去；田里的五谷或许已经发黑。

然而，慈善不过是基督徒仁爱中的一小部分。基督的律法说的是「邻舍」，无论他穷或不穷。他遭遇患难，我心里想去探望他。难道必须等到确知他不会误解我的动机，才能去吗？我心里愿意饶恕他。难道要等到确信他不会再冒犯我，才肯与他和好吗？或者，是我伤害了他、得罪了他，我想道歉。难道最好还是等到知道他不会拒绝我的示好，再去吗？对这一切问题，智慧人的回答是一声响亮的「不」。他说，你若等着这一切，就等得太久，机会也就过去了。等到风与云都恰如你愿，你就要落到既不撒种、也不收割的地步了。

===Original===
What to do, then? The wise man answers: "In the morning sow thy seed, and in the evening withhold not thy hand, for thou knowest not whether shall prosper, either this or that, or whether they both shall be alike good." Just because you can never fully calculate what the result of your labours may be, give up trying. Don't trouble about it, but do what comes to your hand at the time. If it is sowing time, don't wait for the perfect day. If the weather will do at all, sow thy seed in the morning, and in the evening do not stop. In other words, Take life more royally. Do not be deterred by its ordinary risks. Seize your chance like a brave man. You do not know, of course, whether that seed you sow will prosper or not. But sow it, all the same. Don't let the fact that you don't know cause you to hold your hand. It is just because you do not know but that the kindness which you offer your neighbour may be ill-requited, that there is a royal freehanded self-forgetfulness in offering it. That a man should live his life and do his good deeds with a certain dash and carelessness of consequence—that, the Preacher thought the ideal of noble living. And when we measure it by the standard of Him who said, Do good and lend, hoping for nothing again, it does not seem to come so very far short.

For, of course, there are the continual surprises that life holds for faith. If only the corn reaped when the clouds were just right was safely gathered in, then indeed we might feel that we could not be too careful. But what do we find again and again? Why, we find that men who have had the faith to sow when the day was by no means perfect have been blessed beyond their expectations. We find our barns full and running over, though we reaped on a cloudy day. We have seen men cast their bread upon the waters, where you would say it was certain to be lost, and find it again, after many days. It's perfectly true that you don't know whether shall prosper this or that. Yet how often have you been surprised to find that where you thought you knew, you were proved mistaken, and where you dealt in faith, it stood justified beyond your dreams.

===Chinese===
那么，该怎么办呢？智慧人回答说：「早晨要撒你的种，晚上也不要歇你的手，因为你不知道哪一样发旺；或是早撒的，或是晚撒的，或是两样都好。」正因为你永远无法全然算定你的劳碌会有怎样的结果，就索性不要去算。不要为这事烦扰，只管做当时来到你手边的事。若到了撒种的时节，就不要等候完美的天气。只要天色尚且过得去，早晨就撒你的种，晚上也不要停手。换言之，要把人生过得更豪迈些。不要被人生寻常的风险吓退。要像勇士一样抓住你的机会。你自然不知道你所撒的种子发旺不发旺。但尽管撒下去就是了。不要因为不知道，就把手缩住。恰恰因为你不知道你向邻舍所施的恩惠或许得不到好报，这施与之中才有一种豪迈的、慷慨忘我的气度。人生在世，行善处世都带着几分洒脱，不计较后果——在传道者看来，这才是高贵生活的理想。而当我们用那一位的标准来衡量——他曾说，要行善，要借给人，不指望偿还——这似乎也相去不远。

况且，人生本就为信心预备了接连不断的惊喜。倘若唯有在云色恰到好处时收割的五谷才能安然入仓，那我们的确会觉得，再怎样小心也不为过。可我们一次又一次发现的是什么呢？瞧，我们发现，那些在天气远非完美之日仍凭信心撒种的人，蒙了超过他们所想的福。我们发现，尽管是在阴天收割，我们的仓房却满了，满得无处可容。我们见过有人把粮食撒在水面，照你看来必是丢失无疑，过了多日却又寻了回来。你不知道这个还是那个发旺，这话千真万确。然而你何等常常惊奇地发现：在你自以为知道的地方，你被证明是错了；而在你凭信心行事的地方，它站立得住，得了超乎你梦想的辩白。

===Original===
And so, the end of the matter for the Preacher is, once more, Live your life royally, with a certain loving wastefulness, and an easy disregard of calculations. Do all the good you can, and do it with a free hand, not asking to see your harvest before you sow, but taking your risk of it, and leaving the outcome with God. "Cast your bread on the waters, and you will find it after many days."

But what of the bread one has cast on the waters, only to see it carried away, apparently of no use to anybody? What of the faith that has not been justified? What of the good done to the ill-deserving, of the kindly-meant act repaid with indignity and scorn? It is a hard question, not easy to answer, not fully to be answered at all. "After many days," said the Preacher. And there is no sign yet, we say. Patience, brothers, patience! God's day is not yet done. When the days have run out to the end, it will be time enough to say if we miss the bread returning. We shall be better able to count the gains and the losses, if there are any then—when the "days" are done.

===Chinese===
所以，在传道者看来，这事的总结仍旧是一句话：要把人生豪迈地过，带着几分出于爱的挥霍，几分对算计的从容超脱。尽你所能行一切善事，出手大方慷慨，不要求先看见收成才肯撒种，宁可担起其中的风险，把结局交在上帝手中。「当将你的粮食撒在水面，因为日久必能得着。」

可是，那撒在水面、却只见它漂走、显然对谁都没有用处的粮食，又怎么说呢？那从未得着印证的信心，又怎么说呢？那善待不配之人、出于好意的行为反被报以羞辱与讥诮，又怎么说呢？这是个难题，不易回答，也无法全然回答。「过了多日，」传道者说。「可是如今还不见一点踪影，」我们说。忍耐吧，弟兄们，忍耐！上帝的日子还没有过完。等到日子跑到了尽头，那时再说我们是否错过了归回的粮食，也还来得及。到那「多日」完结之时——倘若那时还有得失可言——我们才能把得失数算得更清楚。

===Original===
Prayer
Teach us, O Lord and Master, the high and difficult lesson that only those who lose their lives shall truly find them. Show us that the manna hoarded in miserly fashion is always touched by Thy curse. In small things as in great, may this be a token that we are Thy disciples, that virtue also goeth out of us. Amen.

===Chinese===
祈祷
我们的主、我们的夫子啊，求你教导我们那崇高而艰难的功课：惟有失丧生命的，才真寻得生命。求你指给我们看，那像守财奴般囤积的吗哪，总沾着你的咒诅。无论小事大事，愿这都作凭据，证明我们是你的门徒——能力也从我们身上出去。阿们。

## V The Doctor / V 医生

===Original===
> “But when Jesus heard that, he said unto them, they that be whole need not a physician, but they that are sick.”
> — Matthew 9:12

===Chinese===
> 耶稣听见，就说：「康健的人用不着医生，有病的人才用得着。」
> — Matthew 9:12

===Original===
Jesus is Himself the best witness as to what He was, and what He wished to do for men. It is a fact, moreover, for which we cannot be too thankful that, in explaining Himself, Jesus used not the language of doctrine, but living figures and symbols which the humblest and youngest could not fail to understand.

When, for example, He compared Himself to a shepherd leaving the ninety and nine in the fold and braving the darkness and the steep places that he might bring back the one that had wandered, He opens a window into His own love for men which is worth pages of description. For those who are familiar with the daily life and work of a shepherd, it means a great deal that Jesus waits to be the Shepherd of men.

But, in these very different days of ours, there are multitudes in streets and tenements who have never seen a shepherd, and know not what manner of life is his. So that one is glad that Jesus gave Himself other names as well. When Matthew Arnold met the pale-faced preacher in the slums of Bethnal Green, and asked him how he did—

===Chinese===
论到他自己曾是怎样的一位、他愿意为世人做什么，耶稣自己就是最好的见证。况且还有一件事实，令我们感激不尽：耶稣在说明他自己的时候，用的不是教义的言语，而是鲜活的比喻与象征，哪怕最卑微、最年幼的人也不致无法领会。

举例来说，当他把自己比作一个牧人——把九十九只羊撇在圈里，冒着黑暗与险峻的山坡，去把那一只迷失的寻回来——他便推开了通向他爱世人之心的那扇窗，这比几页的描述更有价值。对于熟悉牧人日常起居与劳作的人，耶稣甘愿作世人的牧人，这意味深长。

但在我们这些大不相同的年日里，街头巷尾与出租楼房之间有无数的人，从未见过牧人，也不晓得牧人的日子是怎样过的。因此我们高兴，耶稣也给了自己别的名字。当马修·阿诺德（Matthew Arnold）在贝斯纳尔格林（Bethnal Green）的贫民窟遇见那位面容苍白的传道人，问他近来如何时——

===Original===
> “Bravely,” he said, “for I of late have been
> Much cheered with thoughts of Christ,
> the Living Bread.”

===Chinese===
> 「勇敢地，」他说，「因为近来我时常
> 因想到基督——
> 那生命的粮——而大得鼓舞。」

===Original===
If that name for Christ brought him comfort, another preacher may be allowed to confess that he has often been cheered and helped by the thought of Jesus as the Good Physician. I am glad that in effect, at least, if not in actual words, He called Himself by that name.

This is His apology for consorting with publicans and sinners, for being so accessible to those who had lost caste and character. He says it is the sick who need a physician, not those who are well. And His defence implies that Jesus regarded Himself as being in a true sense a physician, not for outward ills merely, but for the whole man, body, mind, and spirit.

The days were, as you know, when priest and physician were one calling; and it is doubtless to the advantage of both vocations that their spheres are now distinct. But it may be, and I think it is, unfortunate that Jesus should be regarded by many as so entirely identified with the priestly side of life and the priestly calling. It is beyond question that a faithful priest is, in his degree, a mirror of Christ, and helps men to see Him more clearly. But it is also true—and a truth worth underlining in these days—that the Doctor, too, is a symbol of what Christ means to be to men—nay, more, that there are respects in which the figure of a beloved physician of today comes nearer to the reality of the living human Christ than any other calling in the world.

===Chinese===
倘若基督的这个名使那位传道人得了安慰，那么也当容许另一位传道人承认：他常常因想到耶稣是那位良医（Good Physician）而得鼓舞、得帮助。我很高兴，他纵使没有明说，至少在实际上，是用这名称呼了自己。

这就是他与税吏和罪人来往、向那些失了体面与名声之人敞开门户的辩白。他说，需要医生的是有病的人，不是康健的人。而他的申辩表明，耶稣看自己实在是一位医生——不只是医治外面的病症，更是医治整个的人：身体、心思与灵性。

你们知道，从前祭司与医生曾同属一行；如今两职各分其域，无疑对彼此都有好处。然而，许多人竟把耶稣与生活中祭司的一面、与祭司的职分看得完全等同，这或许是一件憾事——我想确实是。毫无疑问，一位忠心的祭司，就在他的限度内，也是一面映出基督的镜子，能帮助人更清楚地看见他。但同样真实——而且是这些日子里值得划线标出的真理——医生同样是一个表征，表征着基督愿意在世人中间是怎样的一位；不但如此，在某些方面，今日那位受人爱戴的医生的形象，比世上任何别的职分都更接近那位活着的、为人的基督的实在。

===Original===
It is a sure and unique place which the Doctor holds in the esteem and confidence of the community. He is the most accessible of all professional men, the most implicitly trusted, and, I think, the best beloved. At all hours of the day and night he is ready to give his services to those who need him. His mere presence in the sick room inspires confidence. In the poor districts of town and city especially, he is more really the friend and confidant and helper of everybody than any other person whatever. As no other man does, the Doctor goes about continually doing good. His life is a constant self-sacrifice for his fellow-men. He wears himself out in the interests of the needy. He runs risks daily from which other men flee. He asks not to be ministered unto, but to minister, and often and literally he gives his life a ransom for many.

And I do not know what we have been thinking of that we have not oftener made use of this as Christ’s claim for Himself, that we have not told the ignorant and the very poor especially, who know far more about the Doctor than they do about the Church, who are, in fact, shy of all that is priestly, but who do understand and appreciate the Doctor, I say, I do not know why we have not oftener told them to forget that Jesus is the King and Head of the Church and remember only that He is the best of all physicians. That Christ is compassionate, sympathetic, and approachable, like the Doctor, would be veritable good news to many a poor ignorant soul who is mightily afraid of His priests.

The word which comes to our lips when we seek to characterise the life and work of the true Doctor is Christlike. And big as the title is, it is deserved. In sacrifice and self-forgetfulness, in his care most for those who most need him, in the way he identifies himself with his patient, bearing with, because understanding, his weakness and petulance and fears, and seeking all the while only to heal and help and save him, there is no more Christlike character or calling in the modern world than the Doctor.

===Chinese===
医生在众人的敬重与信任中所占的位置，稳固而独一无二。在一切专业人员中，他是最平易可亲的，是最受人全然信赖的，我想，也是最蒙爱戴的。无论白昼黑夜，任何时辰，他都随时准备服事那需要他的人。他在病室里的出现本身，就叫人信心顿生。尤其在城镇的贫民区，他比任何别人都更真实地是众人的朋友、知己与帮助者。医生周流四方、行善不辍，这是无人能比的。他的生命，是为同胞长年不断的自我牺牲。他为穷乏人的缘故耗尽自己。他天天冒着旁人惟恐避之不及的风险。他不是要受人服事，乃是要服事人，并且常常实实在在地舍命，作多人的赎价。

我真不知道我们一向在想些什么，竟没有更常常拿这一点来作基督为自己所作的宣告；竟没有告诉那些无知的人和极贫穷的人——他们对医生的了解远多于对教会的了解，他们实在惧怕一切带着祭司气派的事物，却确实能明白也能赏识医生——我说，我不知道我们为什么没有更常常告诉他们：把耶稣是教会的君王与元首这件事且先忘掉吧，只记住他是一切医生中最好的那一位。基督像医生那样满怀怜悯、体恤人意、平易可亲——对许多穷苦无知的、又极度惧怕他的祭司的灵魂来说，这就是实实在在的好消息。

当我们想要刻画一位真正的医生的生涯与工作时，最先涌到唇边的词就是「像基督」（Christlike）。这称号虽大，却当之无愧。他舍己牺牲、忘却自己；他最挂念的，正是最需要他的人；他与病人认同，因为懂得，所以容忍病人的软弱、烦躁与恐惧，并且自始至终只求医治他、帮助他、拯救他——在现今的世界上，再没有比医生更像基督的品格或职分了。

===Original===
I am the happy possessor of an engraving—a gift from one whose calling is to teach doctors—of Luke Fildes’ famous picture. Most of you doubtless are familiar with it. It represents the interior of a humble home where a little child lies critically ill. The father and mother, distracted with grief, have yielded their place beside the couch to the Doctor, who sits watching and waiting, all-absorbed in the little one’s trouble. It is a noble face, strong, compassionate, resourceful, gentle; and if the Eternal Christ of God is to be represented to us in His strength and gentleness by any human analogy or likeness whatever, as He wished to be, and indeed must be, no finer figure could be found, I think, than that, none more certain to draw out the reverence and gratitude and trust of men.

Men of all grades and classes appeal to and trust the Doctor. But how many of them realise that Jesus desires that men should come to Him and trust His willingness to help and save them, just as they would do to some good physician? How many men who have found comfort by taking their fears and forebodings to the Doctor and hearing his authoritative “Go in peace!” know or realise that just so would Jesus have us bring Him our unworthiness and shame and sin? Jesus never preached at those whom His compassion drew to Him. He never lectured them, He just helped them, and that at once. He lifted them to their feet and gave them a new hope. He, straightway, in God’s name, assured them of forgiveness.

Ah, if men only understood that Jesus is to be found today down among the world’s burdened and weary souls, not as a priest begirt with ceremony and aloof from daily life, but as a physician, approachable, helpful, human, who sees and pities their weakness, and longs to save them and help them to their best. If men only understood that!

===Chinese===
我幸运地藏有一幅版画——出自一位以教导医生为业的人所赠——画的是卢克·菲尔兹（Luke Fildes）那幅名画。诸位多半都熟悉它。它画的是一处寒微人家的内室，一个小孩病危躺在那里。父亲和母亲悲痛欲绝，已把病榻旁的位置让给了医生；医生坐在那里守望等候，整个人都系在那小小孩子的病痛上。那是何等高贵的面容：坚毅、怜悯、沉着有方、温柔；倘若上帝那永恒的基督，果真要——如他所愿、也如他必然要的——借着某种人间的比拟或形像，把他的刚强与温柔向我们表现出来，我想，再找不出比这更好的形像了，也再没有哪一个更能唤起人的敬畏、感激与信靠。

各等各阶层的人都来求告医生、信赖医生。可他们当中有几人明白，耶稣也巴望人们来到他面前，像信任某位好医生那样，信靠他乐于帮助他们、拯救他们？有多少人曾把恐惧与不祥的预感带给医生，听见他那句斩钉截铁的「平平安安地去吧！」便得了安慰——这些人当中有几个知道、有几个人意识到，耶稣也正要我们照样把我们的不配、羞耻与罪带到他面前？耶稣从不对那些被他的怜悯吸引到他面前的人说教。他从不训斥他们，他只是帮助他们，并且是立刻帮助。他扶他们起来，赐给他们新的盼望。他随即奉上帝的名，向他们保证赦免。

啊，但愿人能明白：今天要寻见耶稣，须到世上那些负重而困乏的灵魂中间去寻；他不是一位被仪文缠裹、远离日常生活的祭司，而是一位医生——平易可亲、乐于相助、有着人的体温；他看见他们的软弱并且怜恤，他渴望拯救他们，扶助他们达到各自的最好。但愿人能明白这个！

===Original===
Prayer
We come to Thee, Thou Good Physician, with all our ills and fears. We would whisper in Thine ear the troubles that frighten and shame us. Surely Thou wilt hear. Draw near us in Thy strength and pity, and in Thy mercy heal us all. Amen.

===Chinese===
祈祷
良医啊，我们带着一切的病痛与恐惧来到你面前。我们要在你耳边轻诉那些叫我们惊惶、叫我们羞愧的重负。你必定垂听。求你带着你的刚强与怜恤亲近我们，并在你的怜悯中医治我们众人。阿们。

## VI Well and Now / VI 做好，趁现在

===Original===
> “Whatsoever thy hand findeth to do, do it with thy might, for there is no work nor device nor knowledge nor wisdom in the grave whither thou goest.”
> — Ecclesiastes 9:10

===Chinese===
> 「凡你手所当作的事，要尽力去做；因为在你所必去的阴间没有工作，没有谋算，没有知识，也没有智慧。」
> — Ecclesiastes 9:10

===Original===
In popular and condensed form, the golden rule according to Ecclesiastes is, “Do it well and do it now.” His own words are, “Whatsoever thy hand findeth to do, do it with thy might, for there is no work nor device nor knowledge nor wisdom in the grave whither thou goest.” We want to let that precept soak into our minds for a little.

*Do it well*. “Whatsoever thy hand findeth to do, do it with thy might.” Among the lesser joys of life there are few that thrill one with a more pleasurable sense of satisfaction than that which goes with the bit of work finished, rounded-off and done as well as one can do it. No matter what the job may be, if it is worth doing at all, or if it is one’s business to do it, it is not difficult to recognise in the curious inward glow over its honourable completion, a token of God’s good pleasure, some far-off echo of His “Well done!”

It is a truism which never loses its point that it is enthusiasm that commands success. In her weird book called *Dreams*, Olive Schreiner tells the parable of an artist who painted a beautiful picture. On it there was a wonderful glow which drew the admiration of all his compeers, but which none could imitate. The other painters said, Where did he get his colours? But though they sought rich and rare pigments in far-off Eastern lands they could not catch the secret of it. One day the artist was found dead beside his picture, and when they stripped him for his shroud they found a wound beneath his heart. Then it dawned upon them where he had got his colour. He had painted his picture with his own heart’s blood! It is the only way to paint it, if the picture is to be worth while at all. If we would have the work that we do live and count, our heart’s blood must go into it. Whatsoever thy hand findeth to do, do it with thy might.

===Chinese===
照通俗而浓缩的说法，《传道书》里的金科玉律便是：「做好它，现在就做。」他自己的原话是：「凡你手所当作的事，要尽力去做；因为在你所必去的阴间没有工作，没有谋算，没有知识，也没有智慧。」我们要让这条训诲在我们的心思里浸润片刻。

**做好它**。「凡你手所当作的事，要尽力去做。」在人生诸般细微的欢乐中，很少有什么比一件工作的大功告成更叫人心里涌起甜美满足的震颤——那活计已经完成、已经收尾，而且是照你所能做到的最好程度做成的。无论那活计是什么，只要它值得去做，或者做它本是你的本分，那么，当它体面告成之际，你心中那阵奇异的内在光焰并不难辨认：那是上帝悦纳的记号，是祂那句「做得好！」的悠远回声。

惟有热忱能赢得成功——这句老生常谈永远不会失去锋芒。奥丽芙·施赖纳（Olive Schreiner）在她那本奇书《梦想》（*Dreams*）里讲过一则寓言：有位画家画成了一幅美画。画上有一道奇妙的光焰，赢得同行人人叹服，却无人能够模仿。别的画家问：他的颜色是从哪里得来的？他们远赴东方异域寻求浓艳珍稀的颜料，却始终窥不破其中的奥秘。一天，人们发现画家死在他的画旁；为他更衣入殓时，见他心口底下有一道伤口。众人这才恍然大悟，他的颜色从何而来——他是用自己的心血画成了那幅画！倘若那幅画真要有几分价值，也唯有这样去画。我们要想让自己的工作有生命、有分量，就必须把自己的心血浇灌进去。凡你手所当作的事，要尽力去做。

===Original===
What magnificent heart-stirring examples are coming to us every day just now, from sea and battlefield, of the good old British virtue of sticking in gamely to the end and “seeing the thing through!” If the stories of the old English admirals are calculated, as Stevenson says, to “send bank clerks back with more heart and spirit to their bookkeeping by double entry,” shall not the story that unfolds day by day of what our own kith and kin are doing, nerve and inspire us all to “do *our* bit,” to face up to *our* duty, humdrum and ordinary though it be, with the same grit and energy, with the same determination to see it through, and make as good a job of it as we can?

The Preacher has his reason for this advice. Because, he says, some day you will have to stop and lay down your tools, and that will be the end. No more touching botched work after that. No going back to lift dropped stitches then. Such as it is, your record will have to stand as you leave it, when Death raps at your door. Even for us in this Christian age, this ancient Preacher’s reason still stands valid and solemn. Do what you are at now as well as ever you can, for you shall pass that way no more again forever.

The Apostle Paul, who expresses practically the same sentiment, gives a different reason. “Whatever ye do,” he writes to the Colossians, “do it heartily as to the Lord.” And that is the point for you and me. Not merely because we have a limited time to work, but because our work is Christ’s service, we must do it heartily, with all our might. It is to the Lord. To us all in our different labours, in the things we work at day by day, and the worthy interests we endeavour to support, there comes this call that transforms the very commonest duty into an honourable obligation to a personal living Master—Whatever ye do, do it heartily as to the Lord.

===Chinese===
眼下每一天，海上与战场都向我们送来何等激动人心的榜样，显出英国人那股咬牙坚持到底、善始善终「把事情办完」的古老美德！倘若昔日英国海军上将的故事，如史蒂文森（Stevenson）所说，足以「叫银行职员们心头更热、精神更足，回到复式簿记的账册前」，那么，我们的骨肉至亲日日展开的事迹，岂不更该使我们众人得着胆气与激励，去尽「我们」的那一份力，直面「我们」的本分——纵然它平凡单调——拿出同样的坚忍与干劲，拿出同样坚持到底的决心，把事情做到我们所能及的最好？

传道者劝这句话，自有他的理由。他说，因为有一天你必须停下来，放下工具，那便是终局。此后再不能修补做坏了的活，再不能回去补上脱落的针脚。死亡叩门之时，你的记录就只能照你丢下的原样立住了。甚至对我们这些身处基督教时代的人而言，这位古代传道者的理由依然确凿而肃穆。此刻你手头正做的事，要竭尽所能做好，因为这条路你永远不再经过第二遭。

使徒保罗所表达的几乎是同一番情怀，给出的理由却不同。「无论做什么，都要从心里做，像是给主做的，」他写信给歌罗西人如此说。这正是你我问题的关键所在。我们必须从心里做、全力以赴地做，不仅因为做工的时日有限，更因为我们的工作是基督的服事。这是做给主的。我们各人，在各自的劳作里，在日日操持的事务中，在努力扶持的正当事业上，都有这呼召临到，把最寻常不过的本分化作对一位又真又活之主的光荣职分——无论做什么，都要从心里做，像是给主做的。

===Original===
Yes, and *do it now*. For the amount of misery and suffering and remorse that is directly due to putting off the God-given impulse or generous purpose to some other season, is simply incalculable. If all the kind letters had been written when the thought of writing was fresh and insistent—ah me, how many burdened souls would have been the braver and the stronger. If only the friendly visit had been paid when we thought about it—and why wasn’t it? “Never suppose,” says Bagshot, “that you can make up to a neglected friend by going to visit him in a hospital. Repent on your own deathbed, if you like, but not on another’s.”

An old writer on agriculture says that there are seasons when if the husbandman misses a day he falls a whole year behind. But in life the result is often more serious still. When you miss the day, you miss it forever. Wherefore, let us hear the words of the Preacher. If we have a kind purpose in our heart towards any living soul, let us do it now. If we think of beginning a better way of living, let us begin now. If we propose to end our days sworn and surrendered servants and soldiers of the Lord Jesus Christ, let us volunteer now, for this is the day of salvation.

It is said that a great English moralist had engraved on his watch the words, “The night cometh,” so that whenever he looked at the time he might be reminded of the preciousness of the passing moment. The night cometh. How far away it may be, or how near to any one of us, no one of us knows. But near or far it cometh with unhalting step. Wherefore, whatsoever the thing be that is in your heart to do, great or little, for yourself or for others, for man or for God—*do it now*!

===Chinese===
不错，还要——**现在就做**。多少人把上帝所赐的感动、把慷慨的美意推到「改日再说」，由此直接造成的悲苦、伤痛与悔恨，实在无从计算。倘若一切善意的书信，都是在提笔的念头最新鲜、最迫切时写就的——唉，多少负重的心灵本可以因此更勇敢、更刚强。倘若友善的探访在我们想起时就已登门——可为什么没有去呢？「切莫以为，」巴格肖特（Bagshot）说，「冷落了朋友，事后到病房里去看他一趟就能补偿。你要悔改，随你在自己的临终榻前悔改好了，只是不要在别人的病榻前。」

有位古代农学家说过，一年之中有些节候，庄稼人若误了一日，便要整整落后一年。而在人生里，后果往往还要严重。误了那一天，便是永远错过了它。所以，让我们听传道者的话。心里若对世上任何一个活人存着善意，就现在去做。若想开始一种更好的生活，就现在开始。若打算以立誓归降的仆人和士兵的身份终我们之日事奉主耶稣基督，就现在志愿入伍，因为现在正是拯救的日子。

据说有一位英国大道德家，在表壳上刻着「黑夜将到」的字样，好叫他每次看时刻，都被提醒这流逝的一瞬何等宝贵。黑夜将到。它离我们各人是远是近，谁也不知道。但无论远近，它迈着不肯停歇的脚步而来。所以，凡是你心里要做的事，无论大小，无论为自己还是为他人，无论为人还是为上帝——**现在就做**！

===Original===
Prayer

O Lord our God, by whose command it is that man goeth forth to his work and his labour until the evening, grant us all a more earnest regard for the sacredness of each passing moment, and help us to do with our whole heart whatsoever our hand findeth to do. For Jesus’ sake. Amen.

===Chinese===
祈祷

主我们的神啊，人出去做工，劳碌直到晚上，本是凭祢的吩咐；求祢使我们众人更恳切地看重每一个流逝瞬间的神圣，帮助我们尽心竭力，去做凡我们手所当作的事。奉耶稣的名求。阿们。

## VII The “Washen Face” in War Time / VII 战时的「洗净的脸」

===Original===
> “And he washed his face, and went out, and refrained himself, and said, Set on bread.”
> — Genesis 43:31

===Chinese===
> 「他洗了脸出来，勉强隐忍，吩咐人摆饭。」
> — Genesis 43:31

===Original===
That is what Joseph did when his feelings nearly overmastered him at the sight of his brother Benjamin standing before him, all unconscious of who he was. He “sought where to weep,” says the record with quaint matter-of-factness, for of course he did not want his brothers to see him weeping just yet. So “he entered into his chamber and wept there.” But Joseph’s secret affections being thus recognised and allowed their expression, he had a duty to perform. He put a curb upon his feelings. He took a firm grip of himself. He “washed his face and went out, and refrained himself, and said, Set on bread.” One cannot help admiring that. It was a fine thing to do.

And there are two classes of people in our own time in whom one sees this same attitude, and never without a strange stirring of heart.

===Chinese===
弟弟便雅悯站在他面前，全然不知他是谁；约瑟一见之下，情感几乎不能自持，而他所做的正是这样一件事。记载以古朴的平实口吻说，他「急忙寻找可哭之地」，因为他自然还不愿意让哥哥们看见他流泪。于是他「进入自己的屋里，哭了一场」。但约瑟这份深藏的手足之情既已得到承认、得到抒发，他就还有当尽的本分要做。他给情感勒上了缰绳，紧紧地把握住自己。他「洗了脸出来，勉强隐忍，吩咐人摆饭」。这一切叫人不能不钦佩。这实在是一件美事。

而在我们这个时代，有两类人身上正可以见到这种同样的态度，并且每次都免不了令人心头起一种奇异的激动。

===Original===
The first and most honourable are those who have already tasted of the sorrows of war and lost some dear one in the service of King and country. We speak of the courage and sacrifice of our men, and we cannot speak too highly or too gratefully about that. But there is something else that runs it very close, if it does not exceed it, and that is the quiet heroism and endurance of many of those who have been bereaved. Time and again one sees them facing up to all life’s calls upon them with a marvellous spirit of self-restraint. God only knows how sad and sore their loss is. And upon what takes place when they enter into their chamber and shut the door and face their sorrow alone with God, it does not beseem us to intrude. Such sorrow is a sacred thing, but at least we know, and are glad to know, that God Himself is there as He is nowhere else. It is never wrong and never weak to let the tears come before Him. As a father understands, so does He know all about it. As a mother comforteth, so does the touch of His Hand quieten and console.

===Chinese===
第一类也是最高贵的一类，是那些已经尝过战争之苦、在效命君王与国家时失去了至亲的人。我们讲论我们将士的勇气与牺牲，无论说得多么崇高、多么感恩都不为过。可是另有一件事与之紧紧相随，即便不曾超过，至少也不相上下，那就是许多丧亲者身上那种安静的英雄气概与坚忍。一次又一次，你会看见他们以一种令人惊叹的自我克制精神，迎对生活向他们提出的一切要求。他们的损失何等悲伤、何等惨痛，唯有上帝知道。至于他们走进自己的屋子、关上门、独自与上帝面对悲伤时发生了什么，我们不便闯入窥探。这样的悲哀是神圣的事；但我们至少知道，并且乐于知道，上帝亲自在那里，非任何别处可比。在他面前让眼泪流出来，从来不是错，也从不软弱。人间的父亲怎样体察，他也照样尽知这一切；人间的母亲怎样安慰，他手的抚摸也照样使人心平、得安慰。

===Original===
But what fills one with reverent admiration is that so many of those whose hearts we know have been so cruelly wounded have set up a new and noble precedent in the matter of courage and self-control. They are not shirking any of the duties of life. They are claiming no exemptions on the ground of their sorrow, and they excuse themselves from no duty merely because it would hurt. They wear their hurt gently like a flower in the breast. They carry their sorrow like a coronet. Out from their secret chambers they come, with washen face and brave lips to do their duty and refrain themselves. How beautiful it is! What a fine thing to see! The sorrowing mother of a noble young fellow I am proud to have known, said to a friend recently who was marvelling at her fortitude, “My boy was very brave and I must try to be brave, too, for his sake.” Dear, gentle mother! One cannot speak worthily about a spirit so sweet and gracious as that. One can only bow the head and breathe the inward prayer, “God send thee peace, brave heart!” But, surely, to accept sorrow in that fashion is to entertain unawares an angel of God! The feeling which underlies this new etiquette of sorrow with the washen face is not very easily put into words. But it rests, I think, upon the dim sense that the death which ends those young lives on this noble field of battle is something different from the ordinary bleak fact of mortality. If death is ever glorious, it is when it comes to the soldier fighting for a pure and worthy cause. There is something more than sorrow, there is even a quiet and reverent pride in the remembrance that the beloved life was given as “a ransom for many.” When one thinks what we are fighting for, one can hardly deny to the fallen the supreme honour of the words “for Christ’s sake.” And it is not death to fall so. Rather is it the finding of life larger and more glorious still. It is that that marks the war-mourners of today as a caste royal and apart. It is that that moves so many of them by an inward instinct to wear their sorrow royally. Hidden in the heart of their grief is a tender and wistful pride. Lowell has put this feeling into very fine words:

===Chinese===
但最令人肃然起敬的是：我们知道这些人的心曾受何等残酷的创伤，而其中竟有这么多的人，在勇气与自制的问题上立下了一个崭新而高贵的典范。他们不逃避生活的任何本分。他们不因悲伤而要求任何豁免，也绝不因为尽某项本分会痛就推托不做。他们把伤痛温柔地佩戴着，像胸前的一朵花；他们把悲伤承担起来，像一顶冠冕。他们从隐秘的内室走出来，带着洗净的脸（washen face）与坚毅的嘴唇，去尽他们的本分，去克制他们自己。这是何等美丽！看在眼里是何等美好！有一位高贵的青年，我以认识他为荣；他那哀伤的母亲近来对一位惊叹她坚毅的朋友说：「我的孩子非常勇敢，为了他的缘故，我也必须努力勇敢。」亲爱而温柔的母亲啊！对这样一种甜美、仁厚的精神，人的言语是不配的；只能低下头来，在心中默祷一句：「愿上帝赐你平安，勇敢的心！」然而，以这样的方式领受悲哀，岂不正是不知不觉间接待了上帝的天使！这种以洗净的脸为表征的新哀悼礼节，其底下的情感并不容易用言语道出。但我想，它立足于一种朦胧的感觉：在这片高贵的战场上终结那些年轻生命的死亡，与寻常那种冰冷无情的死亡事实，是有所不同的。倘若死亡也有荣耀可言，那便是在它临到一个为纯洁而高贵的事业作战的士兵身上的时候。在追念之中，有比悲伤更多的东西，甚至还有一种安静而虔敬的骄傲——那亲爱的生命是献上作了「多人赎价」的。当人想到我们是在为何而战，就几乎不能不把「为基督的缘故」这一至高尊荣加给那些倒下的人。这样倒下，并不是死；毋宁说，那是发现了更宽广、更荣耀的生命。正是这一点，使今日战争中的居丧者成为一个尊贵而独特的族类；正是这一点，凭着一种内在的本能，打动他们中间如此多的人，叫他们以王者之风佩戴自己的悲伤。在他们悲伤的内里，藏着一种温柔而怅惘的骄傲。洛威尔（Lowell）已把这种情感化作了极美的诗句：

===Original===
> “I, with uncovered head,
> Salute the sacred dead,
> Who went and who return not—
> Say not so.
> ’Tis not the grapes of Canaan that repay,
> But the high faith that fails not by the way.
> Virtue treads paths that end not in the grave;
> No bar of endless night exiles the brave,
> And, to the saner mind,
> We rather seem the dead that stayed behind.”

===Chinese===
> 「我，摘帽露顶，
> 向神圣的亡者致意，
> 他们一去，不复回还——
> 切莫这样说。
> 酬报我们的，并非迦南的葡萄，
> 乃是那途中不挫的高信。
> 德行踏出的路，不终于坟墓；
> 无尽长夜的门闩，放逐不了勇者；
> 而在较清醒的心智看来，
> 我们反倒像是滞留在后的死者。」

===Original===
The other class who are teaching us a new and better way to bear burdens are the friends at home of those who are on active service. Men, with sons in the trenches, are going about our streets these days almost as if nothing were happening, making it a point of honour not to let the lurking fear in their hearts have any outward expression. Wives and mothers and sisters are filling their hands and their hearts full of duties, and putting such a brave face on life that you would never suspect they have a chamber that could tell a different tale. It is absolutely splendid. There is no other word for it. I walked a street-length with a young wife recently whose man has been ill and out of the fight for a while. She hoped that he might have been sent home, and who can blame her? but he has gone back to the trenches instead. And how bravely and quietly she spoke of it! Pride, a true and noble pride in her beloved soldier, a resolute endeavour to do her difficult bit as uncomplainingly and willingly as he—it seemed to me that I saw all that in her brave smile. And I said to myself, “Here is the cult of the washen face! And a noble cult too! Britain surely deserves to win when her women carry their crosses so!”

===Chinese===
另一类正在教我们以更新、更好的方式背负重担的人，是现役军人留在家中的亲人们。儿子们身在战壕的父亲们，这些日子照常在我们的街上来来往往，几乎像什么事都没有发生，他们把不让心中潜伏的恐惧有丝毫外露，当作荣誉攸关之事。妻子、母亲和姊妹们，双手与心头都装满了当尽的本分，对生活摆出如此勇敢的面容，你绝不会疑心她们还有一间内室，能讲出另一番光景。这实在壮美无比——再没有别的字眼可以形容。前不久，我陪一位年轻的妻子走了一程街路；她的丈夫病了，已有一阵子离开战斗。她曾盼望他会被送回家来，这又怎能怪她？可他却已重回战壕。而她说起这件事来，是何等勇敢、何等安静！自豪，是对她那亲爱的士兵的一份真切而高贵的自豪；是一种坚定的努力，要像他一样毫无怨言、甘心乐意地尽她自己那份艰难的本分——在我看来，这一切都在她那勇敢的微笑里。我对自己说：「这就是洗净脸的风尚！而且是一种高贵的风尚！英国的妇女这样背负她们的十字架，英国实在配得胜利！」

===Original===
It is easy, of course, to read the thought in their minds. Our men, they say, are splendid, why should we be doleful and despondent? They have made a new virtue of cheerfulness; let us try to learn it too. They have offered everything in a cause which it is an honour to help in any degree; let us lay beside theirs the worthy sacrifice of the washen face and a brave restraint. Such, I imagine, is the unconscious kind of reasoning which results in the resolute and cheerful bearing you may see on all sides of you every day.

And wherever it is seen, it carries its blessing with it. Others with their own private burdens and anxieties are encouraged to hold on to that hope and cheerfulness which are just the homely side of our faith in God and in the righteousness of our cause.

===Chinese===
当然，他们心里的想法不难读出。他们说：我们的人那样出色，我们又何必哭丧着脸、垂头丧气？他们已把开朗愉快造成一种新的美德，让我们也试着学会它。他们已在一项但凡出力分毫都属光荣的事业中献上了一切；让我们把洗净的脸与勇敢的克制这份配得上的祭，摆放在他们的祭旁。我想，正是这种不知不觉的推理，造就了你每天在四围都能见到的那种坚毅而欢欣的风度。

而这种风度无论在哪里出现，都带着它自己的祝福。那些自己也有私人的重担与忧虑的人受到激励，得以持守住那份盼望与乐观——那正是我们对上帝、对我们事业之正义的信心最家常的一面。

===Original===
The cult of the washen face is contagious. It spreads like a beneficent stain. And since it is entirely praiseworthy, we can but wish it to spread more and more. Those who come out from the chambers where they have kept company with sorrow or anxiety, to face life and duty with shining face and mastered feelings, are not only proving their faith in the Divine Strength, they are making a precious contribution to the moral steadfastness of the nation.

“And he washed his face and went out and refrained himself.” Good man!

===Chinese===
洗净脸的风尚是有感染性的。它像一种有益的染渍一般蔓延开来。既然它全然值得称赞，我们只能愿它越传越广。那些从曾与悲伤或忧虑相伴的内室中走出来，以光亮的脸和制伏了的情感去面对生活与本分的人，不但在证明他们对神圣能力的信心，也是在为这个国家道德上的坚定作出宝贵的贡献。

「他洗了脸出来，勉强隐忍。」好样的！

===Original===
Prayer

We bless Thee, O God, for the assurance that Thine ear is ever open to our cry, that it is never wrong to take our sorrows and our cares to Thee. But help us also, endowed with Thy strength in our secret chambers, to bear our burdens bravely in the sight of men. For Thy Name’s sake. Amen.

===Chinese===
祈祷

神啊，我们称颂你，因为你确凿地保证：你的耳朵常向我们的呼求敞开；把我们的悲伤与挂虑带到你面前，永不会错。但也求你帮助我们，既在你的内室里得着你的力量，就在人眼前也勇敢地背负我们的重担。为你名的缘故求。阿们。

## VIII The Real Martha / VIII 真正的马大

===Original===
> “But few things are needful, or one.”
> — Luke 10:42; R.V. (margin)

===Chinese===
> 「但是需要的不过几样，或是一样。」
> — Luke 10:42; R.V. (margin)

===Original===
When Jesus said, upon one occasion, that He had not where to lay His head, He was speaking the bitter and literal truth. He had really no home of His own, but was everywhere a wanderer, dependent on others for shelter and food; and though the New Testament draws a veil over all the hardships which that entailed even in the hospitable East, imagination can picture something at least of what the homelessness of Jesus must have meant.
But He had close and warm friends who made it up to Him as far as friends could, and of these were the two sisters, Martha and Mary, who with their brother, Lazarus, had a house in Bethany. This place was His haven and shelter, for “Jesus loved Martha and her sister and Lazarus.” The sisters were unlike in disposition. Mary, we can imagine, was dreamy, meditative, perhaps a little delicate and fragile, and gifted with a quick and loving sympathy. Martha was robust, practical, energetic. Her way of showing the Master that she considered it an honour to have Him for a guest was to give Him the very best that her housewifely skill could suggest. No trouble was too much for her. And it is very possible that one of the charms which this home had for Jesus—one of the qualities which made it a real place of rest—was its well-ordered arrangements, the quiet, efficient, capable way in which things were done. And whose was the credit for that? Martha’s.

===Chinese===
耶稣有一次说，他没有枕头的地方；这话说的正是令人心酸的字面事实。他实在没有自己的家，处处漂流，容身与饮食都仰赖他人；新约圣经虽然用一层纱遮起了这一切艰辛——纵然在好客的东方，无家可归也必带来种种困苦——我们至少还能凭想象约略描出，耶稣的居无定所必定意味着什么。

然而他有亲密温暖的朋友，凡朋友所能补偿的，他们都尽力补偿了他；其中就有那两姊妹——马大（Martha）和马利亚（Mary）——她们与兄弟拉撒路（Lazarus）在伯大尼（Bethany）有一所房子。那地方是他的避风港、他的庇护所，因为「耶稣素来爱马大和她妹子并拉撒路」。两姊妹性情迥异。可以想见，马利亚耽于梦想、喜好沉思，或许还有几分娇弱，却生就一种敏捷而满有爱心的同情。马大则健壮、务实、精力充沛。她要向主表明，有他作客是何等的尊荣；她的方式，就是把她那持家的巧艺所能想出的最好的东西摆在他面前。再多的劳苦，她也不以为烦。这所房子之所以令耶稣喜爱——之所以使它成为一个真正可以安息的地方——很可能正在于它的井然有序，在于凡事都办得安静、利落、能干。这份功劳属于谁呢？属于马大。

===Original===
What would that household have been like without Martha? And what would any home that is fortunate enough to have a Martha in it, be like without her? The truth is our debt to the Marthas is one which we have never fully acknowledged. You would imagine, hearing the way in which her name is sometimes used, that it has an apologetic character, as if the making of a home comfortable and homelike were a gift to be lightly esteemed in comparison, for example, with the ability to write verse! It is foolish to play Mary off against her sister in this way. Martha did what she could do best, and showed her love for Christ in that fashion, and you may be quite sure that He understood. Mary served Him in her way, by giving Him what He needed more at times than food—a heart to listen to His message, and a sympathy which made the telling of it meat and drink to Him. Each sister was the complement of the other.
But we wrong Martha, of course, in thinking of her as always in the kitchen. Certainly when there was a meal to be prepared you would find her there, and well that was for the household and the servants. But nobody is always eating or thinking about eating; and often of an evening, doubtless, when the labours of the day were over, Martha would join her sister at the feet of the Master whom she loved as much as Mary did.

===Chinese===
倘若没有马大，那个家会成为什么样子？任何一个有幸有一位马大的家，若没有她，又会是什么样子？说实话，我们对众「马大」所负的亏欠，我们从未完全承认过。听到人们有时提起这名字的那副口气，你会以为它带着几分需要致歉的意味，仿佛把一个家操持得舒适温馨、富有家的情味，比起写诗的才干之类来，竟是一种不足挂齿的恩赐！这样抬出马利亚来与她的姊妹对垒，是愚拙的。马大做了自己最擅长的事，并以这种方式表明她对基督的爱，你尽可放心，他全然领会。马利亚也以她的方式服事他，把他时而比饮食更需要的东西给了他——一颗聆听他信息的心，一种同情，使传讲这信息于他就如同饮食。两姊妹彼此相辅相成。

当然，我们若以为马大总是守在厨房里，那就是错待了她。的确，每逢要备饭，你总会在那里找到她，这对一家上下和仆人们都是好事。但没有人总是在吃饭，或总想着吃饭；可以确信，许多个傍晚，当一天的劳碌做完，马大也会来到她所爱的主的脚前，与她的姊妹同坐——她爱主，与马利亚同样深切。

===Original===
The incident which has given rise to the popular misconception of Martha’s character occurred during a visit which Jesus paid in the days before Lazarus fell sick. Something went wrong in Martha’s department that day. Perhaps it was a mistake of a servant that irritated the usually self-controlled Martha, or maybe some oversight of her own. At anyrate, it set up a condition of worry which straightway began to add to itself, as its habit is, seven other devils. And as Martha went out and in the dining chamber getting things ready, the sight of Mary sitting there at the Master’s feet doing nothing, struck her, perhaps for the first time, as rather out of place. Things began to go further wrong. Just when Martha wanted to do special honour to Jesus, the ordinarily smooth-running wheels of that home began to creak and grind. Each time she entered the room where Christ and Mary were, Martha’s steps grew brisker and more emphatic; and then the last straw was laid on, and the outburst came! Martha asked Jesus if He really did not care that Mary was leaving her to do everything. Bid her come and help me, she said.

===Chinese===
引起世人普遍误解马大性情的那桩事，发生在拉撒路患病之前、耶稣来访的那些日子。那一天，马大主管的一摊子出了岔子。也许是哪个仆人的过失惹恼了平日极有自制的马大，也许是她自己有什么疏忽。无论如何，这造成了一种焦烦的状态，而这状态随即照它素常的习性，开始给自己添上另外七个鬼。马大在餐室里出出进进地张罗备办，一眼看见马利亚坐在主的脚前什么也不做——她也许破天荒头一遭觉得，这景象实在不像话。事情开始越弄越糟。恰在马大想要向耶稣致特别的敬意时，这个家平日转动顺滑的轮子开始吱嘎作响、磕磕碰碰。每回她走进基督与马利亚所在的那间屋子，马大的脚步便越来越急、越来越重；终于，最后一根稻草压了下来，爆发来了！马大问耶稣：马利亚这样撇下她一个人包揽一切，他当真全不介意吗。「叫她来帮我吧，」她说。

===Original===
Of course, Jesus knew that it was for His sake that Martha was giving herself all this trouble. He saw, as even we can see, that this kindhearted, worried woman was speaking crossly, as the very best will do at times, because she was tired and a bit overdriven. And with a perfect and gentle chivalry and tact He made His reply. As the Authorised Version puts it, it jars on one, somehow. But King James’ translators have misread their text. What Jesus said was: “Martha, Martha, you are unduly anxious and troubled. Only a few things are necessary, or even one. Mary has chosen a good part, and I cannot allow you to take it from her.”

===Chinese===
当然，耶稣知道马大这样劳心费力，正是为了他的缘故。他看得分明——连我们也看得出来——这位心地良善、满腹焦忧的妇人出言呛人，正如最好的人有时也难免的那样，只因她累了，又有些被赶得过了头。于是，他以全然温柔的骑士风度与体贴机敏，作出回答。照《钦定本》的措辞，这话不知怎的总有些刺耳。但钦定本的译者误读了他们的底本。耶稣所说的乃是：「马大，马大，你忧虑得过了分，心里烦扰。需要的不过几样，甚或只有一样。马利亚已经拣选了美好的福分，我不能容你把它从她夺去。」

===Original===
Martha, remember, was making a feast worthy of the Master, and Jesus, looking upon the various dishes being got ready, said, in effect, I do not really need so many as that. One would do quite well. And I must not let you think that Mary is doing nothing. She, too, is ministering to me by her sympathy and her willing ear, and you must not take away the good part she has chosen.

===Chinese===
要记得，马大正在预备一席配得上主的筵席；耶稣望着各样正在备办的菜肴，意思等于说：其实我并不需要这么多，有一道就尽够了。我也不可任你们以为马利亚什么都没做。她也正以她的同情、她乐意的耳朵服事我，你不可把她所拣选的美好福分夺去。

===Original===
Jesus was not speaking about the personal salvation of either Mary or her sister. He was only dealing gently with a good and true friend of His who had not served Him as she had wished to do. When He spoke of what was needful, He meant needful for Himself, the Guest whom both the sisters were seeking to honour.
He made no comparison between Martha’s service and Mary’s. He did not say, as we have read it so often, that Mary had chosen the better part. He said, in her defence, that Mary’s was also a good part. He is not blaming Martha, but only expostulating with her in the gentlest fashion, and defending Mary from the charge which Martha in her heat had made against her, the charge of being useless, and doing nothing to help to entertain the Master. Jesus said, She is helping to entertain Me in her own way, and, He added, it is a good way.

===Chinese===
耶稣说的并不是马利亚或她姊妹的个人得救。他只是在温柔地对待一位善良忠实的朋友——她未能照自己的心愿服事他。当他说到「需要」时，他指的是他自己的需要——是那位两姊妹都想要尊崇的宾客的需要。

他没有在马大的服事与马利亚的服事之间作什么比较。他没有像我们惯常读到的那样，说马利亚拣选了那「更好」的福分。他只是为她辩护，说她所拣选的也是美好的福分。他不是在责备马大，只是以最温和的方式婉言相劝，并且为她辩白马大在气头上加给她的指控——指控她一无用处，对款待主人分毫无助。耶稣说，她正以她自己的方式帮助款待我；他又加上一句：那是一个好的方式。

===Original===
When Jesus having said that only a few things were necessary, dropped His voice, as we may imagine, and added “or indeed one,” He may have meant more than He seemed to say. For there was one thing that was more than meat to our Lord, and that was to find a soul with heart and sympathy open to His message. And it may be that He felt, as He said the words, that Mary’s ministry met a need of His deeper than that for which Martha was catering. At anyrate, the oldest and best versions of this Gospel give Christ’s words as we have rendered them, and they stand here, not to be used as a peg on which to hang doctrines, but rather as a proof of the gentle courtesy of our Lord, of His insight into character and motive, and of His gracious recognition of the worth of any and every kind of service that has love at its heart.

===Chinese===
耶稣说完「需要的不过几样」，我们可以想见，他放低了声音，又加上一句「甚或只有一样」——这时候，他所意味的，或许比表面听来更多。因为我主心里有一件事比食物更要紧，那就是寻见一个心灵敞开、富有同情、肯领受他信息的灵魂。也许正当他说这话时，他感觉到马利亚的服事满足了他的一个需要，比马大所张罗的更深。无论如何，这卷福音书最古老、最可靠的版本，都照我们所译的那样记下基督的话；这些话立在这里，不是给人拿来当悬挂教义的钉子，而是要证明我主的温柔谦礼，证明他对性情与动机的洞见，证明他满有恩慈地承认：一切以爱为核心的服事，无论何种何样，都有其价值。

===Original===
Martha went back to her kitchen, and Mary remained where she was. Mary was not asked to go and help. Martha would have protested if she had come. Martha was not called upon to go and sit beside Mary. Each continued the service for which she was best fitted. But each, I think, had learned something that day. And you and I must not leave this page of our New Testament till we have learned it too—that we serve best when we do gladly that for which we are best qualified; that it belongs to our Christian service to recognise in all loyalty that, though others find different ways of expressing it, theirs is a good part; and that we must never either belittle it or seek to take it from them.

===Chinese===
马大回到她的厨房，马利亚仍坐在原处。没有人叫马利亚去帮忙；她若真去了，马大反倒要抗议。也没有人叫马大去坐到马利亚身旁。各人继续做她最适合的服事。但我想，那一天，两人各有所学。你我若不也学会这一课，就不可翻过新约的这一页——那就是：当我们甘心去做自己最适合做的事，我们的服事就做得最好；行基督徒的服事，就当尽忠承认：别人虽以不同的方式表达爱心，他们所有的也是美好的福分；我们万不可轻看它，也不可设法把它从他们手中夺去。

===Original===
Prayer
O Lord our God, Who by many diverse ways dost bring us near to Thee, and in differing modes and stations dost appoint our service, help us gladly and gratefully to do the things we can do, neither envying those whose opportunities are greater, nor forbidding those who follow not us. For Thy Name’s sake. Amen.

===Chinese===
祈祷
主我们的神啊，你用多种不同的途径领我们亲近你，又以各异的方式与职分指派我们的服事；求你帮助我们欢然感激地去做我们所能做的事，既不嫉妒那些机会更大的人，也不禁止那些不跟从我们的人。为你名的缘故求。阿们。

## IX Our Unearned Increment / IX 不劳而获的增益

===Original===
> “He giveth (to) His beloved (in their) sleep.”
> — Psalm 127:2

===Chinese===
> 「他必赐给他所亲爱的人（在他们睡时）。」
> — Psalm 127:2

===Original===
“It is vain for you,” says the writer of the 127th Psalm, “to rise early and sit up late and eat the bread of sorrow, for so He giveth to His beloved (in their) sleep.” That is the true reading, and I want you to think about it. “God giveth to His beloved while they sleep.” Over and above what you have yourself achieved, you *get* something you have never worked for. And you get that, as it were, in your sleep. This is a beautiful thought, and there are three people to whom I want to offer it as God’s comfort.

The first is the worried man. It is indeed directly against worry that this psalmist sets forth his reminder. It is not that he minimises the need for hard work and watchful care. But he tells the man who is feverishly burning his candle at both ends, and consuming himself in a frenzy of tense anxiety, to leave something for God to do. It is as if he said, “Why so hot, little man, why so fiercely clutching all the ropes? Remember that God is working too as well as you, working in your interest and in love for you. When you have done your best therefore, go to your bed and sleep with a quiet mind, for God giveth to His beloved even so.”

===Chinese===
「你们清晨早起，夜晚安歇，吃忧伤的饼，本是枉然，因为他是这样赐给他所爱的人（在他们睡时）。」诗篇第一百二十七篇的作者如此说。那才是正确的读法，我要你们思想这话。「上帝在他所爱的人睡时赐给他们。」在你自己成就的一切之外，你还*得到*一些你从未为之劳作的东西；而你得到它，就仿佛是在睡梦之中。这是一个美丽的思想，我要把它作为上帝的安慰，献给三种人。

第一种是忧虑的人。这位诗篇作者提出他的提醒，的确正是直接针对忧虑而发。他并非轻看辛勤劳作与警醒看守的需要。但他对那个狂热地把蜡烛两头点燃、在紧张焦虑的狂乱中耗尽自己的人说：要留一点事给上帝去做。他仿佛在说：「何必这样火热，小小的人儿，何必这样紧紧攥住所有的绳索？要记住，上帝也和你一样在作工，为你的益处、怀着对你的爱而作工。所以，你尽了本分之后，就安然上床，静心去睡吧，因为上帝正是这样赐给他所爱的人。」

===Original===
One can imagine how a word like that would relax the tension and lead some persuadable Hebrew who heard it to say, “Ah, well, I worry far too much. After all, I am not Providence.
