I am always getting a great many things I have not wrought for. I shall worry less about securing the good things I desire for me and mine, and trust more to God to give them as He sees fit.” If all of us who needed this reminder just had the sense to come to the same conclusion!

I have seen a man compass his family with so many careful regulations and observances that the criticism of a candid friend seemed entirely just. “You would think,” he said, “to see so-and-so shepherding his family, that there was no other providence than his own.” You can’t be with your best beloved all the while. And you ought to know that God too is watching even while you sleep.

===Chinese===
可以想见，这样一句话会怎样松开那绷紧的弦，叫听见它的某个肯受劝的希伯来人说出这样的话：「唉，是啊，我忧虑得太多了。毕竟，我并不是上天的眷护。我素来总是白得许多我并未为之劳作的东西。我要少为我和我家的人得着我所渴望的美物而忧虑，多信靠上帝，让他照他所看为合宜的赐下。」但愿我们这些需要这提醒的人，都有这样的见识，能得出同样的结论！

我见过一个人，用许许多多细致的规条与守则把他的家人团团围住，以致某位坦率朋友的一句批评显得完全公允。他说：「你若看见某某人这样牧着他的家人，准会以为除了他自己的眷护，再没有别的眷护了。」你不可能时时刻刻都守在你最亲的人身边。你也当知道，甚至在你睡觉的时候，上帝也在看顾。

===Original===
If there be some plan on which you have set your heart, and you are overanxious about it, quote this text to yourself. Do your best, of course, but, having done so, leave the outcome with God. About a great many of the things over which we worry ourselves needlessly, I believe God’s word to us is:—Leave these things to Me. You can’t work for them. And anxiety won’t bring them. But you will get them, as you need them, just as if they came to you in your sleep.

Said one hermit to another in the Egyptian desert, as he looked at a flourishing olive tree near his cave, “How came that goodly tree there, brother? For I too planted an olive, and when I thought it wanted water, I asked God to give it rain and the rain came, and when I thought it wanted sun I asked God and the sun shone, and when I deemed it needed strengthening, I prayed and the frost came—God gave me all I demanded for my tree, as I saw fit, and yet it died.” “And I, brother,” replied the other hermit, “I left my tree in God’s hands, for He knew what it wanted better than I, and behold what a goodly tree it has become.”

===Chinese===
倘若你把心放在某个计划上，又为它过分忧虑，就把这节经文念给自己听。当然要尽你最大的努力，但尽了之后，就把结局交托给上帝。对于许多我们无谓地为之自寻烦恼的事，我相信上帝对我们说的话乃是：「把这些事留给我。」你无法为它们劳作，忧虑也不能把它们带来。但你必得着它们，在你需要的时候得着，仿佛它们是在你睡梦中临到你一样。

在埃及的旷野，一个隐士望着另一位隐士洞穴旁一棵繁茂的橄榄树，对他说：「弟兄，那棵美好的树是怎么长在那里的？我也栽过一棵橄榄：我以为它缺水，就求上帝赐雨，雨就来了；我以为它需要阳光，就求告上帝，太阳就照耀；我以为它需要强健，就献上祈祷，来的却是霜冻——上帝照我自己的意思，把我为我的树所求的一切都给了我，它却死了。」「弟兄，我呢，」另一位隐士回答说，「我把我的树交在上帝手里，因为他比我自己更知道它需要什么。你看，它如今长成了多好的一棵树。」

===Original===
The second man to whom I would offer the comfort of this word of God is the man who is disappointed. Things have gone wrong with him. The plan on which he spent so much of his time and energy has miscarried, and a very different result has emerged from what he counted on. His way, as he saw it, is blocked, and he has had to turn aside.

Now, there are not many things one can say usefully to a disappointed man. And it is cruel kindness to try to heal his hurt lightly. Nevertheless, to him also the psalmist’s message applies, and what he needs to remember, that he may pick up heart and go on again, is that God giveth to His beloved while they sleep.

===Chinese===
第二个我愿把上帝这话的安慰献给他的，是那失望的人。他的事不顺遂。他倾注了那么多时间与精力的计划中途流产，浮现出来的结果与他所指望的大相径庭。在他看来，他的路被堵住了，他不得不转向别处。

说实话，对一个失望的人，能说的有用的话并不多。想轻轻巧巧地医治他的伤痛，那是一种残忍的仁慈。然而诗篇作者的信息对他同样适用；他若要重新鼓起勇气、再往前走，所要记住的正是这个：上帝在他所爱的人睡时赐给他们。

===Original===
We have all had disappointments, sore enough at the time, which after-experience proved to have been blessings in disguise. Many a man can point to a signal failure as the beginning of a true success or usefulness or happiness. We did not feel as if we were being enriched when our plan fell through, and we were bitter and rebellious enough at the time, it may be, but it is quite clear to us now that God was at that very time giving to us with both His hands.

No one, of course, can see that about any more than a few of his disappointments. It would be false to experience to speak as if we could. But what is manifestly true about one or two may conceivably hold with regard to them all, if we knew more, or could see better. And the Christian Gospel calls us to believe and trust that that is so. There is another Hand than ours shaping our life, a wiser Hand. Better things are being done for us than we can see in the meantime. And the man whose hopes and plans have turned out amiss, but whose trust is still in God, is invited by our psalmist to reason with himself thus:—“I am like a man asleep, and I do not rightly understand at present, but I will trust that it is not for nothing that misfortune has come, and when I wake I shall hope to see that God has been giving to me in love and mercy when I was not aware of it at all.”

===Chinese===
我们都经历过失望，当时痛切难当，事后的经历却证明它们是化了装的祝福。多少人能指着某一次惨重的失败说，那正是真正的成功、真正的用处或真正的幸福的开端。当我们的计划落空时，我们并不觉得自己正在变得丰富；那时我们或许满心苦毒、悖逆不服，但如今我们看得分明：上帝正是在那时伸出双手赐给我们。

当然，一个人能在自己的失望中看出这一点的，顶多不过寥寥几件。若说得仿佛我们事事都能看出，那就不合经历了。但在一两件事上显然为真的，若我们多得些见识，或能看得更清楚，或许在一切事上都同样成立。而基督教的福音正是呼召我们相信并信靠事情确是如此。塑造我们人生的，是我们自己的手之外的另一只手，一只更有智慧的手。有比我们目前所能看见的更美的事，正在为我们成就。那盼望与计划尽皆落空、信靠却仍在上帝里头的人，我们的诗篇作者邀请他这样对自己剖白：「我像一个睡着的人，眼下不能明白其中原委；但我要相信，不幸的临到决非徒然，等我醒来，我指望看见：上帝在我全然不觉的时候，一直在爱与怜悯中赐给我。」

===Original===
The third man whom this text will help and comfort is the worker, the man or woman who is trying to do something for Christ’s sake. The Christian worker needs to be told that what he is trying to do is not nearly all that he is doing. What he is, is speaking as loudly as what he does or says. There is an aroma and fragrance about the life of the consecrated Christlike man or woman which sweetens and sanctifies other lives beyond what he or she can ever know. Some of the best sermons in the world have been preached by people who least suspected what they were doing. The invalid in the home does not know how real religion becomes to all who watch her patience and unselfishness. And among the busy and vigorous we often catch hints and reflections, that they never suspect, of what Christlikeness means. The man who has surrendered his life to God, indeed, is a channel of blessing to others beyond all he ever dreams of. He must not be disheartened when he realises how little he is doing, for the truth is he is doing far, far more than he knows. Wherefore, my brother, be of good cheer, and render your service to Christ with a quiet heart. Lay your course, and work your ship, and hoist your sail and trust. And the gifts of God will enrich you, and the winds of heaven will bring you on your way, even while you sleep.

===Chinese===
这节经文要帮助、要安慰的第三种人，是做工的人——那为基督的缘故努力要做点什么的人，无论是男是女。基督徒的工人需要有人告诉他：他着手要做的事，远不是他正在做的全部。他之所「是」，与他之所做所言，同样响亮地说话。那奉献给基督、像基督的男子或女子，其生命自有一种馨香与芬芳，使别的生命变得甘甜、得以成圣，远超他或她所能知道。世上一些最好的讲章，正是由最想不到自己在讲道的人宣讲出来的。家中的病人不知道，对一切看见她的忍耐与无私的人而言，信仰显得何等真实。而在忙碌而精力充沛的人中间，我们也常常捕捉到他们自己绝无察觉的蛛丝马迹与映影，照出像基督意味着什么。那把生命交托给上帝的人，实在是使别人蒙福的管道，其宽广远超他一切所梦想的。当他觉察自己做得何等少时，决不可灰心，因为实情是：他所做的，远远、远远多于他所知道的。所以，我的弟兄，你当欢然振作，以安静的心服事基督。定准你的航向，驾好你的船，扬起帆来，并且信靠。上帝的恩赐必使你丰富，诸天的风必送你前行——甚至在你睡觉的时候。

===Original===
Prayer
We give Thee thanks, O God, for all Thy bounties, undeserved and unearned; for the increase Thou dost send us while the stars are shining; for Thy gracious thirty-fold and sixty-fold beyond what we have sown. Every morning Thou leavest gifts upon our doorstep and dost depart unthanked. But this day we remember, and we bow our heads to render unto Thee our humble and our hearty thanks for all that Thou hast given us while we slept. Amen.

===Chinese===
祈祷
我们的神啊，我们感谢你一切的厚赐——那非我们所配、非我们所赚的；感谢你趁着群星闪耀时赐给我们的出产；感谢你恩典的三十倍、六十倍，远超我们所撒的种。每天清晨，你都把礼物留在我们门口，又悄然离去，未受感谢。但就在今日，我们记念了；我们低头俯首，为着你趁我们睡觉时赐给我们的一切，把你当得的、谦卑而诚挚的感谢献给你。阿们。

## X Smoking Wicks / X 将残的灯火

===Original===
> “The smoking flax he shall not quench.”
> — Isaiah 42:3

===Chinese===
> 「将残的灯火，他不吹灭。」
> — Isaiah 42:3

===Original===
We read the 42nd chapter of Isaiah now as if it were a part of the Christian Evangel. And that is right. For whoever the Servant may have been, of whom Isaiah was thinking, it is Christ and only Christ who completely fulfils this prophecy. This is a true description of His spirit and His method. “The dimly-burning wick he shall not quench.”

The figure is easily understood. Here is a piece of flax floating in oil, and burning so faintly that it seems a mere charred end from which the smoke coils thinly upwards. Someone comes and snuffs it out, because it smells. That is the way of the world’s reformers, as Isaiah saw it, and we can see it still. By and by they will trim the wick and light it with fire of their own, but first they will quench the spark. But there is One to come, said Isaiah, shooting his arrow of prophecy in the air, who will go otherwise about it. He will not despise the spark because it is so feeble. He will tend it and foster it, and make the evil-smelling bundle of flax into a clear, shining light. And the saying has found its mark in Jesus Christ.

===Chinese===
今天我们读以赛亚书第 42 章，仿佛读的是基督福音的一部分。这样读是对的。因为无论以赛亚心里所想的那位仆人究竟是谁，唯有基督，唯独基督，完完全全应验了这一预言。这段话真实地描写了祂的性情与祂的方法。「将残的灯火，他不吹灭。」

这个比喻不难明白。这里有一线灯芯浮在油上，燃得那样微弱，看上去只是一段烧焦的残端，一缕细烟袅袅上腾。有人走来，嫌它有气味，便把它掐灭。以赛亚所看见的世上改革者的行径正是如此，我们今天也还看得见。过些时候，他们自然也要修剪灯芯、用自己的火把它点亮，但第一件事总是先把那点火星掐灭。然而以赛亚把预言的箭射向空中，说：有一位要来，要以别样的方式成就这事。他不因那火星微弱便轻看它；他要守护它、培育它，把那股气味难闻的灯芯束变成一盏皎洁明亮的灯。而这话果然射中了鹄的——应验在耶稣基督身上。

===Original===
When a woman that was a sinner made her way into the house where He sat at meat, and wept at His feet, He amazed all those present by the extraordinary gentleness of His dealing with her. He did not refer to the evil in her life. He did not, as other good men would have done, first cast her down, that He might afterwards lift her up. He simply took the beautiful impulse after good which she brought Him out of a life besmirched and tawdry, held it in His hands—a mere spark of virtue—and breathing on it, blessed it, and behold it was a flame, burning up the evil in her life, a lamp lighting her path along a new and hopeful way. That was Christ. He does not, He will not quench the dimly-burning wick.

===Chinese===
当那有罪的女人进到祂坐席的屋里，在祂脚前哭泣时，祂待她的那种异乎寻常的温柔，令在场的众人无不惊讶。祂没有提起她生活里的恶。祂也不像别的善人所惯于做的，先把她压下去，好随后再把她扶起来。祂只是把她从那被玷污、被糟践的生活里带给祂的那一缕向善的美意接过来，捧在祂的手中——那不过一星德性的火花——向它吹气，为它祝福；看哪，它竟成了一团火焰，把她生命里的恶烧尽，成了一盏灯，照亮她踏上崭新而满有盼望的路。那就是基督。祂不吹灭，祂也绝不肯吹灭那将残的灯火。

===Original===
Now—and this is our point—if those who profess and call themselves Christians are to have the spirit in them that was also in Christ Jesus, must not this be their mark too? Does not this prescribe their attitude to life, that many-coloured, strangely-mixed compound of good and evil? Good in any form, however feeble, however mixed, as in this world it inevitably is, with what is evil, should find in those who call themselves by Christ’s name, its truest supporters, sympathisers, friends.

To the eye and heart in sympathy with it, beauty often peeps out in strange places.

===Chinese===
如今——这正是我要着重的要点——凡认信而自称为基督徒的人，若要有那在基督耶稣里的心也存在他们里面，这不也必须是他们的标记么？生活乃是一种五光十色、善恶奇异掺杂的混合物，难道这不正为他们规定了对它的态度么？凡善，无论以何种形式出现，无论怎样微弱，无论像它在这世上不可避免的那样与恶相混，都应当在奉基督之名自称的人身上，找到它最忠实的扶持者、同情者与朋友。

在与它心弦相和的眼与心看来，美常常在奇异的地方探出头来。

===Original===
> “The poem hangs on the berry bush,
> When comes the poet’s eye,
> And the whole street is a masquerade
> When Shakespeare passes by.”

===Chinese===
> 诗篇悬在浆果丛的枝头，
> 只待诗人的眼睛来到；
> 莎士比亚一路走过，
> 整条街便成了一场假面舞会。

===Original===
So the mark of the Christlike heart is just that it discerns, and, discerning, loves the feeblest tokens of some inward grace that redeems a life from evil. Do not be afraid that by welcoming the scant good, you may be held to approve of the greater evil. That is a risk that God Himself rejoices to take. Did not Christ risk that, when He accepted that poor woman’s worship? Did He not risk it when He held out His hands to a man like Zaccheus? Does He not risk it always when He declares, “Him that cometh unto Me I will in no wise cast out?” And shall we refuse because the risk is too great?

Life presents us with many anomalies that refuse to square with our theories. You find men exhibiting qualities of character, which any Christian might be proud to emulate, outside of the Church altogether. And you cannot simply label these—“glittering vices,” and pass on. God is not two but One, and goodness is His token wherever it be found. “The World,” says John Owen, “cannot yet afford to do without the good acts even of its bad men.” And the truth for us to learn is that the grace of God is not bound by our standards or limits. Make the circle as wide as you like, you will still discover fruits of the Spirit outside, where by all our canons they were never to be expected.

===Chinese===
所以，像基督之心的标记，恰恰在于：它辨识那救赎一个生命脱离罪恶的内在恩典，哪怕其征兆再微弱，并且一经辨识，便加以珍爱。不要害怕：你欢迎这一点微薄的善，或许会被人认为是在赞许那更大的恶。这是一桩连上帝自己也乐于去冒的风险。基督接纳那可怜妇人的敬拜时，岂不曾冒这险？祂向撒该那样的人伸出双手时，岂不曾冒这险？祂宣告「到我这里来的，我总不丢弃他」时，岂不无时无刻不在冒这险？难道我们要因为风险太大，就加以拒绝么？

人生向我们呈现许多不肯与我们的理论相合的反常现象。你会发现有人全然在教会之外，却展现着任何基督徒都会以能效法为荣的品格。而你总不能给这些品格贴上「闪光的恶德」的标签，便掉头而去。上帝不是有二，乃是一；善无论在何处寻见，都是祂的印记。「这个世界，」约翰·欧文（John Owen）说，「甚至还离不开它那些恶人所行的善事。」我们要领受的真理乃是：上帝的恩典，不受我们的标准与界限的捆束。随你把圈子画得多宽，你仍会在圈外发现圣灵的果子，而照我们一切的定规，那里原是不该指望有它们的。

===Original===
> “And every virtue we possess,
> And every victory won,
> And every thought of holiness
> Are His alone.”

===Chinese===
> 我们怀有的每一种美德，
> 每一场争战得来的胜利，
> 每一个圣洁的念头，
> 都单单属于祂。

===Original===
It is for something more than tolerance I am pleading. For that may be a weak and a wrong thing, if it spring not from belief in the good. What our calling demands is something more, the rejoicing, hopeful recognition of the good deed or purpose anywhere, and the offer of a sympathy and a faith in which it can grow. That gift of yours may actually be the decisive factor in a life balancing perilously betwixt good and evil. Three times, the other evening, I tried to light my study fire, and each time it went out. The paper burned, but the sticks apparently would not light. At last in despair I flung in a burning match and went away—and when I returned I found a cheerful blaze: the brief glimmer of that last match had been the determining factor. You will smile perhaps at the illustration, but you will remember, all the better, that where the flax is even smouldering, there the angels are still fighting for a soul. And you will, maybe, remember also that even your warm sympathy may turn the scale, and fan the flicker to a flame.

===Chinese===
我所恳求的，还不止于宽容。因为宽容若不是出于对善的信念，或许倒是一件软弱而错误的事。我们的呼召所要求的更多：无论在何处遇见善行或善念，都欢然存着盼望加以承认，并献上一份同情、一份信心，使善得以在其中生长。你所献上的那一份，也许恰恰就是一个在善与恶之间危殆摆荡的生命里那决定性的因素。前几天晚上，我想点着书房的炉火，试了三回，三回都熄了。纸烧着了，柴却分明不肯燃。末了我灰心绝望，把一根燃着的火柴丢进去便走开了——回来时却见满炉欢腾的火焰：最后那根火柴短促的一闪，竟是决定成败的因素。这个比方或许要引你一笑，但你反而会因此记得更牢：哪里有一线灯芯尚在阴燃，哪里就仍有天使为一个灵魂争战。你或许还会记得，连你那一份热切的同情，也可能扭转天平，把那点闪烁的微光扇成一道火焰。

===Original===
Prayer
O Lord our God, God and Father of our Lord Jesus Christ, we pray that the mind that was in Him may more and more be found in us. Help us to offer to what is good anywhere a sympathy in which it may grow and increase. Grant us a helpful faith in the struggling good in every man, even as Thou, our Father, dost call us sons while as yet we are but prodigals, afar off. For Jesus’ sake. Amen.

===Chinese===
祈祷
主我们的神，我们主耶稣基督的父神啊，我们祈求祢，使那在祂里面的心，也愈发可以在我们里面寻见。求祢帮助我们，向各处的善献上一份可以叫它生长、加增的同情。求祢赐我们一份有益的信心，信靠各人里面那挣扎着的善，正如祢——我们的父——呼召我们作儿子，而我们此时还不过是远方流荡的浪子。奉耶稣的名求。阿们。

## XI Culpable Goodness / XI 可责的善行

===Original===
> “Let not then your good be evil spoken of.”
> — Romans 14:16

===Chinese===
> 「不可叫你的善被人毁谤。」
> — Romans 14:16

===Original===
In his letter to the Christians at Rome, the Apostle Paul counsels them not to let their “good be evil spoken of.” And at first we ask ourselves if this is a possible thing. Can you have good that is evil spoken of? Since this is a matter that ought to concern us all, I want to suggest one or two ways in which this very result may be brought about, that those of us who are trying to follow an ideal of goodness may be on our guard.

First, we can very readily have what is good in us evil spoken of because of our *censoriousness*. When men come upon some fruit that grows upon a goodly-looking tree, or one at least that has a trustworthy label attached to it, and find it sour or bitter to the taste, they are apt to be particularly resentful. And it is with precisely such indignation that they observe men and women who profess themselves followers of Christ exhibiting a censorious and critical spirit. Where ought you to find the broadest charity, the kindliest judgment, the most Christlike forbearance and restraint? Among Christians, of course. And yet—alas! alas!

===Chinese===
使徒保罗在写给罗马基督徒的书信中，劝他们不可叫自己的「善被人毁谤」。起初我们不免自问：这竟会是可能的事么？善还可能遭人毁谤么？既然这件事与我们人人都有关，我想指出一两条恰恰会招致这种结果的途径，好叫我们这些努力追求善之理想的人有所儆醒。

首先，我们里面的善可以轻易地因我们的*好论断*（censoriousness）而遭人毁谤。人从一棵看上去佳美的树上——至少是一棵挂着可靠标签的树上——摘下果子来，一尝却酸苦难当，便往往格外愤懑。而当人看见自称跟从基督的男男女女显出一种好论断、好批评的灵时，心里所怀的正是这种愤懑。最宽广的爱心、最仁厚的评断、最像基督的容忍与节制，应当到哪里去寻见？自然是在基督徒中间。然而——唉！唉！

===Original===
Just keep your ears open with this end in view for a week, and you will be surprised at the appallingly hard judgments that come tripping daintily from the lips of some of those you know best. And if that line of investigation be not very handy, just watch yourself for the same time, and you will learn what a rare thing Christian charity is.

We talk a lot about it, but in real life we “forbid” men very readily “because they follow not us,” we belittle things which we do not understand, we speak rashly about people whom we do not know, and we are ready, without the least consideration, with our label for the movement or the man, who happens to be brought to our notice.

Ah, if we could only see how far astray we often are, what a libel our label is, and how unChristlike many of our speeches appear! We don’t know enough of the inner life of any man to entitle us to pass judgment upon him. A critical spirit never commends its possessor to the affection or the goodwill of men. Besides, it blinds him to much that is really beautiful, and cuts him off from many sources of happiness. You will see evil in almost anything if you look for it, but that is not a gift that makes either for helpfulness or popular esteem. “I do not call that by the name of religion,” says Robert Louis Stevenson, “which fills a man with bile,” and, on the whole, the ordinary man is of the same mind with him.

===Chinese===
带着这个目的留神听上一个星期，你会吃惊地发现，一些你最熟悉的人的唇边，竟会轻盈地溜出何等骇人的苛刻论断。倘若这条考察的路子你不便去走，那就同样用上一个星期省察你自己，你便会明白基督徒的爱心是何等稀罕的东西。

关于爱心，我们谈得很多；可在实际生活里，我们动辄「禁止」人，「因为他们不与我们一同跟从」；我们贬低自己不明白的事物，对不认识的人轻率妄论，又对一个偶然进入我们视线的运动或人物，不假思索地便备好了我们的标签。

啊，但愿我们能看见：我们何等常常走偏，我们贴的标签是何等样的诽谤，我们许多的言谈又显得何等不像基督！对任何一个人的内在生命，我们的所知都不足以使我们配去论断他。批评的灵从不叫它的主人赢得人的爱戴与善意。况且，它使人对许多真正美的事物视而不见，又切断了他幸福的许多泉源。只要存心去找，你几乎能在任何事物里看出恶来；但这算不得恩赐，既无补于助人，也无补于得人的敬重。罗伯特·路易斯·史蒂文森（Robert Louis Stevenson）说：「那使人满腹苦毒的东西，我不称之为宗教。」大体而论，寻常人与他抱有同样的看法。

===Original===
> “Judge not; the workings of his brain
> And of his heart thou canst not see.
> What looks, to thy dim eyes, a stain,
> In God’s pure light may only be
> A scar brought from some well-won field,
> Where thou wouldst only faint and yield.”

===Chinese===
> 「不要论断；他头脑的活动，
> 他内心的活动，你无从看见。
> 在你昏花的眼中像是一块污渍的，
> 在神纯洁的光里，也许不过是
> 一道从某处光荣战场上带回来的伤疤——
> 那样的地方，换作是你，只会昏厥屈服。」

===Original===
Sometimes one must, in the interests of true religion, pass judgment, but these times are not so frequent as we suppose. And if there are occasions more than others when the disciple needs an overflowing measure of Christ’s spirit, it is when it is his clear duty to diagnose, disapprove, and condemn.

Secondly, we may have our good evil spoken of by our *extremeness*. I should be very chary of saying that there is such a thing as being righteous overmuch, but for two reasons. The first is that there is an injunction in Scripture against it. And the second is that I have met people, of whom, in all charity, it was true! The modern name for being righteous overmuch is being a “crank.” Now, nobody loves a crank. The extremist always does his own cause harm. Carefulness about one’s food is a good thing, but to take an analytical chemist’s outfit to table with us is simply to ask for the contempt of all sensible people.

===Chinese===
有时候，为着真宗教的缘故，人必须作出论断；但这样的时刻远没有我们想象的那么频繁。倘若说门徒在什么场合格外需要基督的灵满溢的分量，那就是在他明确的本分是诊断、不认可并谴责的时候。

其次，我们的善可能因我们的*极端*（extremeness）而遭人毁谤。行义过分这回事是否存在，我本来该说得十分谨慎，只是有两个理由使我说它存在。第一个理由是，圣经中有明诫禁止行义过分。第二个理由是，我确实遇见过这样的人——凭最大的爱心说，行义过分在他们身上是实话！行义过分在现代的名字叫作「怪人」（crank）。可没有谁喜欢怪人。走极端的人总是害了自己的主张。留意自己的饮食是件好事，可要是带着分析化学师的整套行头来与我们同桌上饭，那就简直是自讨一切明理之人的鄙夷了。

===Original===
Paul’s advice to the Philippians was, “Let your moderation be known to all men.” And Paul was himself a splendid example of the true moderation as distinguished from that which is merely indolent and uninterested. Earnest, enthusiastic, loyal, there was yet about him a big and healthy sanity, a sweet reasonableness, and—what the extremist always lacks—an engaging tact. In other words, Paul was a Christian gentleman, and if you want to know what that means, read his letter to Philemon about Onesimus the runaway slave. There are blunt words with which a man can be felled as effectually as with the “grievous crab-tree cudgel” of which Bunyan speaks. Paul did not consider it any special virtue to employ such words. His Christian zeal did not lead him to make a statement in a way that would irritate and rasp a man’s soul. There is a certain extreme candour affected by some Christian people, who pride themselves on always calling a spade a spade. But if it hurts my friend to hear me say “spade” I know of no law of God that compels me to name the implement at all!

===Chinese===
保罗给腓立比人的劝勉是：「当叫众人知道你们谦让的心。」保罗自己就是真谦让的辉煌典范，与那种不过是怠惰漠然的「谦让」判然有别。他恳切、热忱、忠贞，然而身上自有一种宽宏健全的清醒，一种甜美的通情达理，还有一种——那总是为极端派所缺少的——动人得体的圆融。换言之，保罗是一位基督徒绅士；你若想知道这是什么意思，就去读他写给腓利门、论到那逃跑的奴隶阿尼西母的信。世上有一些直截的字眼，能把人打倒，其效力不亚于班扬（Bunyan）所说的那根「伤人的酸木棍」（grievous crab-tree cudgel）。保罗并不认为使用这样的字眼有什么特别的美德。他基督徒的热忱，并没有引他用一种激惹人、磨锉人灵魂的方式去陈述一件事。有些基督徒沾染一种极端的坦率，以凡事「是铲子就说铲子」为荣。可要是我的朋友听见我说「铲子」就受伤，那我不知道上帝有什么律法，非逼我把那件家什的名字说出来不可！

===Original===
And then, lastly, we can have our goodness “evil spoken of” because it is so *cold*. It sometimes seems as if, in our day, warmth of manner had gone out of fashion. Ian Maclaren once said of our generation that it will “smile feebly when wished a happy New Year as if apologising for a lapse into barbarism.” But I don’t think any sensible person, not blinded by an absurd convention, cares for that type of rarified demeanour. No one likes to get a hand to shake which feels like a dead fish!

In one of his books, Dr. Dale of Birmingham criticised that line in Keble’s hymn which speaks about the trivial round and the common task giving us “room to deny ourselves.” “No doubt,” he says, “but I should be very sorry for the people I live with to discharge their home duties in the spirit of martyrs. God preserve us all from wives, husbands, children, brothers, and sisters who go about the house with an air of celestial resignation.” Ah, no, that’s not the goodness, either at home or on the street, which wins men. It is not beautiful because it is too cold. The religion of Jesus is something much more than duty-doing. Thou shalt love the Lord thy *God with all thy heart*. Whosoever compels thee to go a mile, *go with him twain*. Whatsoever ye do, do it *heartily as unto the Lord*.

===Chinese===
再者，末了，我们的善还可能因其过于*冷淡*（cold）而「被人毁谤」。有时候似乎在我们这个时代，待人的温热已经过时了。伊恩·麦克拉伦（Ian Maclaren）有一次论到我们这一代人说，人们被人祝一句「新年快乐」，也只会「有气无力地笑一笑，仿佛在为失足落入野蛮而赔罪」。但我以为，凡是明理的人，只要没有被一种荒谬的习俗蒙住眼，没有谁会喜欢那一种稀薄高冷的仪态。没有谁愿意握到一只摸起来像死鱼的手！

伯明翰的戴尔博士（R. W. Dale）在他的一部书里，批评了基布尔赞美诗中的诗行——那行诗说，琐碎的例行事务与寻常的日常职务，给我们「留下舍己的余地」。「这自然不错，」他说，「可是，倘若与我同住的人带着殉道者的精神来尽家中的本分，我会替他们难过得很。愿上帝保守我们众人，脱离那些带着一脸属天听任的神情在家中走动的妻子、丈夫、儿女、弟兄和姊妹。」啊，不是的；无论在家中还是在街上，能赢得人心的都不是那样的善。它并不美，因为它太冷了。耶稣的宗教远不止于尽本分。「你要*尽心*爱主你的*神*。」「有人强逼你走一里路，你就*同他走二里*。」「无论做什么，都要*从心里做，像是给主做的*。」

===Original===
Prayer

From all unkind thoughts and uncharitable judgments; from all intemperate speech and behaviour; from coldness of heart and a frigid service, Good Lord, deliver us. For Thy Name’s sake. Amen.

===Chinese===
祈祷

求主救我们脱离一切恶念与不存爱心的论断；脱离一切放肆无度的言语与行为；脱离心中的冷漠与冰冷的事奉。良善的主啊，拯救我们。为你名的缘故求。阿们。

## XII A Khaki Virtue / XII 卡其美德

===Original===
> “God loveth a cheerful giver.”
> — 2 Corinthians 9:7

===Chinese===
> 「神喜爱捐得乐意的人。」
> — 2 Corinthians 9:7

===Original===
We are proud to believe that, in the article of courage, our men are second to none in the world. They have glorious traditions to live up to, and they are adding to these pages—nay, a whole volume, as splendid as any in our annals. Yet it is not of our soldiers’ courage I wish to speak.
For we are told on all hands that there is another quality shining brighter still these days in the trenches in France and Belgium, in ambulance wagons and field hospitals, and in the camps at home, namely, cheerfulness. Again and again the same tale is repeated from one quarter or another—“our men are simply wonderful,” “they treat discomfort as a joke.” They label the very instruments that deal death among them with names that raise a smile. Nurses, doctors, and correspondents tell us that the lighthearted way in which our soldiers face pain and suffering and force twisted lips to smile has created a new record for the British Army. When the story of this war is written, and the world gets a nearer glimpse into those awful trenches, I venture to prophesy that the quality in our countrymen which will most capture the imagination and fill us with the greatest pride will be the gay, undaunted cheerfulness with which they faced it all.

===Chinese===
我们自豪地相信，在勇气这一项上，我们的人不逊于世上的任何民族。他们有光荣的传统要无愧地承继，而且他们正在给这些书页增添新页——不，是增添整整一卷，其辉煌不亚于我们史册里的任何一卷。然而，我此刻要讲的，并不是我们军人的勇气。
因为四面八方都传来消息说，如今还有一种品质，在法国与比利时的战壕里，在救护车与野战医院里，在本国的兵营里，闪耀得更加明亮，那就是快乐。同一个故事，从这个角落传到那个角落，一遍又一遍——「我们的人简直了不起」，「他们把困苦当作笑话来讲」。他们甚至给那些在他们中间施行死亡的兵器，起了令人发笑的名字。护士们、医生们和随军记者们告诉我们：我们的士兵面对痛苦磨难时那副无忧无虑的神情，那种强使扭歪的嘴唇绽出笑容的本领，已经为英国陆军创下了一项新纪录。当这场大战的历史写成，世人得以更近地窥见那些可怖的战壕时，我斗胆预言：我们的同胞身上最能动人心魄、最令我们满怀自豪的品质，将是他们面对这一切时所怀的那种欢然无畏的快乐。

===Original===
Surely we who stay at home may learn something of that virtue too. For it is worth learning. Ordinary people who only know what they like, without knowing why they like it, have a very warm side towards the person who, when things are grey and gloomy, can keep cheerful. They would much rather see him come in on a dull day than a wiser man whose wisdom was a burden to him, or even than a pious person whose piety ran to solemnity and gloom. It is high time, indeed, that the tradition was broken for good and all which associates moral excellence with a funereal heaviness of manner and denies the favour of the Lord to one who, as Goldsmith has it, “carols as he goes.”
For the blessing of God is written visibly upon the results of cheerfulness wherever you find it. God rewards the gallant souls who keep their colours flying through every battle, even though they have to nail them up over a sorely damaged ship. If you want a proof that the hopeful and cheery way of facing the rebuffs of life and tholing its aches and disappointments is more in the line of what God expects from His children than the doleful whining temper, you have it shown unmistakably in the fact that the gallant unconquerable soul solves problems, overcomes difficulties, endures pains, and wins successes where the solemn and easily depressed would simply have given in and lain down. You can safely prophesy that the man whom you hear singing as he goes through the valley, like the pilgrim that Bunyan’s Christian heard, is going to get out of it safely and honourably in the end. The Lord Himself will deliver him, as He delights to deliver all those who face life smiling and unafraid, and meet His Fatherly discipline with a stout heart.

===Chinese===
当然，我们这些留在家中的人，也可以学一点那样的美德。因为它值得学。寻常人只知道自己喜欢什么，说不出为什么喜欢；可对那种天色阴沉灰暗时仍能保持快乐的人，他们总有一副热心肠。在阴郁的日子里，他们宁肯见他走进门来，也不愿见一个更有智慧、智慧却成了他重担的人，甚至更不愿见一个虔诚、虔诚却流于肃穆阴沉的人。的确，那种把道德上的优秀同一副丧礼般阴沉的举止绑在一起、不肯把主的恩眷加给那如哥尔德斯密斯（Goldsmith）所说「且行且唱」之人的传统，如今早该一劳永逸地破除了。
因为无论你在哪里看见快乐结出的果子，上帝祝福的印记都明明写在上面。上帝赏赐那些英勇的灵魂：他们在每一场战斗中都让军旗高扬，即便不得不把旗钉在一艘受创惨重的船上。你若要一个证明，证明那满有盼望、欢然喜乐，直面人生的挫折、硬扛（tholing）它的伤痛与失望的态度，比愁苦哀哼的脾气更合乎上帝对他儿女期望的路数，那么，这证明就摆在一个无可误认的事实里：英勇不屈的灵魂，在肃穆易沉者只会干脆认输、躺倒不起的地方，解决了难题，克服了困难，忍住了痛楚，赢得了成功。你可以放心预言：那一个你听见他且行且唱穿过幽谷的人——正如班扬笔下的基督徒（Christian）曾听见的那位天路客——至终必平安而荣耀地从谷中出来。主自己必搭救他，正如主乐于搭救一切面带微笑、毫无惧色地面对人生，并以刚毅之心迎接他为父般的管教的人。

===Original===
Cheerfulness, in other words, pays for oneself. But it is also a great blessing to others. One very safe and sure way to help our fellows up their hills is to breast our own as bravely and gaily as we can. And the cheerfulness which heals and blesses like the breath of morning is that which shows up against a background of cloud and trouble. Let us all in this year of war and clean courage, register a vow that we shall take a leaf out of our soldiers’ book, and think less about our own troubles, teach our lips to smile when things are wrong, and keep our eyes wider open for trouble’s danger signals among our friends. It’s a simple way of doing good, but a very effective one. For cheerfulness, like mercy, is twice blessed. It blesseth him that has, and him that sees!

> “It was only a glad Good Morning
> As she passed along the way,
> But it spread the morning’s glory
> Over the livelong day.”

===Chinese===
换句话说，快乐是付得回自身代价的。但它也是给他人的极大祝福。要扶助我们的同伴登上他们的山坡，一个极稳妥、极可靠的办法，就是尽我们所能，勇敢而欢畅地去攀我们自己眼前的坡。而那如清晨的气息一般医治人、祝福人的快乐，乃是衬托在乌云与患难的底色上显出来的快乐。在这战争与纯洁勇气之年，让我们大家一同立下誓愿：我们要以我们的军人为榜样，少想自己的烦恼，事情不顺时教导我们的嘴唇微笑，又把眼睛睁得更大，留心察看朋友们身上患难的危险信号。这是行善的一个简便法门，却极其有效。因为快乐正如仁慈，是双重的祝福：它祝福那拥有的人，也祝福那看见的人！

> 「那不过是一声欢欢喜喜的『早安』，
> 当她沿路走过的时候，
> 却把清晨的荣光
> 铺满了整整一天。」

===Original===
But cheerfulness needs its explanation. It implies something. A man is not cheerful without some underlying philosophy of life to sustain him, some pillar of faith or hope at his back. When a man faces life dauntless and smiling, he does so because some inward and, it may even be, unconscious faith or hope thus finds its expression. What that faith is, different men will describe in different ways.
But however much the descriptions vary, it all comes back to this in the end, that the man who is living bravely and cheerfully is expressing by his conduct at any rate his faith in the Fatherhood and good Providence of God. He knows that “God’s in His Heaven”; at any rate he believes so. He believes that things do not just fall out by chance, but that a Father Hand controls all, and a Father Heart cares even for the sparrow’s unheeded fall. The God who rules all makes no mistakes.

===Chinese===
但快乐需要它的解释。它意味着某种东西。一个人若没有某种潜藏的人生哲学支撑着他，背后没有一根信心或盼望的柱子，就不会快乐。当一个人无所畏惧、面带微笑地面对人生时，他之所以能如此，是因为某种内在的、甚至可能是不自觉的信心或盼望，正借此得着表达。那信心究竟是什么，各人的描述会各不相同。
但无论这些描述如何分歧，归根结底总回到这一点上：那勇敢而快乐地活着的人，至少正以他的行事为人，表达他对上帝的父性与美善护理的信心。他知道「上帝在他的天上」；至少他如此相信。他相信万事并非偶然落定，乃是一只父亲的手掌管一切，一颗父亲的心连麻雀那无人注意的坠跌也顾念。统管万有的上帝不会出错。

===Original===
And is not that a cardinal part of the faith which Jesus brings near to all who are learning of Him? There are various adjectives used to qualify the title Christian. One hears, for example, of “earnest Christians,” and earnestness is a very necessary quality, even though one does occasionally happen upon “earnest Christians” who are rather unlovable and irritating people. But there’s another adjective, not nearly so common—and yet it denotes a quality just as essential in those who have taken Christ’s gospel of God’s Love and Fatherhood to their hearts—namely, cheerful. A “cheerful Christian.” Let us all try to be that kind of Christian at least.

===Chinese===
耶稣带到凡跟从他学习之人跟前的信心，这岂不是其中首要的一环吗？人们用种种形容词来修饰「基督徒」这称呼。例如，人们常听说「热诚的基督徒」；热诚确是一种十分必要的品质，尽管偶尔也会碰上一些「热诚的基督徒」，却是相当不可爱、惹人恼火的人。但另有一个形容词，远没有那么常见——然而它所指的品质，对那些已把基督所传上帝之爱与父性的福音接到心里的人同样必不可少——那就是：快乐的。「一位快乐的基督徒。」让我们大家至少都试着做这一种基督徒吧。

===Original===
Prayer

> “The day returns and brings us the petty round of irritating concerns and duties. Help us to play the man, help us to perform them with laughter and kind faces, let cheerfulness abound with industry. Give us to go blithely on our business all this day, bring us to our resting beds weary and content and undishonoured, and grant us in the end the gift of sleep. Amen.”
> — R. L. Stevenson.

===Chinese===
祈祷

> 「白日又回来了，给我们带来那恼人琐务与本分的小小循环。求你帮助我们作大丈夫，帮助我们带着欢笑与和善的面容去尽这些本分，让快乐与勤勉一同丰盈。求你使我们这一天都欢然去做自己的事务，到夜晚把我们带到安歇的床上——困倦而满足，且未受羞辱；末了，求你赐下睡眠的恩赐。阿们。」
> — R. L. Stevenson.

## XIII The Overcoming of Panic / XIII 克服恐慌

===Original===
> “Jeremiah dwelt among the people that were left in the land.”
> — Jeremiah 40:6

===Chinese===
> 耶利米住在境内剩下的民中。
> — Jeremiah 40:6

===Original===
Once upon a time Jeremiah the prophet had asked for only one thing, that he might get away from that strange cityful of perverse men to whom it was his hard lot to be the mouthpiece of a God they were forgetting. He was tired of them. “O that I had in the wilderness a lodging place of wayfaring men that I might leave my people and go from them.”

Well, time passed on. The people got no wiser, and Jeremiah’s burden certainly got no lighter. But the very chance he prayed for came. He had a clear and honourable opportunity to go to the lodge in the wilderness, or anywhere else he liked, away from the men who had disowned his teaching. His work was done apparently, and he had failed. Yet with the door standing invitingly open, see what Jeremiah did! He “went and dwelt among the people that were left in the land.” He had his chance and he did not take it!

===Chinese===
从前，先知耶利米只求过一件事：好让他离开那座满了乖僻之人的奇怪城邑；向那些人作他们所正忘记之上帝的出口，原是他命定的苦差。他厌倦了他们。「惟愿我在旷野有行路人住宿之处，使我可以离开我的民出去。」

这样，时间过去了。百姓没有更智慧，耶利米的重担也当然没有更轻省。可恰恰是他曾祈求的那个机会来了。他有一道清清楚楚、光明正大的门路，可以往旷野那住宿之处去，或往他所喜欢的任何地方去，远远离开那些拒绝他教导的人。他的工作看来已经完毕，而他是失败了。然而，门正敞着招手，请看耶利米做了什么！他「就去住在境内剩下的民中」。机会来了，他却没有拿它！

===Original===
We all know something of this desire to get rid of a present hard duty, or a difficult environment, or a perplexing problem. And yet I wonder, if the way were similarly opened up for us, how many would seize the opportunity? I believe that the feature of such a situation would just be the large number of us who, when it came to the pinch, would choose as Jeremiah did, to remain where we are! Something would hold us back.

Yet the desire itself is natural enough, and a man need neither be a coward nor a weakling who confesses to it. The hours when the daily round seems altogether flat and unprofitable, and when one would gladly change places with almost anybody, are real hours in life, and it is no shame to have known them. But between that knowledge and the actual escape, the actual fleeing from one’s post, there is a great gulf fixed that, for very many with any high ideal of duty, is impassable. For, though a man has known the state of mind that looks for some back door out of a depressing situation, he has had the other experience also, the joy of self-mastery, the keen sense of pleasure that comes to him when he discovers that his surroundings do not count for so much as he himself does. That experience, though it be only in memory, will stand between a man and retreat. He has conquered before, and the thrill of victory over material discouragements may be his again. And so, though the way of escape be open, he will choose to remain and fight it out.

===Chinese===
我们都知道一点这样的心愿：想摆脱眼前一桩艰难的本分，或一个难处的环境，或一个缠磨人的难题。然而我纳罕：倘若同样有一条路为我们敞开，究竟有多少人会抓住那机会呢？我相信，这样一幅光景的特色恰恰在于：我们中间会有许多人，到了紧要关头，却像耶利米那样选择留在原地！总有什么东西会把我们拉住。

然而这心愿本身原是再自然不过的，一个承认它的人未必就是懦夫，也未必就是弱者。日常周而复始的光景显得全然平淡无味、使人愿意同几乎任何人调换位置的那些时辰，是人生里实实在在的时辰，经历过它们并不可耻。可是在这样的自知与实际的脱逃——实际逃离自己的岗位——之间，有一个深渊限定了，对许多怀着任何崇高本分理想的人，都是过不去的。因为，一个人虽然晓得那种想从沮丧境地里寻个后门溜走的心境，却也另有别的经历：制胜自我的喜乐，当他发现自己周围的环境并不如他自己要紧时所感到的那份强烈的快意。那经历纵然只在记忆里，也必站立在人与退却之间。他从前得胜过，那战胜物质挫折的战栗的狂喜，可能再成为他的。这样，纵然逃生之路敞开着，他仍要选择留下，奋战到底。

===Original===
Sometimes the mere weight of his responsibility may tempt a man to wish that he might escape. There is a fairly well-known symptom of nervous disease whose name signifies the fear of being shut in, when the patient dreads the experience of being in any closed place. Sometimes a moral panic of that kind comes to a man when he realises that he is shut in with some duty which must be gone through with. With something of the instinct of the trapped animal he may look round for a way of escape.

Yet does that mean that he would take the chance deliberately, with eyes full open to the consequences, if it were offered? I think not.

===Chinese===
有时候，单是责任本身的重量，就足以试探一个人，叫巴不得能够逃脱。神经疾病有一种相当知名的病征，名字的意思便是惧怕封闭：病人一想到被关进任何闭合的地方就惊惧。有时候，这一类的道德恐慌也会临到一个人，当他发觉自己与一桩非了结不可的本分同被关在一处的时候。他或许会带着几分困兽的本能，环顾四围，寻找一条逃路。

然而，这是不是说，倘若机会当真摆在他面前，他就会睁大眼睛、明知一切后果地故意去抓住它呢？我想不会。

===Original===
You can apply the test to yourself. Have you ever accepted some responsibility, and then, when the occasion came nearer, backed out of it for no other reason than that you were afraid? If you have, you will perhaps remember whether you felt proud of yourself, whether, beneath the undoubted relief, there was not a good deal of quiet shame and self-scorn. If the same thing were to happen again, you might feel the impulse to desert, but if you remembered your former experience, you would hardly yield to it, I imagine.

The plain truth is that no proper man really likes a soft job. “In the long run,” says J. A. Symonds, “we really love the sternest things in life best.” And he speaks truth. There is a certain exhilaration in the endurance of hardness. Responsibility braces most men like a shock of cold water. What is arduous calls them as with a trumpet. And in the general sense of quiet contempt for the person who in a panic flings up his responsibility, we may recognise one of God’s elementary checks upon cowardice.

===Chinese===
你可以把这试验用在自己身上。你可曾接受过某种责任，及至那日子临近，却仅仅因为害怕就打了退堂鼓？倘若有过，你或许还记得你当时是否觉得自己可夸；在那无可置疑的如释重负之下，是否并没有藏着好些暗暗的羞惭与自鄙。倘若同样的事再度发生，你或许仍会感到想临阵脱逃的冲动；但你若记起从前的经历，我想你几乎不会向它屈服。

明白的实情乃是：没有一个真正的人当真喜欢轻松的差事。「终究而论，」西蒙兹（J. A. Symonds）说，「我们真正最爱的，还是人生里最严酷的事物。」他说的是实话。忍受艰难里头自有一种昂扬。责任像一盆冷水，使大多数人挺拔振作。艰巨的事如吹号一般召唤他们。而对那在恐慌中把责任一撒手的人，人人心里那暗暗的鄙夷之感，我们从中可以认出，正是上帝遏制怯懦的一种初步的管制。

===Original===
There are those who are reading these words who are enduring hardness and making sacrifices from which they might easily escape. They do at times desire relief. But the point is that they don’t take it, when it is possible. And I say there must be some reason for this. What is it that holds men back from the easy way when it stands open before them?

For one thing, I think, the sense of the place that hardness and effort and endurance play in every true life. For centuries men have climbed up to strength of character, if at all, by ways uniformly arduous and steep; and distrust of the primrose path, however alluring, has passed as an instinct into our blood. In the small unheroic affairs of life we have learned that a difficulty faced and overcome, or a duty doggedly fulfilled, add a precious something to experience that there is no other way of securing. The schoolboy on a hot summer day may look up from his task, away out wistfully to the cool shade of the trees across the playground, and wish that he were there, rather than where he is. Yet even he knows, what we all come to learn, that that is not the road to anything in life worth the gaining.

===Chinese===
读这些话的人当中，就有人正在忍受艰难、作出牺牲，而这些他们原可以轻易逃避。他们确有时渴望舒缓。但要点在于：当舒缓可能的时候，他们并不取它。我说，这里头必定有个缘故。当那条容易的路敞开在人面前时，究竟是什么拦阻人不去走它呢？

一个缘故，我想，是感到艰难、劳苦与坚忍在每一个真实人生里所占的地位。多少世纪以来，人若真要攀登到坚强的品格，所走的路无一不是又难又陡；对那条诱人的安逸花径的不信任，已然作为一种本能传进了我们的血脉。在人生那些细小而无关英雄业绩的事务里，我们已经学会：一个迎面面对并且胜过的难处，或一桩咬牙完成的本分，会给阅历添上一样宝贵的东西，此外再无别法可以得着。学童在炎热的夏日，会从功课上抬起头，痴痴望向操场对面清凉的树荫，巴不得自己在那里，而不是在自己所在之处。然而连他也知道——这是我们众人终要学得的——那条路通不到人生里任何值得赢得的事物。

===Original===
Another deterring impulse is the sense of a divine vocation. Our calling and circumstances are ordained for us by God, and we must not quit the field till the day is done. It is He who has chosen our lot in life and summoned us to the sphere we fill.

We may succeed or fail as seems to Him best. Sometimes he places men, for reasons of His own, in corners where success, as commonly measured, is not possible. But one thing—success or failure—we must not do. We must not shirk. We must not run away. God means us to stand fast and do our best. For failure even, if it be honourable, He may have His good word at the last. But to the man who has shirked life’s hard duties, not even God can say, “Well done!”

===Chinese===
另一个拦阻人的冲动，是对神圣呼召的感觉。我们的蒙召与境遇是上帝为我们指定的，日子未完，我们不可离开战场。拣选我们一生之分的、召我们到现今所处地位的，正是祂。

我们可以照祂看为最好的或成功或失败。有时候，祂因着自己的缘故，把人安置在照寻常标准无成功可能的角落里。但有一件事我们断乎不可做——无论成败。我们不可推诿。我们不可逃跑。上帝的意思是要我们站立得稳，竭尽己力。纵然是失败，只要是光荣的，祂末后或许还有嘉许的话。但对那推诿了人生艰难本分的人，连上帝也无法说：「好！」

===Original===
Prayer
Lord of our life, and God of our salvation, make us strong to endure hardness as good soldiers of Jesus Christ. Thou sendest no man a warfare upon his own charges. In dependence on Thy help, grant us grace to do each duty, as the hour and Thy will may bring it. And, with Thy fear in our hearts, grant us deliverance from all other fears whatever. For Thy Name’s sake. Amen.

===Chinese===
祈祷
我们生命的主，我们救恩的神啊，求祢使我们刚强，得以作为耶稣基督的精兵忍受艰难。祢差人出征，从不叫人自备粮饷。求祢借着倚靠祢的帮助，施恩使我们尽每一样本分，如时候与祢的旨意所带来的一般。并且，愿敬畏祢的心在我们里面，使我们脱离其余一切的惧怕。奉祢的名求。阿们。

## XIV The Day’s Darg / XIV 一天的活计

===Original===
> “Whatsoever ye do, do all to the glory of God.”
> — 1 Corinthians 10:31

===Chinese===
> 「你们无论做什么，都要为荣耀神而行。」
> — 1 Corinthians 10:31

===Original===
It is never hard to connect the presence of our Lord and Master Jesus Christ with our Sabbaths and our hours of worship. If ever Christ comes near us in spirit at all, we say, it is when in the quiet of the sanctuary we reach out hands of prayer and desire to Him. The link between our worship and our Lord is strong and obvious. But, when the din of business shuts out all else, when the hard, toilsome duty of the ordinary day is to be done, when we are at work amid surroundings that have no suggestion of sacredness or of God about them—what of the link with Christ then? It is much harder then, is it not? to imagine any thinkable and workable connection that our Lord has with that sphere of life, broad and extensive as it is. There are many indeed who forget that there is any, and live as if there were none. And yet the solemn truth is that if that link is not strong and real, we don’t know what religion means. We have hardly the right to call ourselves Christian men and women unless we can relate our weekday labours to the fact of Christ.

So let us try to strengthen that link. Let us look at our daily work in the light of religion.

===Chinese===
要把我们主耶稣基督的同在，与我们的安息日、我们敬拜的时辰联结起来，从来不是难事。我们说，基督若真会在灵里亲近我们，那必是在圣所的宁静中，我们向祂伸出祷告与渴慕之手的时候。我们敬拜与我们主之间的这一环，坚固而显明。可是，当事务的喧嚣把其余一切都隔绝在外，当寻常一日艰苦劳碌的本分有待完成，当我们在全无圣洁意味、毫无上帝气息的环境里做工时——那时，与基督的这一环又如何呢？到了那时，要设想我们的主与人生那片领域之间还有什么想得通、行得通的联结，岂不难得多么？——那领域何等宽广辽阔。的确有许多人忘了有这联结存在，活得仿佛根本没有这一回事。然而严肃的真理乃是：这一环若不坚固、不真实，我们就不知道信仰意味着什么。我们若不能把工作日的劳作与基督这一事实关联起来，就几乎无权自称基督徒男女。

这样，就让我们试着坚固这一环。让我们在信仰的光照下，来看我们每日的工作。

===Original===
First, let me remind you that our work is by divine commandment. It is not something that God allows us to do when we are not worshipping. It is His ordinance that we should all work at something. The business of life is labour of some sort. I do not know if we all realise how the Fourth Commandment begins—“Six days shalt thou labour and do all thy work.” And the man who is inexcusably idle, or who belittles his work, even in the interest, as he thinks, of religion, is breaking this commandment as truly as he who neglects the other half of it and dishonours the Sabbath day.

No one will accuse the Apostle Paul of any indifference or lukewarmness where true religion was concerned. Yet it was this Apostle who ordered the Thessalonians to go on with their daily occupations even though they believed, as so many did at that time, that the return of the Lord to earth was just at hand. By our daily work we serve the Lord as truly as when we gather to His worship. Let us get out of our heads, then, the false and foolish idea that all the working part of our week is the part at which God looks askance. Man’s chief end is to glorify God, and one of the ways of doing that is by being loyal to the duties of each hour whatever they may be.

===Chinese===
第一，请容我提醒诸位：我们的工作乃出于神圣的诫命。它并不是上帝在我们不敬拜的时候才容许我们去做的什么事。人人都当操些什么营生，这原是祂的定规。人生的营生总归是某种劳作。我不知道我们是否都意识到，第四条诫命是怎样开头的——「六日要劳碌做你一切的工」。那无可推诿而闲懒的人，或轻看自己工作的人——哪怕他自以为是出于信仰的缘故——正在违犯这条诫命，其真实程度，与那忽略这诫命的另一半、羞辱安息日的人无异。

在真信仰的事上，谁也不会指责使徒保罗冷淡怠慢、不冷不热。然而正是这位使徒，吩咐帖撒罗尼迦人继续操持他们日常的本业，尽管他们——当时确有许多人这样信——相信主回到地上已近在眉睫。藉着每日的工作，我们事奉主，其真实不亚于我们聚集敬拜祂的时候。这样，就让我们把这虚假而愚妄的念头从头脑里清除出去，以为我们一周中做工的日子，正是上帝皱眉侧目的日子。人的首要目的乃是荣耀上帝，而荣耀上帝的途径之一，就是忠于每个时辰的本分，无论它是什么。

===Original===
Secondly, I would ask you to think of those quiet, unrecorded years of our Lord’s life on earth before His public ministry. The Gospels give no details, but the fact is perfectly certain that up till His thirtieth year Jesus of Nazareth worked at His trade as a carpenter. If only we would let that fact soak into us, it would alter our whole idea of the relation of our daily work to religion. Jesus worked Himself.

And we have, as has been pointed out, interesting indirect proof as to what manner of life He lived on those workaday levels that we all know so much about. For, to this Carpenter of Nazareth there came a day when, in Nazareth itself, He stood forth as representative of a morality and religion higher than ever was proclaimed before. He spoke to men about the true way to live like one having authority. And there were many who so resented what they deemed His presumption that anything that reflected on His claims or belittled His authority would gladly have been seized upon and made the most of. Had there been in Nazareth a bit of botched work of His doing, “a door of unseasoned wood or a badly made chest,” don’t you think it would have been produced to discredit His mission? If anyone could have been found with whom the Carpenter had not dealt honourably and justly, if, as He walked the streets of His native town and lived His humble daily life in the sight of all men, there had been anything that weakened His claim to guide and teach His brethren, don’t you think they would have found it out and taxed Him with it?

===Chinese===
第二，我要请诸位想一想，我们主在地上、在祂公开传道以前那些安静的、未载经传的岁月。福音书没有记述细节，但有一件事千真万确：直到三十岁，拿撒勒人耶稣一直操着木匠的手艺做工。我们若肯让这个事实浸透进来，它必改变我们对每日工作与信仰之关系的全部观念。耶稣自己就曾做工。

而且，正如有人指出的那样，关于祂在那我们都再熟悉不过的日常劳作层面上过着怎样的生活，我们还有一些耐人寻味的间接证据。因为，有一天来到了：就在拿撒勒本地，这位拿撒勒的木匠挺身而出，作了一种道德与信仰的代表，比从前所宣讲过的一切都更高。祂像有权柄的人一样，向人讲论生活的正道。而许多人极其愤恨他们所认为的祂的僭妄，以致凡有丝毫可攻讦祂的主张、贬抑祂的权柄的事，他们都巴不得抓住，大做文章。倘若拿撒勒有过一件祂做糟了的活儿——「一扇用未干木料做的门，或一口做工粗劣的箱子」——你们难道不以为，人早就把它翻出来，败坏祂的使命了么？倘若能找出一个人，证明这木匠待他不诚不公；倘若祂在本乡的街上行走、当众人面前过祂卑微的日常起居时，有任何削弱祂引导并教导弟兄之凭据的事——你们难道不以为，他们早就把它查出来，拿来责问祂了么？

===Original===
There was nothing of that. Jesus faced His fellows with His daily duty behind Him, and it reinforced every word He said. His message to men was backed up by His daily life. He spoke of religion as no other son of man ever did, but He lived it long before He ever opened His mouth. He brought religion down to the workshop and the street, and showed men what it meant there. And unless He had done that, it is difficult to conceive that His public ministry of itself would have satisfied men that He was indeed One sent from God.

Do you see, then, from this point of view, what a great and vital part of religion our day’s work is, and the way we do it, our life at home, our ordinary contact with our fellow-men? It is that that gives weight to any profession we may make. If in our daily life we are not exhibiting our religion, nothing that we can profess or say on Sunday will make up for that defect. It is what we are on Monday and Tuesday that underlines and emphasises the claims we make at church on the Sunday. Behind all our prayer and profession lies the everyday life.

===Chinese===
这样的事一件也没有。耶稣面对祂的乡邻时，背后立着祂每日的本分，而这本分坚固了祂口中的每一句话。祂给人的信息，有祂的日常生活作后盾。祂讲论信仰，是世人中无人曾那样讲论过的；但祂早在开口之先，就已活出了信仰。祂把信仰带到了工场与街头，向人显明信仰在那里意味着什么。祂若不曾这样做，我们就很难设想，单凭祂公开的传道职事，怎能叫人确信祂实在是上帝所差来的那一位。

那么，从这个观点看去，你们可曾看见，我们一天的活计——连同我们做工的方式、我们在家中的生活、我们与旁人平常的往来——在信仰中占着何等重大、何等紧要的一部分么？正是它，为我们任何的宣认添上分量。我们若在日常生活在人面前显不出信仰来，那么主日里无论认信什么、说什么，都补不上这个亏缺。我们星期一、星期二为人如何，才给我们主日在教会里所表明的主张划线加重。在我们一切祷告与认信的背后，立着那日复一日的日常生活。

===Original===
Third, our daily work is sanctified by the fact that our Lord and Master is with us, to help and strengthen us there, as truly as when we pray. Jesus Christ is not far away, as we so pitifully misconceive it, amid the dust of business, when we must keep our temper and follow conscience along the hard way and deal honourably with all men. He is near us there also, ready and willing to help us to be true to God and man on that road which once He trod Himself.

There is a famous unwritten saying of Christ which puts memorably what the Gospels likewise testify. “Raise the stone and thou shalt find Me. Cleave the wood and there am I.” Christ is as near us in our daily work as that! When Peter and his friends went a-fishing, you remember, with heavy hearts because the Master had gone away from them, He met them by the lake as they plied their ordinary calling. So does He wait, my brother, to meet you and me wherever the duty of the hour may take us. For our working life is not outside of His interest nor out with His care and guidance. With reverent imagination Van Dyke has seemed to hear the Christ speak thus—and the words may perhaps further weld the link for some of us between our everyday duty and the Christ whom we worship and seek to serve:

===Chinese===
第三，我们每日的工作得以分别为圣，是因为我们主与我们同在，要在那里帮助我们、坚固我们，正如我们祷告时祂与我们同在一样真实。在事务的尘嚣之中，当我们必须克制脾气、随着良心走那条艰难的路、与一切人诚实相待时，耶稣基督并非远在高天——尽管我们何等可怜地这样误解。在那里，祂也同样临近我们，甘心乐意地帮助我们，使我们在那条祂自己曾亲自走过的路上，对上帝、对人都真诚无伪。

基督有一句著名的、未载于经卷的话语，令人难忘地道出福音书也同样见证的真理：「举起石头，你就必寻见我；劈开木头，我就在那里。」在我们的日常工作中，基督就这样亲近我们！你还记得，彼得和他的朋友们因为主已离他们而去，心情沉重地去打鱼；主却在湖边迎见那正操持本业的他们。我的弟兄啊，无论此时此刻的本分把你我带到哪里，主都照样等候着迎见我们。因为我们的劳作生涯并不在祂的眷念之外，也不在祂的看顾与引领之外。范戴克（Van Dyke）凭着虔敬的想象，仿佛听见基督如此说话——这些话，或许能为我们中间的一些人，把日常本分与我们敬拜、事奉的基督之间的那一环焊接得更牢：

===Original===
> “They who tread the path of labour follow where My feet have trod;
> They who work without complaining do the holy will of God.
> Where the many toil together, there am I among my own;
> Where the tired workman sleepeth, there am I with Him alone.
> I, the peace that passeth knowledge, dwell amid the daily strife,
> I, the bread of heaven, am broken in the sacrament of life.
> Every task, however simple, sets the soul that does it free,
> Every deed of love and mercy done to man is done to Me.
> Nevermore thou needest seek Me; I am with thee everywhere—
> Raise the stone and thou shalt find Me, cleave the wood and I am there.”

===Chinese===
> 凡踏上劳作之路的，正跟随我双脚所行的踪迹；
> 凡做工而无怨言的，就是在行上帝圣善的旨意。
> 众人一同劳作之处，我就在属我的人中间；
> 疲乏的工人安睡之处，我独自与他同在。
> 我，那过于人所能测度的平安，住在日日的纷争之中；
> 我，天上来的粮，在生命的圣礼中被擘开。
> 每一样工作，无论何等简单，都使那做工的灵魂得自由；
> 向人所行的每一件仁爱怜悯之事，就是行在我身上。
> 从此你不必再寻求我；无论何处我都与你同在——
> 举起石头，你就必寻见我；劈开木头，我就在那里。」

===Original===
Prayer
Our Lord and Master, whose command it is that we do with our whole heart whatsoever our hand findeth to do, grant that we may so yield and surrender ourselves, body, mind and spirit, unto Thee, that even in the common business of each ordinary day we may serve Thee and glorify Thy great Name. Amen.

===Chinese===
祈祷
我们的主啊，祢吩咐我们，凡手所当做的事，都当全心去做；求祢恩允我们这样降服自己、献归于祢——身体、心思与心灵——使我们甚至在每日寻常的事务中也能事奉祢，荣耀祢的大名。阿们。

## XV Gashmu the Gossip / XV 传闲话的基善

===Original===
> “Gashmu saith it.”
> — Nehemiah 6:6

===Chinese===
> 「是基善说的。」
> — Nehemiah 6:6

===Original===
Gashmu is a mere name in Scripture. He is mentioned only three times—twice as acting with Sanballat against Nehemiah, and once as the authority for a false piece of news. It is reported, wrote Sanballat in a cruel letter to Nehemiah, that you are plotting against the king, and “Gashmu saith it.” That is what Gashmu stands for in Scripture, a talebearer, a slanderer, a gossip. What an unenviable immortality to be remembered only as the pedlar of a tale he knew to be untrue!

As long as we live together in society, there will be a kind of gossip that is inevitable, the kindly or merely casual relation of small and insignificant matters of fact, as that the painters are in next door, or that Mrs. So-and-So has got a new bonnet. It is not of that I want to speak.

===Chinese===
基善（Gashmu）在圣经里不过是一个名字。他只被提到过三次——两次是与参巴拉一同行动、敌对尼希米，一次是作为一则假消息的出处。参巴拉在一封写给尼希米的恶毒信中如此说：有风声传讲，你正图谋背叛王，「是基善说的。」这就是基善在圣经里所代表的一切：一个搬弄是非的人，一个造谣毁谤的人，一个说闲话的人。只凭着传布一条自己明知不实的流言而留名后世，这是何等不值得羡慕的不朽！

只要我们还群居共处于社会之中，就总有一种闲话是无可避免的：那便是善意地、或仅仅出于随口地把一些琐碎而无关紧要的事实转述出来——譬如说隔壁住进了油漆匠，或者某某太太添了一顶新帽子。我要说的并不是这种。

===Original===
For there is another sort as deadly as the plague, and in civilised countries the cruellest and most devilish instrument that one man or woman can use against another. And that is the inventing of an untrue report about a man’s doings or character, or the unthinking repetition of the same. That is the pestilence that walketh in darkness; that is the destruction that wasteth at noonday. And I wish I had the pen to write of it as it deserves.

It is very, very common. We are all too ready to repeat what we have heard, with a “Gashmu saith it,” as if that certified the tale correct. And the harm done is simply incalculable. If my house is burned or I lose my money, I can still get along by the kindness of my friends for a little, till I find my feet again. But whoever by some lying story takes away my character, deals me a blow from which there is no recovering, which my loyalest friends can do nothing to avert. I have no redress, no compensation, and no help. Anyone may be a victim, and you and I, by thoughtlessly passing on the deadly thing, may all unconsciously be driving another nail into a man’s coffin.

===Chinese===
因为另有一种闲话，其致命犹如瘟疫，而在文明国家里，它是一个男人或女人能用来对付另一个人的最残忍、最恶毒的器械。那就是编造关于某人的言行或品格的不实传闻，或者不假思索地把这传闻照样传播。这就是那黑夜行的瘟疫；这就是那午间灭人的毒病。但愿我有那样一支笔，能把它当受的评判写个透彻！

这种事非常、非常普遍。我们都太急于把听见的话重述出去，还要加上一句「是基善说的」，仿佛这就为传闻的确凿作了保证。而它所造成的损害简直无法估量。倘若我的房子烧毁了，或者我丧失了钱财，我还能靠朋友们的善待支撑一时，直到重新站稳脚跟。但凡有人用一段捏造的谎话夺走我的名誉，那就是给了我一个无法复原的打击，连我最忠心的朋友也无从替我挡开。我无处申诉，无从补偿，也无人援助。任何人都可能成为受害者；而你和我，只消不加思索地把这致命的东西传递下去，就可能全然不觉地又往一个人的棺材上钉进一颗钉子。

===Original===
Did you ever lie awake at night and think that even now the cancer may have begun on *your* good name, that whispers may be going about among your friends concerning you? Those who know you will hear it, and will say, It’s a lie! But that won’t stop it. And you will never know till some day you waken up and find that your reputation is in danger. And not one word or vestige of truth may be in it. It may be a lie pure and simple, or a colourable counterfeit of some quite innocent truth. That won’t make any difference. It is enough merely to start it, and, like a stone thrown down an Alpine slope, it gathers others in its train, till an avalanche swoops down on some unsuspecting head.

===Chinese===
你可曾在夜里辗转难眠，想到此刻那个毒瘤也许已经在*你的*好名声上发作，也许朋友们中间正有耳语在围绕着你流传？认识你的人会听见，会说：这是谎话！可那并不能叫它止住。你也决不会知道，直到某一天你猛然醒觉，发觉自己的名声已危在旦夕。而其中也许连一个字、一丝一毫的真实都没有。它可能是一句彻头彻尾的谎言，也可能是把某件全然清白的事实涂饰改扮成的赝品。但这没有什么分别。只要把它一挑动起来就够了：它像一块从阿尔卑斯山坡上抛下的石头，一路裹挟别的东西同行，直到一场雪崩轰然扑落，罩住某个毫无防备的头顶。

===Original===
When King Arthur enrolled his Knights of the Round Table, he made them take the oath to “speak no slander.” And there is a knightly chivalry of speech which ought to be the mark of all those who have promised fealty to Jesus Christ. Our discipleship of Jesus demands of us the high endeavour to love our neighbour as ourselves, and that presupposes, as one of its consequences, that we guard his name against false witness as carefully as we protect our own. If we hear a good story about someone, a report that is to his credit and honour, let us blazon that abroad. We are all far too slow at that, and somehow the tale that is a little damaging has a far easier and more rapid circulation. Might we not make more of our brother’s successes? Might we not oftener repeat about him what he is too modest ever to say about himself? It were a true and kindly Christian act. But never, as we call ourselves servants of Christ, never do our brother such a grievous irreparable wrong as to start about him a tale which may not be true. God can and will forgive you your sins of speech. But even He cannot make clean the character which a foolish word has sullied.

King Arthur went further, however, than demanding that his knights should speak no slander. Their vow included the words, “no, nor listen to it.” And that is a high and difficult course to keep. It is not easy, when you are being told of something that is striking or sensational of a merely gossipy character, to stop the conversation and lead it into other channels. It requires great courage and as great tact. But how many of us ever try it?

===Chinese===
亚瑟王册封他的圆桌骑士时，叫他们立下誓言：「不进谗言。」而言语上也当有一种骑士的侠义，作一切已向耶稣基督宣誓效忠之人的标记。作耶稣的门徒，要求我们努力达到那崇高的目标——爱人如己；而这目标所预设的一个结果，便是我们看守他人的名誉、使它不受假见证的侵害，如同保护自己的名誉一样谨慎。我们若听见关于某人的好消息，一件于他的信誉与尊荣有益的事，就让我们把它广为传扬吧。我们在这事上都太迟缓了，而那略微有些损人的闲话，不知怎的却流传得远更容易、更迅速。我们岂不更当多传讲我们弟兄的成功么？岂不更当多重复那些他因过于谦逊而从不肯论自己的好处么？这才是一个真实而仁厚的基督徒的作为。但我们既自称是基督的仆人，就断不可对我们弟兄行这样沉重而无可挽回的冤屈，去挑起一则关于他、却未必真实的传闻。上帝能够、也必会赦免你言语上的罪。但即便是他，也无法洗净被一句愚妄的话所玷污的品格。

然而，亚瑟王的要求比叫他的骑士不进谗言更进了一步。他们的誓言里还包括这样的话：「不但不进，也不听谗言。」那是一条崇高而艰难的守则。当有人正向你讲述一件耸人听闻、却不过是流言蜚语的事情时，要叫谈话停下来，把它引到别的轨道上去，实在不容易。这需要极大的勇气，也需要同样大的机敏。但我们中间有几个人试过呢？

===Original===
If, however, the refusal to listen be regarded as a counsel of perfection, there remains yet the further injunction—never *repeat* the gossip you have heard. That at least is homely and possible.

We used to read in our book of fables of the lamb that noticed this significant thing about the track that led to the lion’s den—that all the footprints pointed inwards, but there were none returning. “*Vestigia nulla retrorsum.*” No footprints backwards. It would be a good motto for us all. Let the stories, the ill-humoured, unkind, uncharitable sayings that float and wander about everywhere, let them come to us as they will, but let the traces end there. Be such a person that men may trace a story from its source down the chain *to* you, but never *past* you.

===Chinese===
不过，倘若拒绝聆听被视为一种高不可攀的完全标准，那么还有一条更进一步的命令——永不*转述*你所听来的闲话。这一条至少是平实而可行的。

我们从前都在寓言书里读到过那只羔羊，它留意到通向狮子洞穴的路径上有一件意味深长的事——所有的脚印都朝里，却没有一个回头。「Vestigia nulla retrorsum（来路无痕）」——没有回头的脚印。这倒可以作我们大家的座右铭。让那些四下飘荡游移的故事，那些恶声恶气、刻薄寡恩、不存怜恤的言语，随它们的便来到我们这里吧；但让它们的踪迹到此为止。要作这样一种人：人们可以顺着一条流言的链条，从源头一路追溯到*你*这里，却永远追溯不过*你*去。

===Original===
We can do that much at least for our friends. All about us is the constant, unquiet drift of gossip and distorted half-truth, as restless as the sand in the desert, dancing and whirling with every puff of wind. We can do something to arrest that drift. We can be for our friends in some measure what Isaiah said that God’s Servant, when He came, should be, the shadow of a great Rock in a weary land, stopping the drift of the sand, and sheltering our friends by our loyalty and our silence.

Don’t even repeat the gossip that comes to you, not only for the strong reason already given, but also for this little one, that you won’t likely repeat it correctly. With all the will in the world, it is one of the hardest things to retail a story just exactly as you heard it. Sir Walter Scott, speaking about anecdotes that he had heard, said he always liked to cock up their bonnets a bit and put a staff in their hands that they might walk on a little brisker and sprightlier than when they came to him! But we all do that, without meaning to do it at all. We add a little bit. We exaggerate just the tiniest fraction, and our hearer when he repeats the story does the same, and so the matter grows till it is big enough to do much mischief.

===Chinese===
至少这一点，我们总能替朋友做到。我们四围尽是闲言碎语与变了形的半真之事那种不停歇、不安定的流荡，像沙漠里的沙一样永不安分，随着每一阵风起舞盘旋。我们总可以尽一分力，去拦阻这流荡。我们多少可以替我们的朋友，作以赛亚所说上帝的仆人他来的时候所当是的——疲乏之地上的大磐石的影子，拦住流沙的漂移，用我们的忠诚与我们的静默荫庇我们的朋友。

甚至对你听来的闲话也不要转述；这不仅是为了前面已经说过的那个重大的理由，也是为着这个小缘故：你多半转述得不会准确。纵然你存心再诚恳，要把一个故事一字不差地照你听见的原样复述出去，也是世上最难的事情之一。沃尔特·司各特爵士（Sir Walter Scott）谈到自己听来的轶闻时说，他总喜欢把它们的帽子往上翘一翘，往它们手里放一根手杖，好叫它们走得比来到他跟前时更轻快、更精神些！可我们人人都这样干，而且全然出于无意。我们添上一点点，夸大那么一丝半毫；我们的听者再把故事往下传时又如法炮制，于是事情越滚越大，大到足以造成许多损害。

===Original===
> “A Whisper broke the air,
> A soft light tone and low,
> Yet barbed with shame and woe.
> Now, might it only perish there,
> Nor further go!
> Ah me! A quick and eager ear
> Caught up the little meaning sound;
> Another voice has breathed it clear,
> And so it wandered round,
> From ear to lip, from lip to ear,
> Until it reached a gentle heart,
> And that—it broke.”

===Chinese===
> 一阵耳语划破了空气，
> 声调轻柔、低微，
> 却倒钩着羞辱与哀恸。
> 但愿它只在那里止息，
> 不再远行！
> 唉！一只急切而敏捷的耳朵
> 捕住了那轻微而意味深长的声响；
> 另一个声音又把它清晰地吐露，
> 于是它四处游荡，
> 从耳朵到嘴唇，从嘴唇到耳朵，
> 直到它触到一个温柔的心，
> 而那心——碎了。

===Original===
There is a legend that once a king avoided death in a poisoned cup that had been handed to him by making over it the sign of the Cross—when it broke in pieces at his feet. Let us, when we are tempted to retail the vivid, poisonous piece of scandal, stop and invoke the Spirit of Christ. Is this that I am going to say about my brother the kind of thing I should say if Christ were standing by? Am I justified in turning over that bit of gossip which may be true, but which ought not to be true? Our duty, who profess and call ourselves Christians, is clear. We are to speak no slander no, nor listen to it. We are to retail evil about no man. We are to love one another.

===Chinese===
传说从前有一位王，人递给他一杯下了毒的酒，他在杯上画了十字的记号，竟免于一死——那杯就在他脚前碎成片片。当我们受试探要去传扬那生动而恶毒的丑闻时，但愿我们也停下来，求告基督的灵。我即将说出的关于我弟兄的这话，倘若基督就站在旁边，我还是会说的么？那点闲话，它也许是真的，却是不该为真的，我把它传递下去，能算为有理么？我们这些宣认并自称基督徒的人，责任是清楚的：我们不进谗言，也不听谗言；我们不说任何人的坏话；我们要彼此相爱。

===Original===
Prayer
O Lord our God, whose command it is that we love our neighbour as ourselves, help us to cherish and protect his good name as carefully as we guard our own. Make us more willing to repeat the good about him, but slower to retail or exaggerate the evil. Grant us all a deeper sense of the deadly wrong a foolish tongue can work, and keep Thou the door of our lips. For Thy Name’s sake. Amen.

===Chinese===
祈祷
主我们的神啊，你的命令是要我们爱人如己，求你帮助我们珍爱并护卫邻舍的美名，如同看守自己的美名一样仔细。求你使我们更乐于重述他的好处，却更迟缓于传播或夸大他的坏处。求你使我们众人更深地感到一条愚妄的舌头能行出何等致命的冤害，并求你亲自把守我们嘴唇的门。为你名的缘故求。阿们。

## XVI God in Front / XVI 上帝行在前头

===Original===
> “Thou preventest him with the blessings of goodness.”
> — Psalm 21:3

===Chinese===
> 「你以美福迎接他。」
> — Psalm 21:3

===Original===
You know how, in a happy home, the near approach of a birthday is signalised, how parcels are mysteriously smuggled in and hidden in secret places, and, though everything seems to be going on as usual, yet the plans are being laid in train that will surprise and delight the fortunate owner of the birthday when the festal day dawns. That is our feeble, human way of trying to surprise one another with the blessings of goodness. That is how we “prevent” our beloved with tokens of our remembrance. So, says the Psalmist, does God deal with us. Not only have we—what we so much need—His forgiveness of our past, and His help and presence for the day which now is; He is working for us in the future too, sowing the days to come with blessings for us to pick up when the passage of time brings us to the places where He has hidden them.

The idea that God has been beforehand in our history, getting ready, as it were, for our coming, though not a very usual one, is very helpful, and it finds abundant illustration and proof in all directions. When a child arrives on this earth, he enters into the enjoyment of bounties and blessings prepared, not merely weeks, but literally ages before his coming. Warmth he needs, and aeons ago the coal beds were formed in the bowels of the earth. Food he needs, and God “laboured for ages,” as Sir Oliver Lodge puts it, to bring corn into existence. For corn needs soil, and, to make that, the Creator had to set the glaciers grinding over the granite, and to loosen the forces of rain and frost and running water over great stretches of time.

===Chinese===
你知道，在一个欢乐的家庭里，生日的临近是怎样被郑重张罗的：包裹神神秘秘地被捎进家门，藏进隐秘的角落；虽然一切看上去都如常进行，暗地里却已布好一连串安排，只等那喜庆的日子一朝破晓，便叫那位幸运的寿星又惊又喜。这就是我们这微弱的人，试图用美福彼此制造惊喜的方式。我们就是这样，以记念的表记「赶在前头迎接」我们所爱的人。诗篇的作者说，上帝待我们也是这样。我们不但得着——这正是我们极需要的东西——祂赦免我们的过往，又在这现今的一日赐下帮助与同在；祂还在未来为我们经营，把美福播种在将来的岁月里，待时间的流逝把我们带到祂藏放美福的地方，我们便可一一拾起。

上帝在我们一生的历史中行在先头、仿佛早已为我们的来到预备妥当——这个想法虽然不常见，却大有益处，而且在四面八方都有丰富的例证与凭据。一个孩子来到世上，便进入了种种丰盛宴享的享受之中，而这些预备，不只是在几个星期之前，实在是在许多世代之前就已成就。他需要温暖，而亿万年之前，煤层已在地的深处凝成。他需要食物，而上帝——照奥利弗·洛奇爵士（Sir Oliver Lodge）的说法——「劳作了亿万年」，才使谷物得以存有。因为谷物需要土壤；为造出土壤，创造者还得驱使冰川在花岗岩上碾磨，又在漫长的岁月里松开雨水、霜冻与流水的力量。

===Original===
Every child born into the world becomes the heir of all the ages past. What blessings have been prepared for most of us, in advance, in the homes into which we were born, and the gracious influences under which we have grown up! “I have to thank the gods,” says Marcus Aurelius the pagan emperor, “that my grandfathers, parents, sisters, preceptors, relations, friends and domestics were almost all of them persons of probity.” “I have to thank the gods.” Who else is there to thank but God who prevents us in this way with the blessings of goodness? God is working beforehand in our interest in all these things. So, when we awaken to a sense of Him, there is His Church, established of old, awaiting to take us by the hand and help us on our way. When we learn our need of a Saviour, behold Christ stands at the door and knocks. When, in penitence of heart, we ask God’s mercy, we learn that, long since, it was laid up in store for us. Before we thought of loving God, He first loved us, and gave Himself for us in Jesus Christ our Lord. Is it not gloriously true all the way along that God has been beforehand with His goodness?

And that, of course, is the explanation of all the glad surprises of life. The Lord has prepared them for us beforehand. He has sown the future with good things and watched our surprise as we picked them up. When Mary Mardon and her father, in Mark Rutherford’s *Autobiography*, went to the seaside to look for lodgings they saw a dismal row of very plain-looking houses. Mary objected instinctively to the dull street, but her father said he could not afford to pay for a sea view, so they went in to inquire. To their delight they found that what they thought were the fronts of the houses were really the backs, for the real fronts faced the bay, had pretty gardens before the doors, and a glorious sunny prospect over the ocean. Isn’t that what we often find to be the case? Our most treasured friends are not always those whom we fall in love with at first sight. The thing we greatly fear dissolves like mist. An envied, but despaired-of, blessing is flung into our lap. A door of splendid hope opens in a dead wall. Life is full of the unexpected as if wonder were one of the things God wanted very much to keep alive in us. When, as you think, everything has been exhausted, God surprises you with a fresh gladness. And, aback of all, there is the unending surprise of God’s patience with us, and of that daily mercy of His, which we so ill requite, and so often forget.

===Chinese===
凡生在世上的孩子，都成了以往诸时代的继承者。为我们大多数人，早在我们出生之前，上帝已在所生的家中、在我们长大所受的美善熏陶里，预备了何等的恩福！「我要感谢诸神，」异教皇帝马可·奥勒留（Marcus Aurelius）说，「感谢我的祖辈、父母、姊妹、师长、亲族、朋友与家中仆役，几乎个个都是正直之人。」「我要感谢诸神。」除了那位以美福这样先行迎接我们的上帝，还能感谢谁呢？在这一切事上，上帝都提前为我们经营。因此，当我们醒觉到祂的时候，祂的教会早已设立，正等候牵我们的手，扶助我们上路。当我们明白自己需要一位救主，看哪，基督正站在门口叩门。当我们心里痛悔，求上帝的怜悯，我们便知道，那怜悯早已为我们积存多时。在我们想到爱上帝之先，祂先爱了我们，并在我们主耶稣基督里为我们舍了自己。上帝的美福一路都行在我们前头，这岂不是满有荣耀的真实么？

自然，这正是人生一切欢乐惊喜的缘由。是主预先为我们备下了它们。祂把美善之物播种在未来里，又看着我们拾起时的那份惊讶。在马克·拉瑟福德（Mark Rutherford）的《自传》里，玛丽·马登（Mary Mardon）和她的父亲到海滨寻租住处，望见一排阴沉沉的、模样十分朴陋的房子。玛丽本能地嫌恶这条沉闷的街道，她父亲却说，他出不起海景的钱，于是他们进去询问。叫他们欢喜的是，他们原以为是房子正面的，其实竟是背面；真正的正面朝着海湾，门前有秀美的小花园，越过海面展开一片灿烂明媚的景色。我们岂不是常常发现事情正是这样？我们最珍爱的朋友，未必是我们一见倾心的那些。我们深深惧怕的事，像雾一样消散了。一件人人艳羡、我们却已绝望的福分，忽然落在我们怀中。一扇辉煌的希望之门，在一堵死墙上开了出来。人生充满不期而至之事，仿佛惊奇正是上帝巴不得在我们里面长存不灭的东西之一。当你以为一切都已穷尽，上帝却以新的喜乐叫你惊喜。而在这一切背后，还有那无有穷尽的惊奇：上帝对我们的忍耐，以及祂那日日的怜悯——我们报答得这样薄凉，又这样常常忘却。

===Original===
Of course, no one dreams of suggesting that all our surprises are of a happy sort. It is not so. But the point is that if it is God who has hidden the blessings for us to come upon, it is He also who has hidden the other things. God’s hand does not slip so that we get the wrong parcel by accident. He prevents us also with the blessings that we do not call by that name at all. In his *Lay Sermons*, Huxley, describing the tadpole in its slimy cradle, says: “After watching the process hour after hour, one is almost possessed by the notion that some more subtle aid to vision than an achromatic object-glass would show the hidden artist with his plan before him, striving with skilful manipulation to perfect his work.” If, in that wonderful fashion, God is working beforehand according to a plan of His own, in the life of a tadpole, is it not much more likely that He is so working in your life and mine, not in its joys only, but also in its dark hours and its sorrows? That, indeed, is the very message and comfort of the Lord Jesus Christ, that not even a sparrow falleth to the ground—calamity indeed for the sparrow—without our Father.

If it be true that God our Father is working in advance of us all the time, then surely it is wrong to speak of the monotony of life? For we are on a road which God Himself has sown with surprises for us, and the hour of our deadliest weariness may be the immediate precursor of our richest and most joyous find. Who could have supposed, at the end of the eighteenth century, when poetry in England seemed dead, that a great galaxy of stars—Wordsworth, Coleridge, Byron, Shelley, Keats—was on the very eve of rising? The unexpected can always happen. You may come upon another of God’s hidden blessings tomorrow. Let us not talk of monotony, therefore, in an age which has seen so many wonderful things happen. Rather let us hold to the faith that all the while God is going before us with the blessings of goodness.

===Chinese===
当然，谁也不会妄想说，我们一切的惊喜都属于欢乐一类。事实并非如此。但要点在于：若把美福藏起来、等我们撞见的是上帝，那么把其余诸事藏起来的，也是祂。上帝的手不会失滑，以致我们偶然拿错了包裹。祂也以那些我们根本不称作美福的「美福」先行迎接我们。赫胥黎（T. H. Huxley）在《俗世讲道集》（*Lay Sermons*）里描写躺在黏滑摇篮中的蝌蚪时说：「一小时又一小时地观看这过程之后，人几乎被一个念头攫住：倘若有比消色差物镜更精微的助视之器，便会看见那位隐身的艺术家带着眼前的图样，以娴熟的手法奋力使他的作品臻于完全。」上帝若以这般奇妙的方式，按着祂自己的蓝图，预先在一只蝌蚪的生命中做工，那么祂在你我的生命中——不但在欢乐里，也在幽暗的时辰与忧愁中——也同样如此做工，岂非更是顺理成章？这实在正是主耶稣基督的信息与安慰：若非我们的父许可，一只麻雀也不会掉在地上——对麻雀而言，那固然是灾殃。

若上帝我们的天父真是一直行在我们前头做工，那么，说人生单调乏味，岂不是错了么？因为我们正走在一条路上，是上帝亲自播满了惊喜的；我们最深重的疲乏之时，或许正是我们最丰盈、最欢乐之发现的前奏。十八世纪之末，英格兰的诗坛看似已死，谁能想得到，一大片璀璨的星群——华兹华斯、柯勒律治、拜伦、雪莱、济慈——恰在冉冉升起的前夜？意料之外的事随时可能发生。明天，你就可能撞见上帝另一件藏好的美福。所以，在这个见过如此多奇妙之事的时代，让我们不要再说单调。倒要持守这信仰：上帝始终以美福行在我们前头。

===Original===
This faith puts another complexion on all our fears and forebodings. Before we live it, the web of our life passes through God’s hands. And the shaded parts, as well as the bright parts, are in His wise and loving design. Nobody can promise us freedom from sorrow, but the Bible promises that God is beforehand to make the sorrow bearable. He has adjusted our temptations to our strength, and never a one has He hidden, where we come upon it, that it is impossible for us by His help to withstand. Before the mother puts her little child into his hot bath at night, she tests the water first with her fingers. And the Psalmist means us to believe that life comes to us from God, who has measured and adapted it for us, beforehand, in a like fashion.

Viewed in the light of this faith, Death itself takes on a different aspect. Oliver Wendell Holmes has suggested that the story of this life and the next can be fully written in two strokes of the pen, an interrogation-point, and, above it, a mark of exclamation—fear and question here below, and, above, adoration, wonder, surprise. “I go to prepare a place for you,” said Christ to His disciples. If the preparation for us here is so wonderful, is it likely to fail yonder? If Love made ready for us here, shall it not be beforehand there too? Yea, verily. Our experience of how God prevents us here with His loving kindness ought to strengthen in us all the “faith of our Lord Jesus Christ, and the saint’s trust in every age, that when we pass hence it will be to meet the grandest, the most blessed, and the most surprising provision of all.”

===Chinese===
这信仰使我们一切的惧怕与不祥的预感换了颜色。我们生命的织物，在尚未供我们活过之先，已先经过上帝的手。那些着了暗色的部分，和明亮的部分一样，都在祂智慧而慈爱的设计里。没有人能应许我们免于忧愁，但圣经应许说，上帝早已在先，要使忧愁成为可以担当的。祂早已按我们的力量调准了我们的试探；祂藏下的试炼，没有一件——无论我们在哪里撞见它——是靠祂的帮助仍无法抵挡的。夜里，母亲把小孩子放进热热的浴水之前，总先用手指试一试水温。诗篇的作者要我们相信，生命也正是这样来到我们手中：它从上帝而来，祂早已按同样的方式为我们量度、调配妥当。

以这信仰的光照来观看，连死亡也换了面目。奥利弗·温德尔·霍姆斯（Oliver Wendell Holmes）曾提示说，今生与来世的故事，用笔两划便可写尽：一个问号，其上一个惊叹号——此在下是惧怕与疑问，其上是敬拜、惊奇、诧异。「我去原是为你们预备地方去。」基督对祂的门徒说。祂在这里为我们所行的预备既已如此奇妙，在那里岂会落空？爱既在这里为我们备妥一切，在那里岂不更是行在前头？诚然如此。上帝在这里以祂的慈爱先行迎接我们的经历，理当在我们众人里面坚固那「我们主耶稣基督的信心」，以及历代圣徒的信赖：我们离开此地之时，迎见我们的，将是那最恢宏、最有福、也最出人意外的预备。

===Original===
Prayer
Our Father in Heaven, we shall not be afraid of what life may hold for us when we have learned that our little web has first passed through Thy merciful and loving hands. We have often prayed that Thou wouldest go with us; but Thou hast answered us beyond our asking, for Thou goest before us all. In the faith of that leading, make us to journey bravely and to sleep secure. Amen.

===Chinese===
祈祷
我们在天上的父啊，当我们得知我们这小小的织物先已经过祢怜悯慈爱的手，我们便不再惧怕人生可能为我们存着什么。我们常常祷告，求祢与我们同去；祢的回应却越过了我们所求，因为祢在我们众人的前头行。凭这引领之信，求祢使我们勇敢赶路，安然入睡。阿们。

## XVII “Unbelief Kept Quiet” / XVII 「按捺住的不信」

===Original===
> “Fight the good fight of faith.”
> — 1 Timothy 6:12

===Chinese===
> 「你要为真道打那美好的仗。」
> — 1 Timothy 6:12

===Original===
We are often told that this is not an age of faith, that the day of the beautiful, old, simple acquiescence is past, whether it ever comes again or not. Someone has wittily suggested that the coat of arms of the present age is “an interrogation-point rampant, above three bishops dormant, and the motto ‘Query.’” But, like a great many more witty things, that saying leaves one questioning whether, after all, it be really true. I venture, for my part, to assert that a great many more people are really interested in this matter of faith than most of us imagine. There is something that haunts men as with a sense of hidden treasure about this wonderful thing in life called Faith, that always seems to be going to disappear, and yet somehow does not. With a strange, wistful persistence men linger about this pool, though there are many to tell them that the “desired angel bathes no more.”

===Chinese===
人们常对我们说，这不是一个信仰的时代，那种古老而美好、单纯信顺的日子已成过去，无论它还会不会再来。有人妙语打趣说，本时代的纹章是「一头跃立而起的问号，下面卧伏着三位主教，铭辞则是『存疑』」。然而，正如许多别的妙语一样，这句话反倒叫人疑心起来：它究竟是不是真的。就我而言，我斗胆断言：对信仰这件事真正挂心的人，远比我们大多数人所想象的为多。人生中那称为信仰的奇妙之物，总带着一种萦绕人心、仿佛藏着秘宝的意味；它似乎随时都要消失，却不知怎的并没有消失。人们怀着一种奇异的、怅然不舍的执念，在这池边流连，尽管有许多人告诉他们，「所渴慕的天使已不再下到池中」。

===Original===
I wish to speak a word of encouragement today to all who are finding faith hard. “Fight the good fight of faith,” says Paul to his young friend, Timothy. Fight. I want to remind you that faith often implies effort, that there is nothing in the idea of faith which is incompatible with struggle, that the very form of Paul’s advice implies an antagonism.

It is true that many think of the “faith of the saints” as a quiet, contented habit of gentle acquiescence, a sweet and beautiful state of mind very far removed from the restless, questioning, analytic temper of the man of today. Now, I do not say that faith is never seen now in that placid form, but I do say that that was not the type Paul had in mind when he wrote Timothy, it is not the figure which best described his own faith, and it is certainly not the aspect he would require to deal with, were he writing to the men of today.

===Chinese===
今天，我愿向一切觉得信仰艰难的人，说一句鼓励的话。「你要为真道打那美好的仗」，保罗对他的年轻朋友提摩太如此说。打仗。我要提醒你们：信仰往往意味着努力；信仰这个观念里，没有一样东西与挣扎不相容；保罗这句劝勉的措辞本身，就预示着一场对抗。

诚然，许多人把「圣徒的信仰」想成一种安静而知足的习惯、一种温顺的依从，一种甜美美好的心境，与今日之人那躁动、好问、事事分析的心性相去甚远。如今，我并不是说信仰从不以那种平和的形态出现；但我要说，保罗写信给提摩太时，心中所想的并不是那个类型；那也不是最能描摹他自己信仰的形象；倘若他今日写信给这个时代的人，他所要应付的，断然不是那样一副光景。

===Original===
For they are only too conscious of much inward suspense of judgment and uncertainty concerning many things in Heaven and earth. And that inward conflict seems to many of them a sign that faith is waning, if not dead. They have forgotten that it is that very sense of inward conflict which proves that faith is not dead. Dead things do not offer any resistance. We ought by this time to have learned that a thing “may be for us an intellectual puzzle, and yet a sheer spiritual necessity,” and that the Christian faith is, for every soul who has once caught it. There are a great many earnest and honest men to whom it is the best of news that Christian faith is not incompatible with very grave perplexities. The real opposite of faith is not doubt, as so many suppose, but deliberate and satisfied denial. Faith can live in the same life along with very many doubts—as a matter of fact, in the case of not a few of the most Christlike men of our time, it is living beside them constantly. Paul assures us that outside of him he found fightings and within him he found fears. Yet he kept the faith for all that. They start up on all sides, these spectres of the mind and reason, and they ask questions which a man cannot answer. Yet Faith may be dwelling in his life in very deed and truth, because faith is something more than the sum of all his beliefs. It is the whole conscious and deliberate set and desire of his being.

===Chinese===
因为今日之人对于天上地下的许多事，太深切地感到内心判断的悬而未决与不能确定。而这种内心的冲突，在许多人看来，似乎是信仰衰微——若非已死——的征兆。他们忘了，恰恰是这种内心冲突之感，证明信仰并没有死。死物是不会抵抗的。到如今，我们本该学会：一件事「对我们可以是一道理智的难题，却又是一种全然属灵的必需」；而基督教信仰，对于每一个一旦把它抓住的灵魂，正是如此。对许多恳切诚实的人而言，基督教信仰并不与极深重的困惑不相容——这是再好不过的消息。信仰真正的对立面，并不像许多人所以为的是怀疑，而是存心的、自以为满足的否认。信仰能与极多的怀疑活在同一条生命里——事实上，在我们这个时代那些最像基督的人中间，不少人正是日日夜夜与怀疑比邻而居。保罗告诉我们，他「外有争战，内有惧怕」。尽管如此，他依然守住了所信的道。这些心思与理性的幽灵从四面八方冒出来，问出人无法回答的问题。然而信仰仍可以实实在在地住在他生命里；因为信仰并不止是他一切信条的总和。它是他全人那自觉的、经过思虑的定志与渴慕。

===Original===
It is a well-known fact that a man may be truly courageous, acting, speaking, thinking bravely at the very moment when panic fears are gripping his heart. I like that fine old story of the soldier advancing into the fire zone with steady step, and taunted by a comrade for his pale face. “You’re afraid,” said the other. “I know I am afraid,” said he, “and if you felt half as much afraid as I do, you would turn and flee.” It is the very finest courage that dominates and controls a sensitive organisation, and holds the shrinking other-half to its purpose with firm grip. Just so is it with faith. A man keeps his course, lifts up his eyes to the hills, lives for God and His Christ, prays on, struggles on, and hopes for the home beyond the edge of life, while often enough his mind is full of questioning and the puzzle of God’s deep mysteries. For faith is not what the intellect says merely. It is what the whole man is struggling and trying to say.

===Chinese===
众所周知，一个人可以是真正勇敢的：当惊惶的恐惧正攫住他的心，他的行动、言语、思想却依然带着胆气。我喜欢那个古老的好故事：一名士兵迈着沉稳的步伐开进火线，却因脸色苍白遭同伴讥笑。「你害怕了，」那同伴说。「我知道我害怕，」他说，「你若觉得的害怕有我一半那么多，早就转身逃了。」最高等的勇气，正是那主宰并节制一个敏感肌体的勇气，以坚定的持握，把那畏缩的另一半按在其目标上。信仰正是如此。一个人守定他的航程，向山举目，为上帝和祂的基督而活，祷告不辍，挣扎不止，盼望那在生命边际之外的家；与此同时，他的心思却往往满了疑问，满了上帝深奥奥秘的谜团。因为信仰并不只是理智所宣告的东西；它是整个的人挣扎着、努力要说出的那句话。

===Original===
> “With me, faith means perpetual unbelief
> Kept quiet, like the snake ’neath Michael’s foot,
> Who stands calm just because he feels it writhe.”

===Chinese===
> 「在我，信仰意味着永远都在的不信
> 被按捺得不动声色，像米迦勒脚下的蛇——
> 他之所以站立得镇定，正因他觉出它在扭动。」

===Original===
Don’t do yourself the wrong of thinking that faith has vanished because the snake is felt to be writhing. “Perpetual unbelief kept quiet.” Yes, but what keeps the clamouring doubts and fears under foot? Just yourself, just your highest self, the bit of you made for God, and unable to do without Him! Faith is the vote of the whole man, of the best of the man, in the face of a protesting minority. In other words, fight is a splendid word to use in speaking about faith.

Let a man ask himself—Does he really wish that the best he has dreamed or heard about God and His love for men, His passion to deliver them from evil, and His pity and nearness to us all in Jesus Christ His Son—does he wish all that to be true? No man is without faith who does wish that, and is living in the direction of his desire. In that man’s life who, despite all the clamour and philosophy of Babylon, is keeping his window open towards where he believes Jerusalem to be, there is that vital element of faith that is linking his life to God even now, and will bring him where he would be at last.

===Chinese===
不要错待自己，以为因为觉出蛇在蠕动，信仰就已烟消云散。「永远都在的不信，被按捺得不动声色。」是的；可是，是什么把那喧嚣鼓噪的疑惑与惧怕按在脚下？正是你自己，正是你那最高的自我，正是你身上那为上帝而造、离了祂便活不成的那一部分！信仰乃是全人的投票，是人身上最美好部分的投票，是不顾一个抗议的少数派而投下的一票。换句话说，论到信仰，「打仗」是个再好不过的字眼。
