And it may be that obedience to that involves seeming disloyalty to your friends, either for a while or permanently.

Such a time came to Paul. He had for conscience’ sake to stand alone; and he did it. He was able to do it because his life did not rest for its ultimate pillar on his friendships any more than on his riches. Paul’s real life was within. That inner life of his was enriched and made radiant and constant by one supreme fact—he believed that Jesus Christ his Lord deigned to share it with him in spirit. It is not irreverent to say that in his inner soul Paul lived with Christ.

Maybe his words are too big for us to use, but each of us who, at some hard bit of our journey, has appealed beyond friends to the Christ within, saying, “I have done, O Lord, what seemed to me right. And my friends are hurt and angry. But Thou knowest”—that man has learned, even in a slight degree, that there is a nearer and truer blessing possible for sinful men than even human friendship.

===Chinese===
再拿友谊的问题来说。谁能用恰当的言语说尽，有一位真实而忠诚的朋友意味着什么？然而有时竟会发生这样的事：最亲密的友谊破裂了，人不得不独自站立——不是出于他自己的拣选，而是出于世事冷酷的安排。有一项义务，比你对朋友守信更高。首先，最要紧的，你必须对自己守信，对自己的良心守信，对里面的声音守信。而顺服这声音，或许就意味着对朋友显为不忠——或是一时，或是永久。

这样的时刻，保罗也临到过。为了良心的缘故，他必须独自站立；他这样行了。他之所以能做到，是因为他的生命终极的柱石，既不立于他的友谊，也不立于他的财富。保罗真实的生命在里面。他的内在生命因一件至高的事实而得丰盛、放光辉、得恒固——他相信他的主耶稣基督屈尊俯就，在灵里与他共享这生命。我们可以说，保罗在他的内在灵魂里是与基督同活的；这样说不算不敬。

或许他这样的措辞对我们来说太高，学用不来；但我们中间每一个人，若曾在旅程某段难行的路上，越过朋友而向里面的基督呼求，说：「主啊，我看为是对的，我已经行了。我的朋友们受了伤，也动了怒。但祢知道」——那人就已经学到，纵然只是一点：对罪人而言，还有一种比人间友谊更切近、更真实的福分，是可能得到的。

===Original===
Then there is another thing that has sometimes to be done without. There are privileges that belong to every Christian man and woman, and are in a sense their birthright—the sense of God, confidence, quietness of heart, hope. There is no doubt that every real Christian should be walking and working in the light and gladness of God’s presence.

But it is just as clear that not all are so blessed. It may be their own fault. Doubtless in many cases it is. Or it may be temperament or outward circumstances that determine it. Anyhow, many have to walk, not in the light but in uncertainty, perplexity, and misgiving, and sometimes even in darkness.

But “a bird is a bird even though it cannot sing.” And a Christian is a Christian still even though his soul is dark within him, and he goes on in fear, never daring to look up and hope at all.

===Chinese===
此外还有一样，是人有时不得不忍受其缺乏的。有些福分属于每一个基督徒男女，在某种意义上是他们与生俱来的权利——对上帝的意识、确信、心里的安宁、盼望。毫无疑问，每一个真实的基督徒都当行在上帝同在的光与喜乐里，并在其中做工。

但同样清楚的是，并非人人都如此蒙福。这也许是他们自己的过错；无疑，在许多情形中确是如此。也可能是气质或外在环境使然。无论如何，许多人不得不行路，不在光里，倒在不定、困惑与疑惧之中，有时甚至在黑暗里。

然而「鸟总归是鸟，纵然它不会歌唱」。基督徒也总归是基督徒，纵然他里面灵魂幽暗，纵然他满心惧怕地前行，从不敢抬头仰望，也全然不敢盼望。

===Original===
That is spiritual abasement. It ought not to be. It is never to be lightly acquiesced in. But it happens sometimes to earnest men and women, and it seems to be the settled condition of a few. Is it possible to do without these things? Can a man manage to exist and even move forward who has for a while lost his hold on his faith and on God? There are good and godly men who have done it. Brother Lawrence did it. Robertson of Brighton did it. Horace Bushnell did it. And many, many more. When all that they held most precious in faith had been eclipsed for the time, they steered still by the little light they knew. Though there should be no heaven, they resolved that they were called to be pure, truthful, patient, kind, since these things could never be wrong. Though there were no Christ, they would still follow where He had once seemed to invite them. And so doing and so following they came again to know. The darkness passed, and faith and gladness returned. They had lost hold of God for a little, but He had never lost hold of them. And, brethren, whatever the doubt or darkness be, that’s always true. That is what makes it possible at all. That is what may make it even blessed. For

===Chinese===
这便是属灵的卑贱。这本不该有，也断不可轻率地默认顺受。但它有时确实临到恳切的男男女女，而且在少数人身上，似乎竟成了固定不变的光景。缺了这些东西，还能过得下去吗？一个一时失掉了对信心与上帝之把握的人，还能设法存活，甚至继续前行吗？有良善而敬虔的人做到过。劳伦斯弟兄（Brother Lawrence）做到了。布莱顿的罗伯逊（Robertson of Brighton）做到了。霍勒斯·布什内尔（Horace Bushnell）做到了。还有许许多多别的人。当他们信仰中最珍视的一切一时被遮蔽，他们仍凭着所认识的那一点微光掌舵前行。纵使没有天堂，他们也认定：自己蒙召是要纯洁、真实、忍耐、仁爱，因为这些事断不会错。纵使没有基督，他们仍要循着祂从前似乎邀请他们去的那条路走下去。就这样行着，这样跟着，他们重新得以认识。黑暗过去了，信心与喜乐回来了。他们有一时抓不住上帝，祂却从未失去对他们的把握。弟兄们，无论那疑惑或黑暗是什么，这一点永远真实。正是这一点，使这事根本成为可能；也正是这一点，能使它甚至成为蒙福的。因为——

===Original===
> “It’s better to walk in the dark with God
> Than to walk alone in the light;
> Better to walk with God by faith
> Than to walk alone by sight.”

===Chinese===
> 「同上帝在黑暗中行走，
> 胜过独自在光明中行走；
> 凭信心与上帝同行，
> 胜过凭眼见独自奔走。」

===Original===
Prayer
Our Gracious God and Father in Heaven, whether Thou dost appoint for us poverty or riches, save us from thinking that a man’s life consisteth in the abundance of the things which he possesseth. Beyond all our friendships, be Thou our Friend and Helper, and grant us to seek first the blessing of our God. Make us very sure, for their comforting and our own, that when men in their darkness sorely seek Thy face, the very ache of their quest is token that Thou hast already found them. For Jesus’ sake. Amen.

===Chinese===
祈祷
我们恩慈的上帝、我们在天上的父啊，无论祢为我们派定的是贫穷还是富足，求祢救我们脱离这想法：以为人的生命是在乎家道的丰富。超乎我们一切的友谊，求祢亲自作我们的朋友与帮助者，并恩准我们先求我们上帝的赐福。求祢使我们深深确信——这是为着安慰他们，也是为着安慰我们自己——当人在黑暗中苦苦寻求祢的面时，那寻求的切切疼痛，便是祢已经寻见了他们的凭据。为着耶稣的缘故。阿们。

## XXVII Wonder / XXVII 惊奇

===Original===
> "And Moses said, I will now turn aside and see this great sight."
> — Exodus 3:3

===Chinese===
> 「摩西说：我要过去看这大异象。」
> — Exodus 3:3

===Original===
Moses, adds one commentator significantly, was then eighty years of age. By the ordinary standards, he was an old man, yet he had not lost his youthful sense of wonder. It is a good sign, the best of signs, when a man has lived so long and yet finds wonder in his heart. It is a bad sign when a man at any age, or when a generation of men, find nothing in all God's world to wonder at.

Yet in many quarters it is regarded as the correct attitude to refrain from expressing surprise at anything, no matter how striking. The utmost concession to be made to what is really wonderful is a languid and patronising "Really?" That is always a pitiful thing. For where there is no wonder there can be no religion worthy of the name.

The instinct of worship and the instinct of wonder are very intimately related. And where the one has died, the other cannot be in a very healthy state. "I had rather," said Ruskin once, "live in a cottage and wonder at everything, than live in Warwick Castle and wonder at nothing." And his preference is to be commended. For he who has never wondered has never thought about God in any way to be called thinking.

It was our Lord Himself who said that the ideal of religion was the childlike heart. Everyone knows that these little people are always being brought to a halt to wonder at something. And Heaven is in very truth nearer to them then, and they are more truly filled with its spirit, than either you or I are when the glory and bloom of this world unfold before our eyes, or the thought of the Infinite and Eternal God comes to us and we have not felt impelled to bow our heads in silence and worship, spellbound, and in a godly fear.

===Chinese===
一位解经家意味深长地补上一句：摩西那时正八十岁。照寻常的标准，他已是个老人，却还没有失去他那青年人般的惊奇之感。一个人活到这样长的年岁，心里仍能寻见惊奇，这是一个好兆头，也是最好的兆头。而无论什么年纪的人，或整整一代人，若在上帝的整个世界里找不出一件值得惊奇的事，那就是一个坏的兆头。

然而在许多地方，人们却把这样的态度奉为正确的姿态：对任何事物都不表惊讶，无论它如何惊人。对于真正奇妙的事，肯作的最大让步，不过是一句懒洋洋、带着俯就口吻的「真的吗？」这总是一件可悲的事。因为哪里没有惊奇，哪里就不会有配得上这名称的宗教。

敬拜的本能与惊奇的本能，关系极为密切。这一个死了的地方，那一个也决不会处于多么健康的状态。罗斯金（Ruskin）有一次说：「我宁愿住在茅屋里对凡事感到惊奇，也不愿住在沃里克城堡里对任何事物毫无惊奇。」他的取舍值得称许。因为从未惊奇过的人，从未以任何配称为『思想』的方式思想过上帝。

说宗教的理想乃是一颗孩童般的心肠，正是我主自己。谁都知道，这些小家伙总是动不动就被什么事物拦停下来，惊奇一番。而在那时，天堂实实在在离他们更近，他们也更真切地被天堂的气息充满；比起你我来——当这世界的荣耀与芳华在我们眼前舒展，或那无限而永恒之上帝的意念临到我们时，我们却并未觉得非得默默低下头来敬拜不可，不曾如被摄住心魂，怀着敬虔的畏惧——他们实在更甚。

===Original===
It is not hard to lay one's finger on some of the causes that have brought about this state of things. A silly fashion, for one cause, has decreed that wonder is vulgar. Why that should be so, no one can tell. But if there be higher intelligences than ours in God's Universe, and they see the sons of men, as they have plenty of chances to do, casting an indifferent glance at the full pomp and majesty of the setting sun, or reading such a Psalm as the 103rd with an untouched heart, how they must marvel indeed!

And then, of course, familiarity tends to blunt the sense of wonder in a certain and common type of mind. The best men have always resisted that tendency and recognised that it works harm to life and character. They have remembered to look for God in the common and familiar, and that is a search that goes far to make a man a saint, just because it is a continual prayer, a continual holding open of the heart to God. His answer is to fill the wondering heart, bit by bit, with Himself.

Ignorance, too, is often a cause, the kind of ignorance that calls itself knowledge. It is an innocent delusion on the part of the youthful tyro in science that after he has made a little experiment with a prism and a beam of sunlight, there is nothing wonderful in the rainbow. Pure, profound science on the other hand, speaks very humbly—and wonders all the while.

Nature is dumb and silent concerning the Infinite behind it to him who goes but to catalogue and dissect. Take a heart that can wonder with you on your country-walk, open your eyes and look, open your heart like a child and listen, and you will find, as Moses found, that even in a bush there may be the Voice of God. Hold the door of your heart ajar in simple wonder, and some thing of God will enter to cleanse and freshen it, as the hot and dusty street is washed by the rain from Heaven.

===Chinese===
要指出造成这种光景的若干缘由，并不困难。缘由之一，是一种愚蠢的时尚，它一口断定惊奇是庸俗的。何以如此，没人说得上来。但倘若在上帝的宇宙中真有比我们更高的智慧生灵，而他们——他们有的是机会——看见世人面对落日那全部的壮丽与威严，只是投去漠然的一瞥，或以一颗毫无触动的心诵读诗篇第一百零三篇这样的诗篇，他们又该何等诧异呢！

再者，习熟自然会使某一类常见心智的惊奇之感趋于迟钝。最好的人向来抵抗这种倾向，并承认它有害于生命与品格。他们记得在平凡而熟悉的事物中寻找上帝；这样一种追寻极能使人成为圣徒，正因为它是一种不住的祈祷，是一种向上帝不断敞开心门的姿态。祂的回应，便是以祂自己一点一点地充满那惊奇的心。

无知也常是一个缘由——那种自称知识的无知。科学这一行年青的初学者怀有一种天真的幻觉：他用棱镜和一束阳光做了点小实验之后，便觉得彩虹里再没有什么奇妙可言了。而纯粹深奥的科学却恰恰相反，它说话极为谦卑——并且始终在惊奇。

对那只为编目和解剖而去的人，大自然在它背后的无限者面前是喑哑无声的。带上一颗能在你乡间漫步时与你一同惊奇的心吧，睁开眼睛看，像孩子一样敞开心去听，你就会发现，正如摩西所发现的：纵然在一丛荆棘里，也可能有上帝的声音。以单纯的惊奇将你心的门半开着，上帝的一点什么就要进来，洁净它、使它清新，正如天降的雨水洗净那炎热积尘的街道。

===Original===
Just as he who goes to Nature with a heart that cannot wonder, will find no message there for him, so he who looks out upon the sanctities of home, of human life and love, in that dull mood of mere acceptance, must often find himself hard pressed for material when he makes his thanksgiving to God. George Eliot has spoken somewhere of the agony of the thought that we can never atone to the dead for the stinted affection we gave them, for the "little reverence we showed to that sacred human soul that lived so close to us, and was the divinest thing God has given us to know." The divinest thing God has given us to know!

Have we realised that that gift of God to us lives now in the same home with us? Do you know what it is? It is a wife's devotion, a mother's care, a brother's comradeship, a sister's love. It is the trust and affection of little children, and the patience of those who love us. And yet there have been men—judge ye if this be not true—who have lived close to gifts of God like these, and taken them all unquestioned and never wondered at the undeserved bounty of them or their continuance from day to day.

How easy it is to discover the gifts and charm of a stranger, how easy to wonder at that! But to wonder at the sacrifice and the patience of the love that dwells under the same roof with us, and stoops, in Mrs. Browning's happy phrase, "to the level of each day's most quiet need," how few of us do that! And yet, without daily wonder, how can we be sure that we do not slight it, or requite it ill, how can we truly give our thanks to God whose gift it is?

Most important of all, he who brings no wonder in his heart can never be touched with the sense of God. The lack of the great deep and awful wonder of our fathers in all their thought and speech about God, has brought it about that our religious speech today is too often either superficial, flippant and easy, or syllogistic, mechanical, and hard. It is the absence of wonder that tempts men to imagine that God can be enclosed in any formula whatever, or brought to the hearts of men in so many rigid propositions. If men would but give their wonder expression when they frame their creeds, there would be less chafing where the edges are too sharp.

===Chinese===
正如那带着一颗不会惊奇的心到自然中去的人，在那里找不着给他的信息；同样，那以一味领受的迟钝心境去看待家庭的神圣、人生与爱的神圣的人，当他向上帝献上感恩时，也必常常发现自己苦于凑不出材料。乔治·艾略特（George Eliot）曾在某处说起过这一思想的痛楚：我们给予死者的情爱那样悭吝，我们向「那位与我们亲密同住的属人的神圣灵魂——上帝所赐给我们认识之物中最神圣者」所表示的敬意那样微少，这一切我们永远无法向死者补偿。上帝所赐给我们认识之物中最神圣者！

我们可曾意识到，上帝给我们的这份礼物，如今就住在同一个家里、与我们同在一处？你可知道它是什么？它是妻子的忠诚，母亲的操劳，兄弟的情谊，姊妹的疼爱。它是小孩子们的信靠与依恋，是那些爱我们之人的忍耐。然而曾经有过这样的人——请你们自己评断这话真不真——他们与这样的上帝的礼物比邻而居，把它们一律视作理所当然，从不为它们那非分所配的丰厚惊奇，也不为其日复一日的持续惊奇。

发现一个陌生人的天赋与魅力是何等容易，对之惊奇又是何等容易！但对于那与我们同住一个屋檐下的爱——它甘愿俯就，用勃朗宁夫人（Mrs. Browning）恰切的话说，俯到「每日最安静之需要的高度」——去惊奇它的牺牲与忍耐，我们中间又有几人这样做呢！然而，若没有日日的惊奇，我们怎能确定自己没有轻慢它、没有以恶相报？又怎能真实地向上帝献上感谢——这礼物原是祂所赐的呢？

最要紧的是，心里不带着惊奇的人，永远不会为上帝之感所触动。正因缺少了我们先人在一切关于上帝的思想与言语中所怀的那伟大、深邃而可畏的惊奇，我们今日谈论宗教的言语，才往往不是肤浅、轻佻而随便，就是三段论式的、机械而冷酷。正是惊奇的缺席，诱使人去想象：上帝可以被封进随便什么公式里，可以被拆成一条条僵硬的命题带到人心里。人若在制定信条时，肯让他们的惊奇发为言语，那么在棱角过于锐利的地方，摩擦就会少一些。

===Original===
I am bound to confess that my sympathies are altogether with a working man who once listened to a fervid evangelist at a street corner unfolding a scheme of salvation as clean-cut and mechanical as a problem of Euclid, and buttonholed him afterwards to inquire if he had ever read any astronomy. No, he said, he had not. "That's a pity," said the artisan, "for, eh, man, but ye have an awfu' wee God." In all reverence, my brothers, that is what the absence of wonder brings us to, a small God, a small salvation, and a merely mechanical Christ.

Men have sometimes asked what that childhood of the Kingdom is on which Jesus laid so much stress, and some have taken it to mean renunciation of intellect and reason in favour of a Church's dogma. But it means, says John Kelman, something far more human and more beautiful—"it means wonder and humility and responsiveness, the straight gaze of childhood past conventionalities, the simplicity of a mind open to any truth, and a heart with love alive in it." That is surely right. That is what becoming a little child in Christ's sense does mean. First of all, wonder.

===Chinese===
我必须承认，我的同情完全在一名工人一边：他曾听一位热切的布道员在街角展开一套救恩方案，那方案像欧几里得的一道几何题一样轮廓分明、机械刻板；事后他拉住那布道员，问他可曾读过一点天文学。没有，那人说，他没读过。「可惜了，」这位工匠说，「因为，唉，老兄，你那位上帝未免太小啦。」弟兄们，请容我怀着十分的敬意说：这正是惊奇的缺席把我们带到的地方——一位小小的上帝，一桩小小的救恩，和一个纯然机械的基督。

人们有时问，耶稣如此着重强调的那「国度的孩童样式」究竟是什么，有人把它理解成舍弃智力与理性、去皈依某教会的教条。但它意味着的，照约翰·凯尔曼（John Kelman）的说法，是远为合乎人性、也远为美丽的东西——「它意味着惊奇、谦卑与感应，意味着孩童那越过一切俗套的率直凝视，意味着一种向任何真理敞开的心思所具有的单纯，以及一颗有爱活在其里的心。」这说得确实不错。在基督的意义上变成小孩子，真正的含义正是如此。首先，是惊奇。

===Original===
Prayer
Almighty and eternal God, Creator and Ruler of the Universe, dwelling in light that is inaccessible and full of glory, whom no man hath seen or can see, what is man that Thou art mindful of him, and the son of man that Thou visitest him? Behold what manner of love the Father hath bestowed upon us that we should be called the sons of God! Such knowledge is too wonderful for us; it is high, we cannot attain unto it. O come let us worship and bow down, let us kneel before the Lord our Maker. Amen.

===Chinese===
祈祷
全能永在的上帝啊，宇宙的创造者与主宰啊，祢住在人不能靠近的光里，满有荣耀，人未曾看见祢，也是不能看见的；人算什么，祢竟顾念他？世人算什么，祢竟眷顾他？你看，父赐给我们是何等的慈爱，使我们得称为神的儿女！这样的知识奇妙，是我们不能测的；至高，我们不能及。来啊，我们要屈身敬拜，在造我们的主面前跪下。阿们。

## XXVIII The Fatherhood of God / XXVIII 上帝的父性

===Original===
> “If ye then, being evil, know … how much more … your heavenly Father.”
> — Luke 11:13

===Chinese===
> 「你们虽然不好，尚且知道……何况……你们的天父。」
> — Luke 11:13

===Original===
If it were a conceivable thing that we had to part with all the words of Scripture save one, and if we were allowed to choose that one, there are some of us who would elect to retain that great declaration of Jesus—"If ye being evil know … how much more … your heavenly Father." For, having that, we should still be rich in knowledge of the Love and Fatherhood of God. We should still know Christ's dominating conception of God, and have His last and highest word regarding Him. We should still be able to rise, as Jesus not only warrants but invites us to do, from the little broken arc of true fatherhood on earth to the perfect round in Heaven.

At the warm reassuring touch of that "How much more your heavenly Father" whole systems of brainy divinity vanish away! The truth of the Fatherhood of God, vouched for and lived on by Jesus, kills men's hard and unworthy and hurtful thoughts about God as sunshine kills the creatures that breed and prevail in darkness and ignorance. They can no more live alongside of a realisation that Christ's name for God is His true name, and really describes His attitude to all the sons of men, than the dark, creepy things that live under the stone can remain there when you turn it over and let in the air and the light.

===Chinese===
倘若可以设想：我们不得不舍去圣经的全部话语，只留一句，而又蒙允许自己挑选那一句，那么我们当中有些人就会选留耶稣那句伟大的宣告——「你们虽然不好，尚且知道……何况……你们的天父。」因为，有了这一句，我们对于上帝的爱与父性，仍然富足有余。我们仍然知道基督对上帝那统摄一切的观念，仍然持有祂论到上帝的末了、也是最高的言语。我们仍然能够升上去——像耶稣不仅许可、更是邀请我们做的那样——从地上真实父性那道小小的断弧，升到天上那完全的圆环。

只要那「何况你们的天父」温暖而叫人安心的触碰一临到，整套套自作聪明的神学体系便烟消云散！上帝的父性这条真理，由耶稣亲自印证，也由耶稣终生倚靠，它必要杀灭人心中那些关于上帝的冷酷、卑下、有害的念头，正如阳光杀灭在黑暗与蒙昧中滋生繁衍的造物。人一旦认识到，基督对上帝的称呼就是祂真实的名字，实在描述了祂对世人众子的态度，那些念头就再也无法存留，正如石头底下那些阴暗爬行的东西，你把石头翻过来、放进空气与光，它们就无法仍留在原处一样。

===Original===
But, say some, you must not carry the truth of God's Fatherhood too far. What is too far? I ask. I want to carry it, and I believe Christ means us to carry it, as far as ever it will stretch, and that is "as far as the East is from the West." Think of a father's *goodwill*. It is conceivable that other men may do you a deliberate wrong. But you are entitled to believe that your father won't. You may not understand what he proposes, but you can be quite sure that he means only your good. Henry Drummond tells how his early days were made miserable by the conception he had of God as of some great staring eye in the heavens watching all he did. But that is a policeman's eye, not a father's.

There are many tokens that, even yet, we have not realised what these blessed words of Jesus mean and imply. A mother vainly trying to answer the old, old question why her little one was taken from her, will say, "Perhaps I was too fond of him." Or, should sudden sorrow come, the explanation suggested by the troubled one himself is, "I was too happy." There are plenty of people who are afraid to declare that they feel very well or are very happy, in case the upper powers should hear and send trouble, apparently out of sheer malice! "Bethankit, what a bonny creed!" Oh! what a dreadful caricature of God! How it must pain the Father to hear His children talking so!

===Chinese===
但有人说，上帝父性这条真理不可推得太远。什么叫太远？我要问。我愿意把它推到它所能伸展的尽头，我相信基督的意思也是要我们这样推，那尽头就是「东离西有多远」。想一想一位父亲的*善意*。别人蓄意亏负你，是可以想见的事；但你尽可相信，你的父亲决不会。你也许不明白他所安排的是什么，但你满有把握：他的意思单单是要你得好。亨利·德拉蒙德（Henry Drummond）诉说过，他早年如何愁苦，因为他把上帝想成天上某只瞪视的大眼睛，盯着他所行的一切。但那是警察的眼睛，不是父亲的眼睛。

有许多迹象表明，直到今天，我们仍未领会耶稣这些蒙福的话语是什么意思、含着什么意味。一位母亲徒劳地想解答那个古老又古老的问题——她的小宝贝为什么被夺去——她会说：「也许是我太疼他了。」或者，当突然的忧患临到，那心里烦乱的人自己给出的解释是：「我太快活了。」有许多人不敢承认自己身子康健、心里快乐，惟恐上头的权势听见，就降下祸患来——那看来简直是出于纯粹的恶毒！「谢恩祷（Bethankit），好一副漂亮的信条！」啊！这是把上帝歪曲成何等可怕的一幅漫画！听见自己的儿女这样说话，天父心里该何等疼痛！

===Original===
There is another mark of fatherhood, as we know it on earth—*compassion*, pity, the willingness to forgive. There is no forgiveness on earth like a father's or a mother's, none so willing, none that will wait so long and yet give itself without stint at last. Pity, as the world of business and of ordinary relationship knows it, is at best a transient emotion. It murmurs a few easy words and then forgets. But parent love suffereth long and is kind, hopes against hope, and waits and is still hopeful when everyone else has written the offender down irreclaimable. It is such compassion and pity for us sinners, how great soever our sins be, that Jesus would have us come for to God in Heaven.

But will not men abuse such patience and long-suffering? it is asked. Is it not a risky thing to tell them that God is our Father? It is. But it is the risk that Love takes cheerfully, and that only Love can take. And when men talk lightly and complacently about the great mercy of God, there is something, I think, which they have forgotten, namely, that at the heart of the divine Fatherly forgiveness there lies the shadow of the Cross. I do not say that in any conventional sense. I say it because I have seen for myself that at the heart of all true earthly forgiveness of a fatherly sort there lies this same mysterious shadow. Shall not the father forgive his returning prodigal? Yea, verily, and with all his heart. But, ah, before that, think how the father has suffered with his son, and for his son. The prodigal's shame is the father's shame too, and lies heavy on his heart. And it is out of a chamber where he and that pain have long been companions that the earthly father issues to welcome and receive at last the lad who has sought his face penitent and in his right mind. The welcome is real. The forgiveness is full and free. And yet behind it there is sacrifice. The price of it is suffering. Aback of it lies—the Cross! That is what silences cheap thinking and glib speech about the forgiveness of God. If God's long-suffering be like a father's here, it is, first, long suffering.

===Chinese===
父性还有一个标记，就是我们在地上认识它的那个样子——*怜恤*，体恤，乐意饶恕。世上的饶恕，没有一样比得上父亲或母亲的饶恕：没有那样甘心，没有一样肯等候那么久，而末了又毫不吝惜地倾注出来。怜悯——在生意场与寻常人情往来里人所知道的那种——至多是一阵转瞬即逝的情绪；它咕哝几句省事的客套话，随后就忘了。惟独父母的爱恒久忍耐，又有恩慈；在无可指望的时候仍怀着指望；一直等候，等到人人都已把那犯错的人判定为无可挽回，它还存着盼望。耶稣正是要我们到天上的上帝面前来，领受这样的怜恤与体恤——无论我们这些罪人的罪何等深重。

但有人要问：人岂不会滥用这样的忍耐与恒久容忍吗？告诉他们上帝是我们的父，岂不是一件冒险的事吗？是冒险。但这是爱所甘心乐意去冒的险，也是惟有爱才能冒的险。而当人轻慢自得地谈论上帝的大怜悯时，我想，有一件事是他们忘了的，那就是：在神圣的父式饶恕的中心，横着十字架的暗影。我这样说，并不是套用一句陈言；我这样说，是因为我亲眼见过：在世上一切真实的、带着父的心肠的饶恕之中心，都横着这同一道神秘的暗影。父亲岂不饶恕那回头的浪子吗？是的，实实在在，并且全心全意。可是，啊，在那之先，请想一想：这位父亲曾怎样与他的儿子一同受苦，又怎样为他的儿子受苦。浪子的羞耻也是父亲的羞耻，重重压在他的心头。正是从一间他与那痛苦长久作伴的内室里，这位地上的父亲走出来，迎接并收纳那终于痛悔回转、醒悟过来寻求他的面的孩子。那迎接是真实的，那饶恕是完全的、白白的。然而在它背后有牺牲；它的代价是受苦；在它后面横着的——是十字架！正是这个，叫那些关于上帝饶恕的廉价思想与油滑言语闭口无声。上帝的恒久忍耐若像世上父亲的忍耐，那么，它首先就是长久的受苦。

===Original===
The danger, however, is not that we abuse God's grace knowingly and in callous complacency. Far more is it, I think, that we never actually accept and realise and build our lives upon the gracious compassion of the Heavenly Father and His willingness to forgive.

Every parent ought to know Coventry Patmore's beautiful lyric, "The Toys." In it a father tells how, when his little son had been disobedient again and again, he struck him, and sent him with hard words and unkissed to bed—"his mother, who was patient, being dead." And when, later, he went upstairs to see him, he found him asleep, his lashes still wet with tears, and—what touched him most—on a table beside his bed all his little treasures heaped together to comfort his sad heart—a box of counters, and a red-veined stone, a piece of glass abraded by the beach, and six or seven shells, a bottle with blue bells, and two French copper coins—all his little store of precious things.

===Chinese===
然而，危险并不在于我们明知故犯、麻木自得地滥用上帝的恩典。我以为，更大的危险倒在于：我们从来不曾真正接受、真正体会到那位属天之父恩慈的怜恤和祂的乐意饶恕，并把我们的生命建造在其上。

每位作父母的都应当知道考文垂·帕特莫尔（Coventry Patmore）那首优美的抒情诗《玩具》（"The Toys"）。诗中一位父亲讲述：他的小儿子再三不听话，他打了他，用严厉的话语、不曾亲吻，就把他打发上床——「他那素来忍耐的母亲，已经去世了。」后来他上楼去看他，见他已经睡着，睫毛上还挂着泪珠；而最叫他心头受感的是：床边的小桌上，堆着他所有的小珍宝，好安慰他那颗忧愁的小心——一盒筹码，一块带红纹的石子，一粒被沙滩磨蚀的玻璃，六七枚贝壳，一瓶蓝铃花，还有两枚法国铜币——全是他那小小的一库珍藏。

===Original===
> So when that night I prayed
> To God, I wept and said—
> “Ah, when at last we lie with tranced breath,
> Not vexing Thee in death,
> And Thou rememberest of what toys
> We made our joys,
> How weakly understood
> Thy great commanded good,
> Then, fatherly not less
> Than I, whom Thou hast moulded from the clay,
> Thou'lt leave Thy wrath and say:
> ‘I will be sorry for their childishness.’”

===Chinese===
> 于是那晚我祷告，
> 向上帝，我哭着说：
> 「啊，到末了，我们屏着恍惚的呼吸躺卧，
> 在死中不再搅扰祢，
> 那时祢若记起我们曾拿怎样的玩具
> 造成我们的欢乐，
> 曾何等软弱地领会
> 祢那浩大的诫命美意，
> 那时，祢身为父亲，必不减于
> 我这祢用泥土抟造的人，
> 祢必收起祢的忿怒，说：
> 『我必为他们的孩童无知而忧伤。』」

===Original===
One word more about our Father's *silence*. Our fathers here on earth had their silences when we were children. We asked him for something that we wanted very much. And he gave no reply. We went on asking. We expected to get what we had set our hearts on. He heard us hoping and believing that this good thing would come to us, and he held his peace. But we knew that silence, and we trusted it. We were quite sure that he would have told us if we were deceiving ourselves, that his gift, when it came, would, at least, not be a mere mockery of our hopes.

And I often think of these words of Christ's, "If a son shall ask bread of any of you that is a father, will he give him a stone?" when I stand by a graveside, and speak the words of radiant hope with which we lay our beloved to rest. Our Father hears us speak that hope. He has heard hearts in an agony through all the generations wish that it might be true—that this bleak fact of Death is not the end, but only the beginning of a better thing. But He keeps silence. We have no sure proof, only the blessed hope of the Christian evangel.

He keeps silence. But, my brethren, can we not trust that silence since it is our Father's? We have asked this bread in our pain and through our tears. We have asked it because it seems to us we need it so. And whatever gift His silence hides, this at least is certain, it is not, it cannot be, only a stone.

===Chinese===
关于我们天父的*静默*，还要再说一句话。我们在地上的父亲，在我们作孩子的时候，也有他们的静默。我们向他求一样我们极想要的东西，他不回答。我们继续求，满心期待能得着我们所切慕的。他听着我们盼望、相信这件美事必会临到，却默然不语。但我们认得那静默，并且信得过它。我们深信，倘若我们是在自欺，他必会告诉我们；他的礼物来的时候，至少也决不会是对我们盼望的一场嘲笑。

当我站在坟墓旁边，说着那些光辉夺目的盼望之言——我们正是用这些话，安葬我们所亲爱的人——我常常想起基督的话：「你们中间作父亲的，谁有儿子求饼，反给他石头呢？」我们的天父听见我们说这盼望。历世历代，祂听见过多少在剧痛中的心灵切愿这盼望是真的——愿死亡这萧索的事实并非终点，而只是一个更美之事的开端。但祂保持静默。我们没有确凿的凭据，只有基督徒福音那蒙福的盼望。

祂保持静默。但是，弟兄们，那静默既然是我们天父的，我们岂能不信赖它？我们曾在痛苦中、在眼泪里求这块饼。我们求它，是因为在我们看来，我们何等需要它。祂的静默里藏着什么礼物，我们不知道；但至少这一点是确定的：它不是，它决不可能，只是一块石头。

===Original===
Prayer
Almighty God, who through Jesus Christ has taught us to call Thee our Father, we thank Thee that Thou hast chosen a name so dear to us to reveal Thy care and Love. When our way is dark and our burden is heavy and our hearts are perplexed, grant us the grace to know that Thou who art directing every step of our journey art a God of Love, and Thy true and perfect Name is Our Father in Heaven. Through Jesus Christ our Lord. Amen.

===Chinese===
祈祷
全能的上帝，祢曾藉着耶稣基督教导我们称呼祢为我们的父，我们感谢祢：祢竟拣选了一个于我们如此亲爱的名字，来启示祢的眷护与爱。当我们的路幽暗、担子沉重、心中困惑的时候，求祢赐恩，使我们知道：那位引导我们旅程每一步的，乃是爱的上帝；而祢那真实而完全的名字，就是我们在天上的父。奉耶稣基督我们的主求。阿们。

## XXIX The Unreturning Brave / XXIX 不归的勇士

===Original===
> “Whosoever will lose his life for my sake shall find it.”
> — Matthew 16:25

===Chinese===
> 「凡为我丧掉生命的，必得着生命。」
> — Matthew 16:25

===Original===
Christmas in war time was like an evil dream. Easter is like a breath from Heaven itself, a wind from the pure and blessed heights of God blowing the clouds of battle-smoke apart for a brief space so that we all may see again that beyond the smoke and beyond grim death itself there is the Life Enduring, a Divine Love compared to which ours at the best is untender and hard, a Fatherly welcome beside which welcomes here are faint and cold. This is the strangest Easter Day the world has ever known, yet never have the thousands and thousands of stricken homes and sore hearts needed more the living hope that is begotten anew in the Christian Church this day by our Lord’s rising again from the dead. It is assuredly of God’s mercy that Easter should fall in these days, when so many fathers and mothers, wives and sisters and lovers need its hope and comfort so.

===Chinese===
战时的圣诞节像一场噩梦；复活节却像出自上天本身的一口气息，像从上帝纯洁而有福的高处吹来的一阵风，把战烟的密云吹开片刻，使我们众人得以重新看见：在烟云之外、在阴森的死亡本身之外，有那长存的生命，有一种神圣的爱——与之相比，我们至好的爱也失了温柔、生硬无情；有一种父亲般的迎接——与之相比，人间的迎接都黯淡而冰冷。这是世人所曾知道的最奇异的一个复活节；然而千千万万遭难之家、伤痛之心，却从未比今天更迫切地需要那活泼的盼望——因我们的主从死里复活，这盼望今天正在基督教会里重新生出来。复活节恰落在这些日子里，确乎是出于上帝的怜悯，因为如今有许许多多做父亲的和做母亲的、做妻子的和做姊妹的、做恋人的，这样迫切地需要它的盼望与安慰。

===Original===
We cannot but think today of the many, many homes in our own and other lands from which strong and brave men marched away weeks or months ago, because they had heard the call, and were willing to make the supreme sacrifice for righteousness’ sake, who will never come back again, who have died a soldier’s death and sleep in a soldier’s grave﻿—fathers, husbands, sons, lovers, gallant men, dear lads, cheerful, willing, dauntless. You find their names by the hundred and the thousand in the casualty lists, but the loss you cannot measure unless you could see all the shadowed homes. How many such homes there are in our own land alone, How many such in our own little circle!

Try to realise that, and then ask if a more gracious message could fall upon all these hearts today than the Easter message of the Christian Church﻿—that there is no death and that its seeming victory is not a victory. The old, old question, If a man die shall he live again? is answered today by the triumphant Yes! of Christendom. Yes, he never ceases to live. From the inferno of the battlefield the mortally stricken do but pass across the bridge and stream of death to God’s Other Side. When they fall in battle, they fall into His everlasting Arms. They do not die. They are not dead. It is only their poor mortal bodies that the shrieking shells can maim or destroy. They themselves, the real self and spirit of them, no material force can hurt, for that belongs to a higher kingdom than the visible, and its true goal and home are not here at all.

===Chinese===
今天我们不能不想到，在我们自己和其他各国的国土上，有许许多多的家庭：几周或几月之前，强壮勇敢的男子从那里出征而去，因为他们听见了号召，甘愿为公义的缘故献上至高的牺牲；他们再也不会回来了——他们死了一场军人的死，睡在军人的坟墓里——父亲、丈夫、儿子、恋人，英勇的汉子，亲爱的少年郎，欢欢喜喜、甘心乐意、无所畏惧。你在伤亡名单上成百上千地见到他们的名字，但那损失你无从估量，除非你能看见所有那些蒙上阴影的家庭。单单在我们本国，这样的家庭有多少！在我们自己的小圈子里，这样的又有多少！

试把这一层思量一番，然后问一问：今天还能有什么信息，比基督教会那复活节的信息更施恩地落在这一切心上么——那信息说：没有死亡，死亡那看似的得胜并不是得胜。「人若死了岂能再活呢？」这个古老古老的问题，今天正由基督教世界一声得胜的「必能再活！」作了回答。是的，他永不停止存活。从战场那地狱般的火海里，受了致命伤的人不过是越过死亡的桥、渡过死亡的河，往上帝的彼岸去。他们在战场上倒下，乃是倒进祂永久的膀臂里。他们没有死。他们不是死了。尖啸的炮弹所能伤残毁坏的，不过是他们那可怜的、必死的身体。至于他们自己，他们真正的自我与灵魂，没有任何物质的力量能加害，因为那属于一个比眼所能见更高的国度，而它真正的目标与家乡根本不在这里。

===Original===
To all who are sitting in darkness and in the shadow of death in these days, to all who have watched their beloved go out where every true man would wish to go, and know only too surely that they shall never return﻿—to these today Jesus Christ has His Word to speak﻿—and would that all might hear it and give it room in their hearts to do its blessed work! It is to Him we owe it, and He is our authority for believing that beyond the darkness and separation of death there is the morning of a new and fairer day. The valley of the Shadow, yea, the valley of battle itself opens out again at its far end to the sun’s rising and the untrammelled life in the light and liberty of God. The happy warrior is borne by gentle hands to God’s own land of peace, where the fret and fury of battle slip from him like a discarded garment, and beside the still waters of that better country he finds healing for his hurt. It is that quiet and blessed hope that is being reborn in our hearts this day as the Church keeps her festival of a Risen and a Living Christ. It is that lively hope the Church offers for comfort to all stricken homes and to every sorrowing heart.

They offered themselves, these gallant lads, not for anything they hoped to gain, but for the sake of honour and liberty, of justice and righteousness. And when a man casts himself on God in that fashion, offering not the words of his lips, nor the homage of his worship, but himself, all that he has, his life and all that life holds for him, think you that upon that poor soul, with his priceless offering borne humbly in his hands, the God and Father of us all is going to turn His back? “He that loseth his life,” said Jesus, “for my sake shall find it.”

===Chinese===
在这些日子里，凡坐在黑暗中、死荫之下的人，凡眼看着自己所爱的人出发，往每个真汉子都愿意去的地方去，并且十分确凿地知道他们永不回来的人——今天，耶稣基督有祂的话要对他们说——但愿人人都听见这话，并在心里给它留出地步，让它作成它那有福的工！这盼望是我们从祂受的；我们之所以相信，在死亡的黑暗与离别之外，有一个崭新而更美之日的清晨，所根据的也正是祂。死荫的幽谷——是的，连争战之谷本身——也在它的远尽处重新向着日出敞开，向着上帝的光与自由中那无拘无束的生命敞开。那幸福的勇士被温柔的手抬往上帝自己平安的境地；在那里，争战的烦扰与狂怒像一件脱下的衣裳从他身上滑落；在那更美家乡可安歇的水边，他的创伤得着医治。今天，正当教会守她那复活了、活着之基督的节期，在我们心里重新生出来的，正是这安静而有福的盼望。教会把这活泼的盼望献上，作一切遭难之家、每一个忧伤之心的安慰。

这些英勇的少年献上的是他们自己，不是为了指望得着什么，而是为了荣誉与自由，为了公正与公义。当一个人以这样的方式把自己投靠在上帝身上，所献上的不是他嘴唇的话语，也不是他敬拜的礼，而是他自己，是他的一切，是他的生命以及生命为他所存的一切——你想，当那可怜的灵魂双手谦卑地捧着他那无价的祭物时，我们众人的上帝与父会向他转背么？耶稣说过：「凡为我丧掉生命的，必得着生命。」

===Original===
There are times when the most gracious doctrine is not gracious enough to represent and embody the Spirit of Christ to us. We want something more, and we often seek it and sometimes find it in poetry, in art, or, best of all, in the silence of our own hearts when God-given instinct whispers what no words or doctrine can ever express. Such a time is now. Such a need is ours today.

I make no defence of it theologically, and I ask no man to accept it who does not feel it clamouring at his heart for entrance, but I confess that for me a couple of lines of John Hay’s in his *Pike County Ballads* strike a note which all that I know in my heart of the Spirit of Christ leaps up to welcome and approve. It is when he has told the story of Jim Bludso’s sacrifice. Jim was engineer on the *Prairie Belle*, a river-steamboat, and he was rather a rough, careless man. But when the steamer took fire, it was Jim who held her against the bank till everybody got safely off except himself. With eyes wide open to what he did, he sacrificed his life to save the other souls on board. Hay sums up in these lines:—﻿

===Chinese===
有时候，最有恩慈的道理也还不够恩慈，不足以向我们表明并体现基督的灵。我们还想要更多；我们常去寻求，有时也能寻见——在诗歌里，在艺术里，而最好的，是在自己内心的静默里：那时，上帝所赐的直觉低声说出言语或道理永远无法表达的事。此刻正是这样的时节。今天我们正有这样的需要。

我不在神学上为它辩护，也并不请那感觉不到它正在心头嚷着要求进来的人接受它；但我承认，在我而言，约翰·海（John Hay）《派克县歌谣》（*Pike County Ballads*）里的两行诗，敲出了一个音符，叫我心中所认识的一切基督的灵，都欢跃起来欢迎它、赞许它。那是他讲完吉姆·布拉德索（Jim Bludso）舍己的故事之后。吉姆是内河轮船「草原美人号」（*Prairie Belle*）上的轮机长，为人相当粗豪、马虎。可是轮船着火的时候，正是吉姆把船死死顶在岸边，直到人人都安全下了船，只剩下他自己。他睁大眼睛，清楚知道自己做的是什么，却舍了自己的性命，去救船上其余的性命。海用这几行诗作结：——

===Original===
> “And Christ ain’t going to be too hard
> On a man that died for men.”

===Chinese===
> 「基督断不至于太苛刻
> 对一个为众人舍命的人。」

===Original===
I leave it there. I trust I am a loyal son of the Church, but I must have a place in my creed somewhere for the hope which these lines express that Christ ain’t going to be too hard on a man that died for men.

But there is something more to be said. Every chaplain at the front tells us that the most careless and irreligious youths and men take up a wonderfully different attitude out there. Men pray in the trenches who have never prayed before. I heard some stories recently that brought tears to my eyes, of brave and simple confessions made at little gatherings for prayer in strange places, by some of those very lads whom we reckoned indifferent and heedless before they left home. And some of them, turning their faces simply and earnestly, and by an old, old instinct of the heart, towards God and His Christ before the battle broke upon them, some of them have fallen on the field!

===Chinese===
我就说到这里为止。我自信托我是教会忠实的儿子，但在我信经的某处，总得为这两行诗所表达的那盼望留一个位置——基督断不至于对一个为众人舍命的人太苛刻。

但还有更多的话要说。前线每一位随军牧师都告诉我们：最漫不经心、最不敬虔的少年和成人，在那边却表现出一种奇妙不同的态度。有人在战壕里祷告，那些人从前从未祷告过。近来我听见一些事，叫我眼中落泪：在一些奇异的地方举行的小小祷告聚会中，有人作了勇敢而质朴的告白——而那些小伙子，正是我们在他们离家之前还认为冷漠大意的人。他们中间有人，在战火临到之前，凭着心中一种古老又古老的直觉，质朴而恳切地把自己的脸转向上帝和祂的基督——这些人中间，已有一些倒在了战场上！

===Original===
Many, many more there must be who turned them Godwards even at the eleventh hour in one brief upward glance to ask forgiveness and strength to play the man, about whom no chaplain can report, for no one knows or saw or heard save Christ Himself. But there’s a glorious page in the Gospel to assure us beyond all doubt or question that no one who makes that appeal, though it be the dying thief himself, ever makes it in vain.

And there we leave the issue﻿—with God, who is kinder than our kindest, and whose mercy is from everlasting. It is He who has brought us this blessed hope, through His Son, this Easter Day, and we honour His gift best by taking it in all its breadth and comfort to our hearts. To the brokenhearted wife or mother, to whom the bald War Office report has come, let us take this comfort﻿—“Your beloved is not dead. God has him in His gracious care and keeping till the day break and the shadows flee away.” For that is the Easter message, God be thanked. And this is Easter Day.

===Chinese===
必定还有许许多多的人，甚至在最后关头，只以短短一瞥仰望上天，便转脸归向上帝，求赦免、求作大丈夫的力量——关于这些人，没有随军牧师能报告什么，因为除了基督自己，无人知道，无人看见，无人听见。但福音里有一页荣耀的书页，足以使我们毫无疑虑、毫无疑问地确信：凡发出这样呼求的人，纵然是那个临死的强盗，也决不至于徒然。

我们就这样把这结局交托在上帝那里——祂比我们中间最慈爱的更慈爱，祂的怜悯从亘古就有。藉着祂的儿子，在这个复活节，把这一有福的盼望带给我们的，正是祂；我们尊崇祂这恩赐的最好方式，就是以它全部的广博与安慰，把它接到我们心里。对那接到一纸干巴巴的陆军部通告、心碎的妻子或母亲，让我们把这份安慰带给她——「你所爱的人没有死。上帝正以祂恩慈的眷顾与保守怀抱他，直到天起凉风、日影飞去的时候。」因为这就是复活节的信息，感谢上帝。而今天正是复活节。

===Original===
Prayer
To Thy merciful care and keeping we commend all the sons and daughters of affliction, and especially those who in this great contest have lost some loved one. Grant that even through their tears they may discern the glory that belongs to those who have given their lives a ransom for many. Be Thou their help and their strength, and may the sympathy of all who know them be for them an earnest and token of Thy great Love and Compassion. Through Jesus Christ our Lord. Amen.

===Chinese===
祈祷
我们把一切在患难中的儿女都交托于祢恩慈的眷顾与保守，尤其是那些在这场大冲突中失去了所爱之人的人。求祢施恩，使他们甚至透过眼泪，也能看见那归与舍命作多人赎价之人的荣耀。求祢作他们的帮助、他们的力量；愿一切认识他们之人的同情，对他们成为祢大爱与怜悯的凭据和表记。奉耶稣基督我们的主求。阿们。

## XXX The Sacrament of Sunset / XXX 日落圣礼

===Original===
> “The heavens declare the glory of God.”
> — Psalm 19:1

===Chinese===
> 「诸天述说神的荣耀。」
> — Psalm 19:1

===Original===
“The sky,” says Ruskin, “is the part of Nature in which God has done more for the sake of pleasing man, more from the sole and evident purpose of touching him, than in any other of His works.” It looks like the truth. For there is no scene of earth so fair or majestic that man cannot spoil it. Where the “cataract exults among the hills, and wears its crown of rainbows all alone,” he will build him a powerhouse to supply current to some distant town. But he cannot touch the heavens. In the heart of some fairy glen he will placard the virtues of somebody’s pills, and plaster the gateposts in a sweet country lane with the specious claims of some quack doctor, but above it all, it is God, and God alone, who spreadeth out the heavens like a curtain and in them has set a tabernacle for the sun. Even in places where the face of earth wears no suggestion of natural beauty the face of the sky redeems it from evil. For, above the squalor of the city’s meanest slum, burn the great fires of the setting sun, and overhead the fleecy white clouds sail silently all night long.

===Chinese===
「天空，」罗斯金（John Ruskin）说，「是自然界的一部分；在其中，上帝为着取悦人所做的，出于那唯一而显明的、为要触动人的目的所做的，比在祂其余一切作品中更多。」这话说来像真理。因为地上没有任何景致，美丽或雄伟到人不能加以糟蹋的地步。哪里有「瀑布在群山间欢跃，独自戴上彩虹的冠冕」，人便在哪里为自己盖起一座发电厂，好把电流送到某个远方的城镇。但他动不了诸天。在某处仙境般的幽谷中心，他会张贴某某药丸的奇效招贴，把某位江湖郎中似是而非的许诺刷满可爱乡间小径的门柱；然而在这一切之上，是上帝，唯有上帝，铺张穹苍如铺张幔子，并在其间为太阳安设帐幕。就是在地的面貌不见半点天然美踪迹的地方，天的面貌也能把它从丑恶中救赎出来。因为，在城市最卑陋的贫民窟的污秽之上，燃烧着落日的大火；头顶之上，絮白的云朵整夜无声地航行。

===Original===
But, of it all, the glory of the sunset is chief. The dawn has its cold splendours too, but not many of us are there to see it when it is at its best. It is at eventide, when the work of the day is done, and the spell of its restfulness lays the senses open, it is then chiefly that God unfolds these splendid harmonies of colour in the western heavens. And, by consent, on this Ayrshire coast, on which I look out as I write, these glories can be seen to great advantage. It is into no flat expanse of water that the dying sun sinks here. The peaks and crags of Arran invest its passage with an indescribable pomp and majesty, standing out against it like the massive pillars of some giant gateway of the West. It is never twice the same. Sometimes lurid and blazing, with masses of thundercloud piled high, all their outer edges rimmed with fire; and, next night, peaceful and level, a study in straight lines, as if the great Artist, with even brush, had washed the sky with bands of grey and blue and gold. Each evening God has His own picture for us, His own handiwork, unspoiled by man. How many of us ever pause to recognise its beauty? What does it mean that such a prodigality of harmonious colours should be the most ordinary feature of our evening hour? Is it that God Himself takes delight in the beauty of it all, for its own sake, rejoicing, like all good workmen, in the work of His hands? Or has He some purpose with regard to His children of mankind? Is it, as Ruskin says, for the sake of pleasing man? How unthankful and unmindful we are, if that be so!

===Chinese===
而在这一切之中，日落的荣耀为首。破晓也有它清冷的光华，但它最壮丽的时刻，我们中间并没有多少人在那里观看。而正是在傍晚——白日的工作既毕，那份安息的魅力使感官舒张开放——上帝主要在那时，才在西方的天际铺展开这些壮丽的色彩和声。而且大家都公认，在我写作时向外眺望的这片艾尔郡（Ayrshire）海岸，这些荣耀可以看得格外壮美。在这里，垂死的太阳并不是沉入一片平坦的水面；阿伦岛（Arran）的峰峦与峭壁，为它这一段行程披上难以言喻的堂皇与威严，衬映着落日矗立，宛如西方某座巨人门户的粗大柱石。这景象从无两次相同。有时阴沉而炽烈，大团的雷云高高堆起，其外缘尽镶着火边；隔夜却又宁静平展，成了直线的研究，仿佛那位大艺术家以匀整的笔，把天空洗染上一道道灰、蓝与金。每天傍晚，上帝都为我们备下祂自己的图画，祂自己的手笔，未经人手玷污。我们中间有几人曾驻足认出它的美？如此一份挥霍无度的和谐色彩，竟成了我们黄昏时分最寻常的景象，这意味着什么？是不是上帝自己就以这一切的美为乐，为着美本身的缘故，像一切好工匠那样，以自己手中的工作为喜？还是祂对祂的儿女、对人类，另有一个目的？是不是如罗斯金所说，为的是取悦人？若真是这样，我们是何等不知感恩、何等漫不经心！

===Original===
The sunset teaches us to put together these two ideas—beauty, beyond the wit of man to portray, and God. There is plenty of ugliness and sin in the world, and the life of men. Man himself recognises how much of the beauty that might have been has been marred and disfigured by him. Yet in his heart he worships it, and feels after it afar off. And in the evening sky it is written that Beauty belongeth supremely unto God.

Whatever that far-off divine event be, to which the whole creation moves, one of its features shall be, must be, a beauty which shall fully satisfy. For beauty and God cannot be divorced. And when, of an evening, God for His own good pleasure, working with those material elements which have no power to disobey His behests, unfolds His will in such dazzling visions of splendour, is He not declaring that the end and goal of life itself, when His purpose therewith is completed, and Man, too, has fallen into harmony with His will, shall be fair, and satisfying, and beautiful?

===Chinese===
日落教导我们把这两个观念放在一起——美，远非人的才智所能描摹的美，以及上帝。这世界和人的生命里，丑陋与罪都够多了。人自己也承认，那本可以有的美，有多少已被他毁损、糟蹋了。然而他在心里敬拜美，在远处揣摩追寻它。而傍晚的天空写下这样的宣告：美至高无上地属于上帝。

无论那全部受造之物所趋往的遥远神圣之事究竟是什么，它的特征之一必是、也必须是：一种全然令人满足的美。因为美与上帝不能分离。而当上帝在某个傍晚，出于祂自己的美意，运用那些无力违逆祂吩咐的物质元素，在如此令人目眩的壮丽异象中铺展祂的旨意时，祂岂不是在宣告：生命本身的终点与目标——当祂关乎此的旨意既已成全、人也在祂的旨意中归于和谐之时——必是秀美的、满足人的、美的？

===Original===
Let us not be afraid to say and believe that God speaks to us in the sunset. If I pick up the receiver of a telephone and hear my friend announce some good news that fills my heart with gladness, it does not disturb me to remember that the wire itself has no power to speak. For I feel that somewhere at the end of the wire is a mind and a heart like my own who is using the dead, soulless wire as a medium of speech with me. When the glories of the sun’s setting fall upon your heart like a benediction, stirring you to devout and grateful thought, breathing peace upon you, cleansing your desires of all that is mean and sordid, do not be afraid to believe that, behind and beyond all that is material and visible, there is the Mind and Heart in whose image yours was made, whose gift peace is, whose whisper, though it come along dead ether-waves to reach you, is His whisper nevertheless.

It is perhaps natural that the prevailing quality of the thoughts that arise within us when we watch the setting sun should be pensive, tender, and, not seldom, a little sad. For it speaks of the end of the day and the coming night. Its charm and spell are like that of autumn, the remembrance of what has gone, the tender grace of a day that is dead. For all the beauty and wonder of this world, there is a tear at the heart of things. Beneath all our laughter and happiness there lies that deeper note. The night cometh. There is an end to it all—friendship, love, happiness, work, life itself.

===Chinese===
让我们不要害怕说出并相信：上帝在日落里向我们说话。若我拿起电话的听筒，听见我的朋友传来一个使我满心欢喜的好消息，那么，想起那电线本身并没有说话的能力，并不会叫我心里不安。因为我觉得，在线的那一端某处，有一个和我一样的心智与心肠，正用这条死的、没有灵魂的电线，作为向我说话的媒介。当落日的荣耀如祝祷般临到你的心，感动你生发虔敬而感恩的思念，向你吹送平安，洁净你的愿望，除去其中一切卑贱与污浊，你不要害怕去相信：在一切物质与可见之物的背后与之外，有那一位心智与心肠——你的心智与心肠正是照着祂的形象造的；平安乃是祂的恩赐；祂的低语，纵然沿着死寂的以太之波传到你这里，却仍然是祂的低语。

我们观看落日时心中所起的念头，其主导的性质大约自然是沉思的、温柔的，而且往往带一点忧伤。因为它诉说着白日的终结与黑夜的将临。它的魅力与魔力像秋天——像对逝去之物的追念，像那已死之日温柔的风致。纵然这世界有这一切的美与奇，在万物的心底却含着一滴泪。在我们一切的欢笑与幸福之下，潜藏着那更深沉的音调。黑夜将到。这一切都有终结——友谊、爱、幸福、工作、生命本身。

===Original===
> “For be the long day never so long,
> At last it ringeth to evensong.”

===Chinese===
> 「长日纵然再长，
> 终究要鸣响晚祷的钟声。」

===Original===
And yet, and yet, my brothers, the end is beautiful, more beautiful even than the beginning. God has made the day’s death to be exceeding fair. The sun passes gloriously to its rest. Hopefully too, for, passing thus, it promises a new and fairer morning. So do God’s children die.

===Chinese===
然而，然而，弟兄们，那终结是美的，甚至比开端更美。上帝已使白日之死极其秀美。太阳满有荣耀地归入它的安息。它归去也是满有盼望的，因为这样经过，它便应许了一个更新、更美的早晨。上帝的儿女也是这样死去。

===Original===
Prayer
O Lord our God, who hast written Thy Word of hope and promise in the evening sky, be near us when our day is done, and the wind has fallen silent, and the night is waiting. Put us to sleep in a chamber of peace whose windows open toward the sun rising, and, when we awake, may we be still with Thee. For Jesus’ sake. Amen.

===Chinese===
祈祷
主我们的上帝啊，祢在傍晚的天空写下了祢那盼望与应许的话语；求祢在我们白日已毕、风已归寂、黑夜等候之时，临近我们。求祢使我们睡在一间平安的内室里，其窗朝向日出之地敞开；当我们醒来，愿我们仍然与祢同在。为着耶稣的缘故。阿们。

## Endnotes / 尾注

===Original===
1. Written in February. ↩︎

===Chinese===
1. 写于二月。↩︎

===Original===
2. I wish to acknowledge my indebtedness to Sir Wm. Robertson Nicoll’s “When the Wounded Go Home,” a tender and courageous message. ↩︎

===Chinese===
2. 笔者愿在此致谢：我深受威廉·罗伯逊·尼科尔爵士（Sir Wm. Robertson Nicoll）《当伤员回家时》（“When the Wounded Go Home”）一书之惠，那是一则温柔而勇敢的信息。↩︎
